塔罗冥想 · 中英对照加注 Meditations on the Tarot
第十三封信

死神

LETTER XIII · DEATH · LA MORT
Tarcher/Penguin 2002 · pp. 338–370 ／ 中译本 页 493–536
全信 153 个对照单元 · 50 条注释
点橙色虚线处可展开原译 · 顶栏可一次展开全部
题　辞　一
那女人回答：「园子里任何树的果子我们都可以吃；只有园子中间那棵树的果子不可吃。上帝禁止我们吃那棵树的果子，甚至禁止我们摸它；如果不听从，我们一定死亡。」蛇回答：「不见得吧！你们不会死。上帝这样说，是因为他知道你们一吃了那果子，眼就开了，你们会像上帝一样能够辨别善恶。」——《创世纪》第3章，第2-5节
中 494 · 英 340
And the woman said to the serpent,
“We may eat of the fruit of the trees of the garden;
but God said, ‘You shall not eat of the fruit of the
tree which is in the midst of the garden,
neither shall you touch it, lest you die.’”
But the serpent said to the woman,
“You will not die.
For God knows that when you eat of it
your eyes will be opened,
and you will be as gods,
knowing good and evil.”
(Genesis iii, 2-5)
题　辞　二
告诉枯骨要听上主的话。—《以西结书》第37章，第4节
中 494 · 英 340
O dry bones, hear the word of the LORD.
(Ezekiel xxvii, 4)
1亲爱的不知名朋友：
中 495 · 英 340
Dear Unknown Friend,
2你现在是否因为上帝和蛇对于「堕落造成死亡」这件事的看法相左，感到有些困惑不解？上帝的说法是:「只有那棵能使人辨别善恶的树所结出的果子你们绝对不可吃；你们吃了，当天一定死亡。」但蛇却说：「你们不会死。」在这件事情上面，神和蛇的态度似乎都很绝对。
中 495–496 · 英 340
Have you ever been struck by the contrary statements concerning death made by God and the serpent in the narrative in Genesis on the Fall? Because God says there, “You shall not eat of the tree of knowledge of good and evil, for on the day when you eat from it you will die”, and the serpent says, “You will not die”. Here God is categorical; the serpent is just as much so.
3难道蛇是在说谎？或者蛇的理念根本是错的？亦或是蛇所认知的真理只适用于它自己的世界，在上帝的国度里就变成是不正确的？换言之，是否存在著两种相左的死亡与不朽种是从上帝的视野、一种是从蛇的角度而论？难道蛇所理解的「死」便是神所理解的「生」，蛇所理解的「生」则是神所理解的「死」？
中 496 · 英 340
Did the serpent quite simply lie? Or is it a matter of a fundamental error on the part of the serpent? Or again, did he state a truth from the range of truths proper to the domain of the serpent which are untruths in the domain of truths for God? In other words, are there two immortalities and two different deaths—one from the point of view of God, the other from the point of view of the serpent? Thus, is it simply that the serpent understands by “death” what God understands by “life” and that he understands by “life” what God understands by “death”?
4亲爱的朋友，我请你开始著手探寻这个问题的解答，同时留意一下我的思考方向。眼前这张「死神」卡就有上述问题的答案。卡上呈现的是一个骷髅人，正在收割从黑色土壤里冒出来的一些东西—人头和手等等。
中 496 · 英 342
Now, I invite you dear Unknown Friend to set to work with a view to finding an answer to this question, whilst bringing to your attention fruits of the work that I have made towards the same end. For the answer to this question is the Arcanum of the thirteenth Card of the Tarot, “Death”, which represents a skeleton who reaps only what pushes up from the black soil and rises above it—hands, heads, etc.
5经验性的死亡通常指的是存在于物质世界的生命消失不见了。这是我们透过感官所体认到的外在事实。但这种「消失」不仅发生于五官感知到的外境，同时也发生于内在意识里；个中的意像和心象会随著生物的死亡一起消失不见。这便是我们所谓的「遗忘」。这种状况每个夜晚都会出现在我们的记忆、意志和认知领域，直到我们完全忘却自己为止；此即一般所谓的「睡眠」。
中 496 · 英 342
Our empirical experience of death is the disappearance from the physical plane of living beings. Such is the fact of our experience from without, that we have by means of our five senses. But the disappearance as such is not confined to the domain of outward experience of the senses. It is experienced also in the domain of inner experience, in that of consciousness. There the images and representations disappear just as living beings do so for the experience of the senses. This is what we call “forgetting”. And this forgetting extends each night to the totality of our memory, will and understanding—of a kind such that we forget ourselves entirely. This is what we call “sleep”.
6就我们整体的内在与外在经验来说，遗忘、沉睡和死亡其实是同一件事的三种示现方式—是那件「事」导致了一切的「消失」。有人说沉睡是死亡的弟兄。
中 496 · 英 342
For our whole experience (outer and inner) forgetting, sleep and death are three manifestations of the same thing—namely the “thing” which effects disappearance. It is said that sleep is the younger brother of death. It is necessary to add: forgetting is the brother of sleep.
7我必须再加上一句：遗忘也是沉睡的弟兄。因此，遗忘、沉睡和死亡乃同一本质或能量的三种示现方式，只是程度上有所不同，就是它导致了身、心、灵种种现象的消失。事实上，遗忘即是沉睡，沉睡即是死亡。或者可以说：遗忘和记忆的关系等于沉睡和觉知的关系，沉睡和觉知的关系则等于死亡和生命的关系。
中 496–497 · 英 342
Forgetting, sleep and death are three manifestations—differing in degree—of a sole principle or force which effects the disappearance of intellectual, psychic and physical phenomena. Forgetting is to sleep as sleep is to death. Or again: forgetting is to memory as sleep is to consciousness, and sleep is to consciousness as death is to life.
8人会遗忘、沉睡、死亡，也会忆起、苏醒、诞生。忆起和遗忘对应的是苏醒和沉睡，苏醒和沉睡对应的则是诞生和死亡。人沉睡时会忘记自己，醒来时会记起自己。死亡启动的是遗忘，诞生启动的是记忆。当自然界把我们完全遗忘时，我们便进入了死亡；当我们忘却自己时，则进入了沉睡；当我们对某件事丧失积极的兴趣时，等同于逐渐忘却了它。
中 497 · 英 342
One forgets, one goes to sleep, and one dies. One remembers, one awakes, and one is born. Remembering is to forgetting as awakening is to falling asleep, and awakening is to sleeping as birth is to death. One forgets oneself when one goes to sleep, and one remembers oneself when one awakes. It is also the mechanism of forgetting which is at work when one dies, and it is the mechanism of remembering which works at birth. When Nature forgets us, then we die; when we forget ourselves, then we fall asleep; and when we lose active interest in something, then we forget it.
9但是我们必须记住，遗忘、沉睡和死亡涉及的范畴，比心智的遗忘、生理的睡眠和临床的死亡要深广得多。除了心智的遗忘之外，灵魂和意志也会遗忘，如同它们都有记忆一样，这是一种超越头脑的记忆。譬如，我们可能在脑子里对昔日的朋友还保有清晰的记忆，但心理上却完全忘掉了这个人。虽然我们仍旧记得有这号人物，却不记得过去的情谊了。同样地，人有可能在头脑和心理层面都记得某个人，甚至还保有著鲜活的感受，但意志层面却不记得此人了。或许你对他还存有亲切的回忆，却不想再为他做任何事。
中 497 · 英 342
Nevertheless, it must not be forgotten that the respective domains of forgetting, sleep and death are more vast and more profound than intellectual forgetting, organic sleep, and clinical death. Apart from intellectual forgetting there is also forgetting in the domain of the soul (psychic forgetting) and a forgetting in the domain of the will, just as there is memory in the domain of the soul and memory in the domain of the will—beyond intellectual memory. Thus, for example, one can retain a clear and precise intellectual memory of a friend from the past but at the same time have completely forgotten him psychically. One recalls, but without the living friendship of former times. Similarly, one can remember a person intellectually and psychically, i.e. with vivid feeling, but at the same time have forgotten him in the domain of the will. One remembers him with tenderness perhaps, but one does nothing for him.
10除了生理的睡眠（躺在床上忘却自己的一切）之外，还有灵魂和意志的睡眠。我们醒著的十六至十八个小时当中，灵魂的某些层面其实是睡著的。人清醒时的意识，对许多事情是「不知不觉的」……
中 497 · 英 343
Beyond organic sleep, i.e when one is in bed and oblivious of everything including oneself, there is psychic sleep and a sleep of the will. During the sixteen or eighteen hours that we are in the waking state there are layers of our psychic being which are asleep. During the waking state one is “asleep” to many things—facts, people, ideas, God…
11佛陀之所以被视为被尊崇为对生、老、病、死彻底觉知的人，是因为未成道者多少都能意识到自己对这些事是缺乏觉知的不是头脑缺乏认识，而是在心灵和意志上觉知不到自己正经历著生、老、病、死。人的头脑虽然知道有这些事情在发生，但实际上是缺乏体受的。人只有真正明白自己所知道的，而且深切地体受到自己所明白的，同时又能采取正确的行动，才算是真知或确知。
中 497–498 · 英 343
And if the Buddha is considered—and venerated—as “fully awake” to the facts of human life such as sickness, old age and death, it is because those who are not Buddhas know that they are asleep with regard to these facts—not intellectually, but psychically and in their will. They “know” it and they do not know it at the same time. For one knows truly when one understands what one knows, when one feels what one has understood, and when one put into practice what one has understood and felt.
12除了临床的死亡之外，还有心灵和意志的死亡。我们在七十或八十几年的人生当中，内心有好几层东西已经逐渐死去。我们在灵性和道德上已经丧失了一些东西。失去信、望、爱，是任何辩解、训诫甚至鲜活的典范都无法弥补的。只有神圣魔法—恩宠—能够为死去的东西注入活力。耶稣基督之所以被尊崇为复活之主，是因为受死亡驱迫的人都知道，只有神圣魔法具有起死回生的功能，而复活的耶稣就是它的保证
中 498 · 英 343
Similarly, beyond clinical death there is a psychic death and a moral death. During our seventy or eighty years of life we bear within us layers of death in our psychic being. There are things which are missing from our psychic and moral being. The absence of faith, hope and love cannot be remedied either by arguments or by exhortations or even by a living example. An act of divine magic—or grace—is necessary to accomplish the infusion of life into that which is dead. And if Christ is worshipped as the Risen One, it is because those who bear death within them know that it is only divine magic which can raise what is dead within them and that the risen Christ is the guarantor of this.
13人。遗忘、沉睡和死亡，就如同忆起、苏醒和诞生一样，都有其专属的象征图解。黑色象征著遗忘，草丛象征著沉睡，拿著镰刀的骸骨则是死亡的象征…黑色既代表非自愿和自然的遗忘，也代表圣十字若望所说的自愿和超自然的遗忘—灵魂与上主在感官、认知和意志的三重黑夜里合为一体。草丛之所以象征沉睡，是因为我们熟睡时的状态就像植物一样。熟睡时，人体的有机活动，诸如呼吸、循环、消化及生长，会在「动物性」和「人性」缺席的情况下持续发挥作用。我们熟睡时就像「植物」人一样。而骸骨之所以成为死亡的象征，是因为一个活生生、有意识的人分解成了一堆矿物，也就是骸骨。
中 498 · 英 343
Forgetting, sleep and death—like remembering, waking and birth—have imaginary and symbolic expressions proper to them. Thus black is the image of forgetting, tufts of grass are the image of sleep, and a skeleton with a scythe is the image of death…Black is the symbol both of involuntary and natural forgetting and of that voluntary and supernatural forgetting of which St. John of the Cross speaks—this threefold night of the senses, the understanding and the will, in which the union of the soul with God is accomplished. Tufts of grass or leaves are the symbol of sleep, because deep sleep is the state where we live a vegetative life. Organic life—breathing, circulation, digestion and growth—continue during sleep without “animality” and “humanity” being present. We are “plants” when we are deep in sleep. And the skeleton is the symbol of death because it reduces the phenomenon of the conscious, mobile, living and material man to that which is mineral in him—the skeleton.
14自然的遗忘使人退化成动物；自然的沉睡使人退化成植物；自然的死亡则使人退化成矿物。就是因为「死」可以分成遗忘、沉睡和死亡，所以画面上才会出现黑色的场域，上面有一撮撮的草丛和一个骷髅人。
中 499 · 英 343
Natural forgetting reduces man to animality; natural sleep reduces him to vegetality; and natural death reduces him to minerality. The whole problem of death, comprising three degrees—forgetting, sleep and death proper, or the Arcanum of death—must therefore be presented to us as the image of a black sphere, beneath which there are tufts of grass and above which there is a skeleton.
15第十三张大阿卡纳所攸关的，其实是「减法」的三种示现方式遗忘、沉睡和死亡。除了上述的内容之外，画面上还出现了由人头、手、脚代表的第四种元素，稍后我们再深入地加以讨论。因此这张卡的主题就是「减法」或死亡，它和「加法」或生命恰好相反。
中 499 · 英 343
And it is precisely the thirteenth Card of the Tarot which presents us this image. The context of the Card is that of the threefold manifestation of the principle of subtraction by way of forgetting, sleep and death. We have here the black soil, the blue and yellow tufts of grass, and also the skeleton mowing. The Card contains still a fourth element, represented on the Card by the human heads and hands, and one foot, to which we shall return later.
16如果要理解遗忘的机制作用，就必须从星光体、乙太体和肉体中「减除」自性的作用；如果要进入沉睡状态，就必须从乙太体和肉体中「减除」自性和星光体的作用；如果要变成一具尸体或进入死亡，就必须从肉体中「减除」自性、星光体和乙太体的作用。这三重减法形成了「脱离肉身」的过程，对应的三重加法则形成了「化为肉身」的过程，因为化身就是自性先形成星光体，再形成乙太体和肉体。
中 499 · 英 344
The thirteenth Arcanum of the Tarot is therefore that of the principle of subtraction or death, and is the opposite of the principle of addition or life. It is necessary to subtract the Self from the astral body, the etheric body and the physical body in order to understand the mechanism of forgetting; it is necessary to subtract the Self and the astral body, from the etheric body and the physical body in order to obtain the state of sleep; and it is necessary to subtract the Self, the astral body and the etheric body from the physical body in order to obtain the corpse, i.e. the fact of death. These three degrees of subtraction in their totality constitute the process of excarnation, just as the corresponding three degrees of addition constitute the totality of the process of incarnation. For incarnation is the addition of an astral body to the Self, the addition of an etheric body to the astral body and the Self, and lastly the addition of a physical body to the etheric body, the astral body and the Self.
17卡上的骸骨握著的镰刀，代表的就是减法或脱离肉身的作用力，亦即割断自性和星光体（遗忘）、星光体和乙太体（睡眠）、乙太体和肉体（死亡）之间的连结。
中 499 · 英 344
Now, the scythe which is held by the skeleton of the Card represents the work of subtraction. It is this which symbolises the force of excarnation, i.e. that which severs the ties between the Self and the astral body (forgetting), the ties between the astral body and the etheric body (sleep), and the ties between the etheric body and the physical body (death).
18灵魂和肉体—应该说灵魂和精微体之间到底有著什么样的连结，却被这把具有三重减法功能的镰刀割断了？究竟是什么连结著自性和星光体、星光体和乙太体、乙太体和肉体？换言之，我们为何以及如何记得往世的历史？我们为何以及如何到了早上就会醒来？我们为何以及如何能存活数十年？
中 499 · 英 344
What are the ties between the soul and the body—rather, the soul and the bodies—that the scythe of the threefold principle of subtraction severs? What is it that unites the Self to the astral body, the astral body to the vital or etheric body, and the vital body to to the physical body? In other words, how and why do we remember the past, how and why do we wake in the morning, and how and why do we live several decades?
19让我们先撇开与这些问题相关的庞大文献，试著直接去冥想它，亦即不借助外来资讯而是根据亲身体验获得确知。冥想是一种为了内在的确知而进行的深思，因此必须放下假装知道的一般知识，例如科学常识等等。我们在冥想时，必须本著良知问自己：「我确实知道了什么？」而不是：「大家的答案是什么？」
中 500 · 英 344
In the first place, let us disregard the enormous literature where these questions are dealt with and endeavour to undertake a meditative work, i.e. to think directly about the subject which occupies us, without the intermediary of what may be borrowed from sources other than our immediate experience and understanding. To meditate is to think with a view to attaining certainty in the inner forum of consciousness, renouncing all pretension of arriving at things of general validity (i.e. things which may be a contribution to science). In meditation—and these Letters are only meditations—it is a matter above all of the question, posed in all honesty to our own conscience and answered in all honesty by our own conscience: “What do I myself know?”, and not the question: “What is generally known?”
20亲爱的朋友，我们在探讨灵魂和身体之间的连结时，应该暂时放下一般人对这个议题的认知和说法，试著去思考自己有能力得到的答案。
中 500 · 英 344
Let us disregard for the moment, dear Unknown Friend, what is generally known and said on the subject of the ties between the soul and the body, and let us endeavour to take account—just for ourselves—of what we know and are able to know about it.
21首先让我们思索一下「遗忘和忆起」这件事。从主观的角度来看，忆起算是一种唤回过去事件的魔法。它能够让已经过去的事情出现在当下。如同巫师或招魂预知术士可以唤回死者的灵魂使其显相，记忆同样也可以唤回过去的往事，令其出现在我们脑海里。当下生起的记忆就是主观魔法带来的结果，它帮助人从被遗忘的黑洞里唤出鲜活的过往意像。鲜活的过往意像……是一种灵魂的印记吗？还是象征符号、拷贝或幻影？答案为以上皆是。记忆可以说是一种印章，能够复制来自过去世的印象，同时也是一种象征符号，因为它会利用自我的想像力示现出意像范围之外的真相；它也是一种拷贝作用，目的在于复制过往的原始事件；甚至可以说它是一种幻影，因为是从深渊里冒出来的被遗忘的幽灵。
中 500 · 英 345
Firstly, let us consider the domain of forgetting and remembering—the memory. Memory is the magic, in the subjective domain, which effects the evocation of things from the past. It renders past things present. Just as a sorcerer or necromancer evokes the spirits of the dead by making them appear, so does memory evoke things of the past and make them appear to our inner mental vision. The present remembrance is the result of a magical operation in the subjective domain, where one has succeeded in evoking from the black void of forgetfulness a living image from the past. A living image from the past…imprint? symbol? copy? phantom? It is all of these at once. It is an imprint in so far as it reproduces an impression received in the past; it is a symbol in so far as it makes use of my imagination to represent a reality which goes beyond its imaginary representation; it is a copy in so far as it only aims at reproducing the original from the past; it is a phantom in so far as it is an apparition from the black abyss of forgetfulness and in so far as it recalls to life the past in making it present to my inner vision.
22但究竟是什么力量促成了主观且神奇的唤回作用？
中 500 · 英 345
What is the force at work in the subjective magical operation of remembering?
23人对记忆的体验可以分成四种：机械式或自动式记忆、逻辑式记忆、道德记忆，以及垂直或启示性记忆。
中 500 · 英 345
There are four types of memory that one experiences: mechanical or automatic memory, logical memory, moral memory and vertical or revelatory memory.
24机械式或自动式记忆几乎无须做出任何唤回的举动；它是自然发生的。它会按照联想法则事物之间的相似性、亲和性及关联性自动地出现，人除了觉察以外无须做任何事。当我接收到一种想法时，上述类型的记忆就会提供我许多过往的意像，以便从中做出选择。因此当我看见一支烟斗时，立即会生起各种意像，譬如：「我在一九XX年的某地见到的一位老船长」、「一本和印第安人的仪式有关的烟斗书」、「我的朋友S先生在上一次大战发生时，拿出自己栽培的烟草并点燃了它，由于当时没有人买得到烟草，因此在场的人都为之兴奋」等等。
中 501 · 英 345
Mechanical or automatic memory hardly makes any demand on the act of recall. The remembrance simply happens. It takes place in an automatic way according to the laws of association—i.e. resemblances, affinities and alliances between things—which effect recall without my taking any other part than that of an observer. This sort of memory supplies me, on the occasion of each impression that I receive, with a host of images of the past from which I can choose. Thus when I see a pipe, I can choose between images of the past which present themselves to my mind, e.g. “an old sea-dog whom I saw at B. in 19…”; “a book on Red Indians where it was a matter of the peace-pipe ritual”, “my friend S. who used to put everyone to flight when he lit his pipe of tobacco cultivated and prepared by himself at the time of the last war when there was no tobacco for sale”, etc.
25与自动式记忆相比，我们必须更主动唤起逻辑式记忆。为了唤回这种记忆，我们必须主动思考。假设我忘了印度教三位一体的其中之一，就必须自问：除了造物主和毁灭主、梵天和湿婆神之外，存在于祂们之间的第三种核心力量「应该」是什么？我必须专注在两者之间的空缺上，试著以推理方式去补足它。「啊，祂应该是守护神毗湿奴！」我会如此告诉自己。逻辑式记忆的自动化作用，通常小于主动运思的功能。
中 501 · 英 345
In so far as logical memory is concerned, I am more active than in the case of automatic memory. Here I have to think in order to remember things. Thus, for example, if I want to remember the Hindu Trinity, amongst whom I have forgotten one of the three terms, I ask myself: If there is a Creator and a Destroyer, Brahma and Shiva, which third principle ought to be found between the Creator and the Destroyer? I concentrate on the empty place between the two and I make an effort to fill it logically. “Ah, it is the Conserver principle—this is Vishnu—of course!” I say to myself. In logical memory there is less automatism and more conscious effort.
26道德记忆则几乎不包含任何自动化作用，它是一种虽主观却真实存在的魔法。启动道德记忆的就是爱，爱唤回了过往的记忆。凡是能令你深深感动、难以忘怀或立即想起的，往往是跟敬仰、尊重、友谊、感恩、亲情及上千种与正向感受有关的回忆。人在一件事上付出的爱越多，越容易产生道德记忆。
中 501 · 英 345
With respect to moral memory, there is hardly any automatism. Here the remembrance is no longer something which happens but rather it is an authentic magical act, although subjective. It is love which is at work in moral memory when it recalls things from the past. Here it is admiration, respect, friendship, gratitude, affection and a thousand other things which have deeply moved you, which render things from the past unforgettable, i.e. evocable at each instant. The more one has loved, the more one remembers through moral memory.
27一般而言，年轻人拥有非常强的自动式记忆，但随著年龄的增长，逐渐需要逻辑式或理性记忆的辅助。届时就必须尽量运用理性思考了。无法发展理性思考能力的人，年老时会有失忆问题：自动式记忆会变得越来越靠不住，原本应该辅助它的逻辑式记忆也减退了。
中 501–502 · 英 346
As a general rule young people possess a very strong mechanical memory. It becomes feebler with age and it is logical or intellectual memory which comes to its assistance. This demands an effort to think, an intellectual effort. People who have failed to develop a taste for thinking and intellectual effort will have difficulties with their memory in mature age. Mechanical memory will fail them more and more, and logical memory, called to supplement it, will also be lacking.
28在年老时，道德记忆会取代自动式和逻辑式记忆的功能。爱可以滋养和维护道德记忆，以弥补逐渐消逝的上述两种记忆。老化之所以使人渐失记性，是因为道德记忆无法适时地取代逻辑式记忆的功能更别提自动式记忆的功能了。懂得如何在事物中找到道德价值、看出背后道德意义的人，是不会忘记任何事情的；他们老来仍拥有正常甚至超凡的记性。
中 502 · 英 346
With respect to moral memory, it is above all in old age that it replaces more and more not only mechanical memory but also logical and intellectual memory. It is the heart then which supplies the energy which nourishes and maintains memory and which supplements the growing lapse of mechanical memory and intellectual memory. Senile lapse of memory is due to the fact that the person who suffers from it failed to replace in time the functions of intellectual memory—without mentioning mechanical memory—by those of moral memory. People who know how to and are able to give everything a moral worth and to see a moral sense in everything will not forget anything; they will have a normal, if not excellent, memory to a very advanced age.
29涵盖一切事物的道德记忆，对越不漠视道德的人来说越能生效，这是毫无例外的。由此可知，漠视或者对道德漠不关心，就是记忆衰退的主因。
中 502 · 英 346
Moral memory—which can comprehend everything without exception—is all the more effective the less one is morally indifferent. Indifference, a lack of moral interest, is the fundamental cause of the lapse of memory which often takes place in old age. The less one is indifferent, the more one remembers of the past and the more one is capable of learning new things.
30你越不漠视它，就越能忆起过往的经验，也越有能力学习新颖的事物。除了自动式、逻辑式及道德记忆之外，还有一种「垂直或启示性」记忆。它不是从过去到现在的横向记忆，而是垂直地忆起在现前、高层或更高层发生的事。它不是在身心层次上去连结当下和过去的「印记」，而是一种链接平常意识和高层意识的忆起作用。这也是一种「低层我」复制「高层我」的方式，亦即「高层我」将体悟烙印在「低层我」上面。它是「高层之眼」与「低层之眼」的炼环，能让人拥有真正的信仰和智慧，免于遭受怀疑论、唯物论及命定论的危害。这不但是对上主和一切属灵存有的坚信基础，也是对永恒与确信复活的根基。「黎明是缪斯的挚友」(Dawn is the friend of themuses）这句谚语和其他类似的座右铭，譬如「晨间人的嘴里有黄金」、「人在早晨比在夜晚更有智慧」，都和人在清晨从垂直记忆获益有关；清晨是人从「自然的喜悦」或睡眠层次醒来恢复意识的时段。
中 502–503 · 英 346
Beyond the three types of memory—mechanical, logical and moral—of which it is a matter here, there is still the kind of memory that we have designated as “vertical or revelatory memory”. It is not a memory of the past in the sense of the horizontal line: today, yesterday, the day before, etc., but rather in the sense of the vertical line: here, higher, still higher, etc. It is a “memory” which does not link the present to the past on the plane of physical, psychic and intellectual life, but which links the plane of ordinary consciousness to planes or states of consciousness higher than that of ordinary consciousness. It is the faculty of the “lower self” to reproduce the experience and knowledge of the “higher Self” or, if you like, the faculty of the “higher Self” to imprint its experience and knowledge upon the consciousness of the “lower self”. It is the link between the “higher eye” and the “lower eye”, which renders us authentically religious and wise, and immune to the assaults of scepticism, materialism and determinism. It is this also which is the source of certainty not only of God and the spiritual world with its hierarchical entities but also of the immortality of our being and reincarnation, wherever it is a matter of reincarnation. “Dawn is the friend of the muses” and similar popular proverbs—such as Die Morgenstunde hat Gold im Munde (“the morning hour has gold in its mouth”), or Utro vechera mudreye (“morning is wiser than the evening”), or even De morgenstond heeft goud in den mond (the Dutch version of Die Morgenstunde hat Gold im Munde)—relate to the benefits of vertical memory from which one benefits in the morning, after the return of consciousness from the plane of “natural ecstasy” or sleep.
31垂直记忆比三圣戒顺服、神贫和守贞—更能直接有效地让下界的人听见、觉知和领收上界的讯息。基本上，垂直记忆就是人发展到更高层次的道德记忆。它和三圣戒的目标一样，都是针对德性的净化而非智力的发展。
中 503 · 英 347
Vertical memory is the more effective to the extent that the three sacred vows—obedience, poverty and chastity—render the lower man capable of listening to, perceiving and receiving things from above without distortion. Vertical memory is fundamentally only moral memory carried in its development to a still higher degree. This is why it is only moral purification, which the practice of the three sacred vows entails, that counts in the case of vertical memory. Intellectual interests, as such, do not count here.
32以上是关于记忆的概略说明。现在我们要回头来探讨：究竟是什么力量促成了主观神奇的唤回作用？
中 503 · 英 347
This is an outlined inventory of the domain of memory. Let us now return to the question: What is the force at work in the subjective magical operation of recall?
33首先我们必须明白，我们前面所讨论的不同层次的记忆：「自动式记忆」、「逻辑式记忆」、「道德记忆」、「垂直记忆」，会带来不同程度的启悟。事实上，越是自动化的事物越背离直接的悟性。说真的，纯自动化的解释根本算不上是一种解释，因为它将思想和感受转入无意识领域，导致人无法深解事物。譬如，有人将微笑解释为「大脑的中枢神经将电流传至嘴巴及脸颊部位，使得嘴巴及脸颊的肌肉收缩」，但即便这是正确地描述了肌肉和神经的机制作用，仍旧没有解释「微笑」的真正起因；
中 503 · 英 347
It is necessary, firstly, to take account of the fact that in the scale that we have established: “mechanical memory”—“intellectual memory”—“moral memory”—“vertical memory” it is a matter of degrees of remoteness and proximity, concerning an immediate and lucid understanding of the evidence as to “how” and “why” memory functions through consciousness. In fact, the more something is mechanical, the more it is removed from immediate understanding through consciousness, and the more it is removed from the latter, the more it is mysterious and incomprehensible. A purely mechanical explanation is, truth to tell, not at all an explanation, because it removes the object to be explained from the domain where understanding takes place—by shifting it from the domain of comprehensibility, i.e. from “thinkability” and “feelability”—into the domain of the unconscious and therefore of incomprehensibility. He who wants to explain the phenomenon of smiling, for example, by the contraction of muscles in the region of the mouth and cheeks, and this latter through electrical impulses transmitted through the nerves from the centre called the “brain”, will in no way give an explanation of the phenomenon of “smiling”, even if he correctly describes the entire mechanical process in the muscles and nerves, for the simple reason that he disregards the joy of which the smiling is a manifestation and which set in motion both the muscles of the mouth and the electrical impulses of the nerves. For it is not nerves and muscles which are manifested in smiling, but rather joy.
34他忽略了「喜悦」才是让肌肉与神经系统产生微笑的主因。任何一种自动化的解释都不会是完整的解说，因为问题会从觉知之光转进无意识的暗处。所谓的「自动化」就是缺乏觉知的意思。
中 503 · 英 347
And just as the description of the mechanical process of the muscles and nerves is in no way a reply to the question: What is smiling?, so is every mechanical explanation of whatever it may be in no way an explanation of the whole, but rather it reduces questions to silence by shifting the subject of the questions from the domain of comprehensibility to that of incomprehensibility—from the light of consciousness to the darkness of the unconscious. For what we call “mechanical” is in reality only the unconscious (or rather “that which is deprived of consciousness”) and is therefore inaccessible to consciousness, and therefore incomprehensible, unthinkable and unfeelable. “Mechanicalism” is therefore not at all the realm of answers, but rather the graveyard for real questions.
35因此，「自动化」的答案就是一切问题的坟墓。因此，当我们探讨不同层次的记忆时，要避免从「自动式记忆」的角度去理解唤回作用。我们必须从「道德记忆」和「垂直记忆」的角度去探索它。唯有进入整合的层次，才能为过往的探索带来最深的启悟。只有透过最大值，我们才能理解最小值。
中 503–504 · 英 348
This is why in the scale of memory under discussion we must not—and in fact cannot—seek to understand the working of recall in the domain where it is unknowable and incomprehensible, i.e. in that of “mechanical memory”. On the contrary, we must seek it at the other end of the scale—where it is least embedded in the darkness of “mechanicalism” and where it most reveals its essence in the light of consciousness, i.e. in the domain of “moral memory” and “vertical memory”. For it is the stage of complete development which illumines and explains the previous stages, and not vice versa. The minimum is only the reduced maximum and it is through the maximum that one understands the minimum, and not vice versa. It is consciousness which renders the mechanical and unconscious comprehensible, the latter being only consciousness reduced to a minimum, and not vice versa. It is man who is the key to the biological evolution of Nature and not the primitive organic cell.
36觉醒使我们认识了自动式或无意识的作用，因为后者就是最小值的意识。但究竟是什么力量促成了主观且神奇的唤回作用？
中 504 · 英 348
We must therefore seek for the key to the operation of recall in memory at the highest degree of memory’s development—“moral memory” and “vertical memory”. Therefore, what is the force at work in the subjective magical operation of remembering, such as it is revealed in “vertical memory” and in “moral memory”?
37以下是最高明的答案——其他的都是次级版本：
中 504 · 英 348
The following reveals it to its highest conceivable degree—other degrees being only its analogous, weakened manifestations:
38耶稣一向爱马大和她的妹妹，也爱拉撒路…耶稣到了伯大尼，知道拉撒路已经在四天前埋葬了……耶稣哭了……心里非常激动。他来到坟墓前；那坟墓是一个洞穴，入口的地方有一块石头堵住。耶稣吩咐：「把石头挪开！」
……于是他们把石头挪开……耶稣大声喊：「拉撒路，出来！」
那死了的人就出来；他的手脚裹著布条，脸上也包著布。（《约翰福音》第11章，第5-44节）
中 504 · 英 348
Now Jesus loved Martha and her sister and Lazarus…when Jesus came, he found that Lazarus had already been in the tomb four days…Jesus wept…Then Jesus, deeply moved again, came to the tomb; it was a cave, and a stone lay upon it. Jesus said: Take away the stone… So they took away the stone…Jesus cried with a loud voice: Lazarus, come out. The dead man came out, his hands and feet bound with bandages, and his face wrapped with a cloth. (John xi, 5-44)
39这才是最完整、最强烈以及最高尚的唤回力量。这是一种爱的体现，因为「耶稣一向爱马大和她的妹妹，也爱拉撒路」。
中 504 · 英 348
Here is the force of recall in its most complete, most strong and most elevated manifestation. It is love, for “Jesus loved Martha and her sister and Lazarus”.
40唤回生命有三个步骤：耶稣的到来、挪开石头、耶稣的召唤或大声呼喊。
中 504 · 英 348
The operation of recall to life—or resurrection—comprises three stages: that of coming, that of taking away the stone and that of recall, i.e. “crying with a loud voice”.
41首先是耶稣的到来。「到来」是为了寻找并找到隔绝召唤者与被召唤者的那道门。为了来到拉撒路的坟前，主耶稣从伯大尼走了「约两公里的路」才抵达耶路撒冷，这段路代表的是召唤的第一种作用：目的是为了抵达最接近召唤对象的地点。
中 504–505 · 英 348
Firstly, coming: “To come and to arrive” is the activity which seeks and finds the last door which separates the recaller from the recalled. The “about two miles” between Bethany and Jerusalem that the Master went in order to arrive at the tomb of Lazarus represent the first effort in the whole operation of recall: that which aims at arriving at the point of maximum approach to the subject of recall.
42接著是挪开石头：这个动作是为了消除所有的忧虑、疲惫和绝望，因为它们就像坟前的石头一样挡住了被召唤者的出口。人如果认为自已永远不可能忆起某件事，或不可能将它唤回到意识之光里，就没有能力运用垂直记忆和道德记忆。疑虑或缺乏信心就像坟前的石头一样，瘫痪了人的忆起能力。石头代表的是缺乏鲜活的感受和信心，更别提对前世或轮回的具体记忆了。你再怎么努力敲门，挡在前面的石头还是会阻碍记忆从深处浮现到觉知之光里。
中 505 · 英 348
Then, the taking away of the stone: this is the effort which vanquishes doubt, depression, fatigue and, lastly, despair, which bar the way to the recalled, like the stone placed before the tomb. By analogy, one is powerless to recall in the domain of vertical memory and moral memory things that one believes are lost for ever, or regarding which one believes that it is impossible to call them to the light of consciousness. This doubt and lack of faith paralyses the effort to recall and is like the stone placed before the tomb. This stone is often—if not always—the cause for many people of the absence of all living feeling and conviction, without speaking of precise and concrete remembrances, of former lives, i.e. of reincarnation. The remembrances have knocked at the door in vain, the stone placed before it not allowing them to come out from their depths and enter into the light of consciousness.
43最后要谈的是召唤作用。「大声呼喊」是唤回生命最终极、最崇高的举动；凭著这股爱的力量就能唤回生命和记忆。
中 505 · 英 349
Lastly, recall: “To cry with a loud voice” is the culminating—and the supreme—effort of the operation of recall through the force of love, whether to life, as was the case with Lazarus, or whether to memory, as is the case of recall with vertical and moral memory.
44在物质世界里，声音越是响亮越能被听见，也越能造成空气的振动。在属灵世界里也是一样的，因为只有如此才能表达背后的奋力和痛苦。奋力和痛苦的声音会被属灵存有听见。此即为何我们在念《玫瑰经》时必须复诵《圣母颂》一百五十遍、《主祷文》十五遍。如果念一声「耶稣!」就能被上界听到，那是因为声音中有强烈的痛苦。因此若想让《玫瑰经》的复诵被上界听到，必须使劲地诵念才行。这其实是施展神圣魔法的万能方式。我不把这一点说出来的话，就是对真相不尊重
中 505 · 英 349
A voice is louder, i.e. more audible, in the physical world, the more intense the vibrations are that it produces in the air. It is otherwise in the spiritual world. There a voice is more audible, i.e. “louder”, the more it expresses underlying effort and suffering. Work and suffering are the things which render our voices audible to the spiritual world and in the spiritual world. These are the factors which create “vibrations” sufficiently “loud” in the spiritual world in order to render our voices audible. This is why the rosary-prayer repeats the Ave Maria one hundred and fifty times and the Pater Noster fifteen times. For if it is suffering which renders audible the ejaculative prayer of a single word—“Jesus!”, for example—it is effort which renders the rosary-prayer audible. I would lack respect for the truth if I did not say that the effort of the rosary-prayer founded on suffering makes it a powerful means—sometimes almost all-powerful—in sacred magic.
45了。奋力而痛苦的强烈呐喊，是整个召唤过程中的关键之举：「耶稣哭了……耶稣心里又非常激动。他来到坟墓前……耶稣大声喊：『拉撒路，出来！』」爱让耶稣恸哭，也促成了死而复生的奇迹它同时也是使人恢复记忆的动力。
中 505–506 · 英 349
Now, the “cry with a loud voice”, which is the decisive act in the whole operation of recall, must be strong both in effort and in suffering: “Jesus wept…Then Jesus, deeply moved again, came to the tomb…Jesus cried with a loud voice: Lazarus, come out.” It is love which wept and which strove to accomplish the miracle of recall from death to life—which is also the case in recall from forgetfulness to memory.
46所以唤回记忆是一种奇迹吗？
中 506 · 英 349
Is recall therefore a miracle?
47……是的，这的确是一种奇迹。但亲爱的朋友，请容许我对我所认为最崇高的奇迹做一点补充，因为这是每一位隐修士和卡巴拉弟子都必须思考的事，那就是：奇迹之外，别无真正的自由。
中 506 · 英 349
…yes, a miracle. But allow me, dear Unknown Friend, to say something concerning the miracle that I believe to be of the highest significance, of which every Christian Hermeticist and every Cabbalist should take account: it is that there is no freedom outside of the miraculous and that man is man only in so far as he lives from the miracle, through the miracle and for the miracle.
48只有活在奇迹中、透过奇迹以及为奇迹而活，才是真正自由的人。在身、心、灵三个层次上，凡是非自动化的事物，都是神奇的，凡是非神奇的事物都是自动化的。我们只能在自动化和被奴役以及奇迹和自由之间做选择。
中 506 · 英 349
All that is not mechanical—physical, psychic and intellectual—is miraculous, and all that is not miraculous is only mechanical—physical, psychic and intellectual. Freedom is a miracle and man is only free in so far as he is not a machine—physical, psychic and intellectual. We have no other choice than between the machine and slavery, on the one hand, and the miracle and freedom, on the other hand.
49「机械化的人」依循的法则是「以最少代价换取最多乐趣」，目的只是为了做出精准的反应。他在头脑层面排斥所有非制式的想法及概念，在心灵层面排斥与制式化的「幸福感」不一致的东西，在肉体上则服膺于制式化的「本能反应」所传达的指令。
中 506 · 英 350
The human machine functions according to the determined programme “maximum pleasure at minimum cost” in a way so as to lend itself to precise prediction in its reactions to given circumstances. In the intellectual domain it rejects every notion and every idea which does not harmonise with the intellectual system established in it; in the psychic domain it rejects all that does not harmonise with the complex of “happiness” established in it; and in the physical domain it automatically follows the orders transmitted by the complex “instinct” established in it.
50富人自称为反共者，穷人自称为亲共者，这些都是自动化反应。但富人如果愿意舍弃财产、顺服于神贫精神，如同圣安东尼和其他圣人，以及加尔默罗会、方济会和道明会那些奉行神贫戒律的修士们，那就是一项奇迹了。圣方济不但展现了疗愈麻疯病患的奇迹，同时也展现了对「神贫圣母」的爱。耶稣基督让拉撒路复活之后，在各各他山被钉上十字架，饱受痛苦折磨，他却说：「父亲哪，赦免他们，因为他们不晓得自己在做什么。」（《路加福音》第23章，第34节）
中 506 · 英 350
It is only the functioning of the human machine when a rich man declares himself anti-communist and a poor man declares himself pro-communist. But it is a miracle—that is to say an act of freedom—when a rich man abandons his possessions and embraces poverty, as did St. Anthony the Great and also many other saints, and also Carmelites, Franciscans, Dominicans, etc., who took the vow of poverty. The miracle of St. Francis is not only the healing of a leper but also the love of St. Francis for “Lady Poverty”. Did not the miracles of Jesus Christ, after the resurrection of Lazarus, culminate in the cross on Calvary where, in the full agony of torture, he said: “Father, forgive them, for they know not what they do” (Luke xxiii, 34)?
51这难道不是他施展过最崇高的奇迹吗？人所有的自主行动都是神奇的；按「本性」或自动化反应所展现的行为则是机械式的。山上宝训教导的就是「行动胜于自动化反应」。
中 506–507 · 英 350
All that one does is miraculous; all intellectual, psychic and physical functioning according to “nature”, i.e. according to human automatism, is mechanical. The Sermon on the Mount is the teaching of doing and of the triumph over functioning.
52要爱你们的仇敌，善待恨恶你们的；为诅咒你们的人祝福…并且为迫害你们的人祷告。这样，你们才可以作天父的儿女。（《路加福音》第6章，第27-28节；《马太福音》第5章，第44-45节）
中 507 · 英 350
Love your enemies, do good to those who hate you, bless those who curse you…and pray for those who persecute you, so that you may be sons of your Father who is in heaven. (Luke vi, 27-28; Matthew v, 44-45)
53这段教诲不就是为了让人摆脱自动化反应，然后施展奇迹
中 507 · 英 350
Is this not a teaching which aims at the liberation of the machine, i.e. of all functioning, and which is a school for the miraculous?
54吗？为诅咒你的人祝福便是一种奇迹。它不再是自动化反应而是「自主的行动」。人只有透过奇迹，才可能展现真正的本质，也才可能示现富创造性的「道」。
中 507 · 英 350
Because to bless those who curse you is a miracle from the point of view of the “normal and natural” functioning of the reactions of the human machine. This does not just happen, it is done (it is created); and I repeat, one only does miracles, and all that is done is a miracle, and nothing is done without it being a miracle. All that which is not a miracle is not really done—it happens, as part of automatic functioning. It is only through the miracle that true being expresses itself, that the creative Word is revealed.
55因此，《约翰福音》的前几段话并不是在传播普世性的唯理主义，譬如斯多葛学派对「理性」的主张。不，《约翰福音》宣告的是奇迹的普世性任务。其源头是创造性的道，而不是头脑的自动化反应：
中 507 · 英 350
It is wrong, therefore, to interpret the phrases at the beginning of the Gospel according to John as the teaching of a kind of cosmic rationalism, analogous to the Stoic doctrine of nous (“mind”). No, the phrases at the beginning of the Gospel according to John openly declare the cosmic role of the miracle, and that the world is due to a miracle, i.e. that it was made by the creative Word, and not that it was due to any functioning, to any automatic—even highly intellectual—process:
56上帝藉著他创造万有；在整个创造中，没有一样不是藉著他造的。（《约翰福音》第1章，第3节)
中 507 · 英 351
All things were made through the Word, and nothing that was made was made without him. (John i, 3)
57〔中译本未译此段〕
中 — · 英 351
This is what the Evangelist says; and what we have said above concerning the miracle and the machine—i.e. concerning “doing” and “functioning”—is only the microcosmic analogy to the statement of macrocosmic significance from the Gospel according to John.
58「上帝藉著他创造万有」，其中包含了垂直记忆和道德记忆的唤回。由于唤回记忆是一种「行动」而非「自动化反应」，所以是一项奇迹。唤回逻辑式记忆，则是「行动」和「自动化反应」的混合体。唤回机械式记忆，则显然是一种「自动化反应」，因为其中的道德成分是最少的。
中 508 · 英 351
Now, “all things made through the Word” includes also recall in vertical and moral memory. The act of recall belongs to the sphere of “doing” and therefore to that of the miraculous, and not to the sphere of “functioning”. Recall in “logical memory” is a mixture of doing and functioning. Lastly, recall in mechanical memory is only functioning, i.e. the act of moral recall reduced to a minimum.
59如果唤回之举与拉撒路的复活类似，那么遗忘又是什么呢？
中 508 · 英 351
If recall is an act analogous to the resurrection of Lazarus, then what is forgetting?
60遗忘和唤回一样可以分为不同的层次，它也会自动出现或半自动地出现，或是有意识地自由出现。在机械式记忆里，事情会自动地被遗忘。至于逻辑式记忆，如果不常将它唤回到觉知中，也会逐渐远离和消逝。在垂直和道德记忆里，任何事情都不会被遗忘；如果被遗忘，也是意志基于道德做出的选择。
中 508 · 英 351
Forgetting outlines a scale analogous to that of recall. It can take place automatically, semi-automatically, and in a free and conscious way, according to the category of memory in which it is placed. In mechanical memory one forgets automatically; things are just forgotten. In logical memory things become distant and are effaced little by little if one does not recall them from time to time to the field of conscious attention. In moral memory and in vertical memory nothing is forgotten; forgetting here is a moral act of will.
61现在让我们以相同的方式来探索遗忘，也就是从「遗忘是一种有觉知和可理解的行为」开始谈起。
中 508 · 英 351
Let us follow the same procedure as is the case in recall, i.e. let us begin at the end of the scale where forgetting is an act of consciousness and where it is understandable—where it takes place in the light of consciousness.
62每个人都知道有觉知的行为是需要集中注意力的，集中注意力又意味著带著觉知忘掉不相干的事物。当我们诵念《主祷文》时，不但会暂时忘掉每日的琐事，甚至连其他的祷告文也都记不得了。同样地，如果全神贯注于属灵世界和上主的价值，我们也会忘掉现象界的价值。
中 508 · 英 351
Now, there is no one who does not know through experience that all conscious effort entails concentration or contemplation, and that concentration and contemplation signify conscious and willed forgetting of many things which do not relate to the subject of concentration or contemplation. One knows that when one prays the Pater Noster one forgets not only one’s daily affairs but also all other prayers for the time that one prays the Pater Noster.
63简单地说，灵魂与神结合的三个程序：净化、启蒙、合一，就是灵魂越来越专注于神。有关灵魂与神合一的体验，圣十字若望曾经说过：
中 508 · 英 351
It is the same with spiritual and divine values, and those of the phenomenal world. The three stages on the way towards the soul’s union with God—those of purification, illumination and union—are simply the history of a single growing effort of concentration of the entire soul upon God. St. John of the Cross says of the effect of the experience of actual union of the soul’s powers with God:
64…灵魂在内坛的酒窖里，喝下了具有最高智慧的上主所提供的神秘醇酒后，便忘却了世上的一切。以往
的知识、所有的学问和他今日获得的智慧相比，全都显得肤浅又无知。(St. John of the Cross, A Spiritual Canticle xxvi, 7, 10; trsl. D. Lewis, London, 1909, pp.204-205)
中 508–509 · 英 351
…all the powers of the soul together, because of the union in the inner cellar, drink of the Beloved…This draught of God’s most deep wisdom makes the soul forget all the things of this world, and consider all its previous knowledge, and the knowledge of the whole world besides, as pure ignorance in comparison with this knowledge. (St. John of the Cross, A Spiritual Canticle xxvi, 7, 10; trsl. D. Lewis, London, 1909, pp. 204-205)
65他还说过：
中 509 · 英 352
And again:
66…记忆越是与神合一，就越会丧失各式各样的知识，最后这些知识会完全消失，但记忆却达到了完美的程度。当合一境界开始出现时，记忆会突然忘掉各种事情；这些事情的形式和相关的认知会逐渐从记忆中消失…记忆完全被神所吸引。但习惯合一境界的灵魂并不会忘记与道德或伦理相关的事物，反而能更完美地做出恰当和必要的行动，仿佛神用一种特殊方式赐给了灵魂的记忆真正需要的认知和觉知。（St.John of the Cross, The Ascent of Mount Carmel III, i,5; trsl. D. Lewis, London, 1906, pp. 244-245)
中 509 · 英 352
…the more the memory is united to God the more it loses all distinct knowledge, and at last all such fades utterly away, when the state of perfection is reached. In the beginning, when this is going on, great forgetfulness ensues, for these forms and knowledge fall into oblivion…the memory is lost in God. But he who has attained to the habit of union does not forget, in this way, that which relates to moral and natural reason; he performs in much greater perfection all necessary and befitting actions, though by the ministry of forms and knowledge, in the memory, supplied in a special manner by God. (St. John of the Cross, The Ascent of Mount Carmel III, i, 5; trsl. D. Lewis, London, 1906, pp. 244-245)
67我想再加上一句，胜王瑜伽、奉爱瑜伽及智慧瑜伽的导师，也都是在教人如何忘却现象界的一切，进入彻底的禅定境界。我们也可以从神秘的卡巴拉和伊斯兰的苏菲教派中发现有关遗忘的教诲。
中 509 · 英 352
I may add that the masters of Raja-yoga, Bhakti-yoga and Jnana-yoga teach the practice of complete forgetting of the phenomenal world with a view to attaining perfect contemplation. The teaching of forgetting is found also in the mystical Cabbala and in Mohammedan mysticism, e.g. that of Sufism.
68遗忘是从一种意识状态到另一种意识状态的转换方式。人即便是进入睡眠或是「自然的狂喜」状态，也必须忘记白天的世界，才能进入夜晚的世界。我们为了入睡，必须懂得如何忘记。
中 509–510 · 英 352
Now, forgetting is the means of transition from one state of consciousness to another. Even in the case of sleep, which can be considered as a “natural ecstasy”, one has to forget the world of the day in order to be able to pass into the world of the night. In order to fall asleep one has to be able to forget. Insomnia is due to the inability to forget.
69失眠就是无法忘怀所导致的。那么苏醒呢？苏醒是再度记起白天的世界，忘却夜晚的世界。人如果无法忘记夜晚的世界，就不算是完全醒来这种情况经常发生。这么一来夜晚和白天势必混淆在一起，日间执行的工作就会出现障碍因为夜晚的记忆干扰了专注力。
中 510 · 英 352
And awakening? Awakening is simultaneously an act of recall of the world of the day and an act of forgetting the nocturnal world. Awakening would be incomplete—which it, moreover, often is—if one did not forget the experiences of the nocturnal world. The night would then be mingled with the day and human consciousness would be hindered in its capacity with respect to the tasks and duties of the day—its concentration being hampered by the haunting memory of nocturnal remembrances.
70那么出生和死亡呢？
中 510 · 英 352
And birth and death?
71如果说灵魂与神合一是对现象界的遗忘以及对神的忆起，那么死亡就是忆起了上界、忘却了下界。灵魂与神达成合一的三个程序，净化、启蒙、合一，在死亡后会再度出现：首先是进入炼狱接受净化（〔Catharsis〕洗涤心灵），接著是进入天国接受启蒙；天国指的是灵魂与神合一的状态，类似于密修者活著时的密契体验；这种合一状态会变成惯性，也就是所谓的至福感，然后灵魂就会忆起地球和曾经面临的试炼。这种记忆会使人的一切行为变得「更完善」（圣十字若望曾说过，这是因为灵魂习惯与神合一而恢复了记忆的功能），我在此应该解释一下，所谓「人的一切行为」指的是在世间的作为。
中 510 · 英 352
If the soul’s mystical union with God is forgetting of the phenomenal world and recall of God, death is simultaneously the call from above and forgetting below. The three stages of the way leading to the soul’s union with God—purification, illumination and union—are repeated after death: purgatory is purification (catharsis), which precedes illumination or heaven, and heaven is the state of the soul when it arrives at union with God, analogous to that experienced by mystics during their terrestrial life. This union, there as here, becomes habitual—which is a sovereign good for the soul—and then it remembers anew the earth and its trials. Memory then manifests a “greater perfection” (says St. John of the Cross concerning the resuscitated functions of memory in the case of the soul which has the habit of union) in all its action—let us add, actions directed towards the earth.
72这便是圣人为神做工的动力来源。圣人是「习惯」与神合一的灵魂，因此拥有圣十字若望所说的更高层次的属灵记忆。他们不追求与神合一，因为他们已经与神合为一体了。
中 510 · 英 353
This is the motivation for the blessed work of saints. Saints are souls who possess the “habit of union” and are therefore in possession of the higher spiritualised memory spoken of by St. John of the Cross. They do not seek union with God; they are united with God. This is why they act—their faces being turned towards the earth and not towards God—in the name of God on earth. They act, being united with God, in the guise of organs of his will.
73他们在世间的作为—朝向世界而非神都是奉主之名在运作的。天国的属灵存有—天使—也是一样的。守护天使如果一直朝向神，就会著迷于对神的默观而无法保护人类。幸亏祂们已经习惯与神合一，意志已经与神的旨意结合，所以才能履行守护人类的使命。祂们和圣人一样总是面对著地球。这便是祂们的奉献精神背后的动力。
中 510–511 · 英 353
It is the same with the celestial hierarchies—with Angels, for example. Guardian Angels could never be guardians of human beings if their looks were turned towards God, if they were absorbed in contemplation of God. It is thanks to their habitual union with God, i.e. thanks to the accomplished fact of the union of their will with the divine will, that they are able to fulfill their task as guardians of men. They know the divine will in a “blind” way—through the dim intuition of their own will, i.e. through perfect faith—whilst what they see is the earth and human life on earth. Their faces, as are those of the saints, are turned towards the earth. This is the motivation for devotion to the guardian Angels.
74至于诞生这件事，则可能是源自「神圣」的力量，或「自然」的作用力，亦即顺服于神或「地球的呼唤」所带来的结果。前者意味著臣服于垂直或道德记忆，类似于拉撒路复活的奇迹；后者则是在半自愿、半被迫的情况下，受世间引力吸引而诞生出来，接著便逐渐忘却了上界的经验。「圣诞」显然不是这样的，因为促使灵魂降生的是对上主的记忆。灵魂并没有因化身成人而忘却神，它对神仍保有清晰的记忆。
中 511 · 英 353
With respect to birth, it can, also, be either “holy” or “natural”, i.e. it can either be an act of obedience to divine will or rather it can be effected as a consequence of a “call from the earth”. A soul can be sent to the earth or it can be attracted by the earth. In the first case it is an act analogous to the recall of vertical and moral memory, i.e. analogous to the miracle of the resurrection of Lazarus. In the latter case it is an event that is half-voluntary and half-involuntary, where the soul falls—often without realising it—into the sphere of terrestrial attraction, which bears it to birth, and thereby it is made little by little to forget its experiences above. Birth is then a forgetting of heaven and simultaneously a recall to the earth.
75灵魂是在与神合一的「惯性状态」下投生的。这个烙印会一直在灵魂意志的记号中发挥作用，从「圣诞」一直到它在地球上的生命结束为止。这样的灵魂才有资格谈「天职」或是「被拣选」。真正的天职并不是按自己的品味、兴趣甚至理想去达成的。自行决定的「任务」，即使出发点是为了造福世界，也可能为人类史带来一场大混乱。这些不合时宜的「任务」导致了许多灾难的发生——就像彗星横扫过一样，干扰了真正的祥和以及富建设性的能量流动。
中 511 · 英 353
This is not so with “holy birth”. Here it is the remembrance of the divine which is the force which accomplishes incarnation. It is not thanks to forgetting of the divine that the soul is then incarnated, but rather thanks to its remembering. It is in the state of “habitual union” with God that the soul is incarnated. Then its will does not lose memory of the divine. This memory acts in it, imprinted as it is in the soul’s will, during the whole terrestrial life which follows a “holy birth”. One could then speak of a “mission”, or an “election”…and rightly, because such a mission is the only one which really exists. For the true mission is not what the human being proposes to do on the earth according to his tastes, his interests and even his ideals, but rather what God wants him to do. Arbitrary “missions”, although due to the best intentions in the world, have only contributed confusion to human history. It is to these inopportune “missions” that we owe many crises upsetting the life of mankind’s living traditions—interrupting, in the guise of passing comets, the peaceful and constructive flow of true progress.
76人在地球上真正的任务，就是为灵性的升华及净化效劳。这股驱力能活化和强化灵修传统。自行决定的任务则是以个人的创见取代传统，目标是要改变历史的走向。极端一点的说法是：只有天职才能让地球上的人类——家庭、文明、文化、宗教等等变得更完美；自行决定的任务则往往会招惹火星人或金星人前来干涉地球的事务。
中 511 · 英 353
A true mission on the earth serves the cause of the ennoblement and spiritualisation of that which is, i.e. of what lives as tradition. It brings an impulse effecting the rejuvenation and intensification of tradition. Arbitrary missions, on the contrary, aim at revolutionising the course of mankind’s history and substituting specific innovations for what lives as tradition. In pushing this to the extreme one could say: a true mission brings to greater perfection everything human on the earth—the family, civilisation, culture, religion, etc., whilst arbitrary missions can result in summoning the intervention of Martians or Venusians so that they rule affairs on the earth!
77诞生、苏醒、忆起，以及死亡、沉睡、遗忘，构成了现实的两股「支柱般的力量」。它们会显现在忆起和遗忘里，也会显现在苏醒和沉睡、诞生和死亡的韵律中，在呼吸、血液循环和营养的吸收之中也有它们的作用力。它们是所有领域—身、心、灵三个层次的「是」与「不是」。
中 512 · 英 354
Now, birth, awakening and recall, on the one hand, and death, falling asleep and forgetting, on the other hand, constitute, so to say, the two “pillar-forces” of reality. They manifest in remembering and forgetting, in the rhythm of sleeping and waking and in that of birth and death—as well as in the respiration of organisms, in the circulation of blood, and in alimentation. They are the “yes” and “no” in every domain—mental, psychic and physical.
78福音《箴言》中所提到的：「你们说话，是，就说是，不是，就说不是；再多说便是出于那邪恶者。」（《马太福音》第5章，第37节）彰显出了上面那段话的含义。「是」与「不是」乃现实的本质或真相，任何「多余的话」都是出自魔或蛇的作用力。创世记里蛇所说的话都是「多余的话」「是」与「不是」之外的第三种话。
中 512 · 英 354
The Gospel maxim, “Let what you say be simply ‘Yes’ or ‘No’; anything more than this comes from evil” (Matthew v, 37) reveals its significance in this context. The “yes” and the “no”—this is the essential of reality, i.e. the truth, pure and simple, whilst the “surplus” comes from evil, i.e. it belongs to the sphere of the serpent. For the serpent of Genesis has his own word—the word which is the “surplus” over “yes” and “no”. He is in possession of a third term.
79现在让我们回到这封信一开始所提到的：蛇说「你们不会死」，难道它是在说谎吗？或者它说的是真相，只不过这种真相只专属于蛇的世界？换言之：除了「是」与「不是」所代表的「生」与「死」之外，蛇究竟多说了什么？
中 512 · 英 354
And here we return to the question that we posed at the beginning of this Letter, namely; Has the serpent, having said, “You will not die”, simply told a lie, or has he stated a truth from the range of truths proper to the domain of the serpent? In other words: What is the “surplus” that the serpent adds to the “yes” and “no” understood as life and death?
80亲爱的朋友，假如你认识了前几封信里提到的有关「生命」与「电能」、「圣洁本质」与「蛇的本质」在根本上的差异，就能洞悉在「是」与「不是」所代表的「生」与「死」之外的那些「多余的话」背后的秘密。
中 512 · 英 354
If you accept, dear Unknown Friend, what we have said in the preceding Letters on the difference in principle that there is between life and electricity, between the principle of the Virgin and that of the serpent, you will certainly be able to penetrate more deeply the secret of the “surplus” offered and promised by the serpent to humanity concerning the “yes” and “no” understood as life and death.
81这个秘密是：蛇允诺了一种「结晶化功能」，若是按照它的「卷曲」法则来修练，人便有可能抵挡死亡或是对死亡「免疫」。这种结晶化功能是透过摩擦力产生的；它是一种在「是」与「不是」之间挣扎所产生的电能。
中 512 · 英 354
Here is this secret: the serpent offers and promises such a crystallisation, according to the principle of enfoldment, that the human being will resist death and become, so to say, “death-proof”, immune to death. This crystallisation is effected through friction, i.e. by the electrical energy which is produced by the struggle between “yes” and “no” in man.
82亲爱的朋友，你或许已经知道某些门派，像是密修派或其他派别，一直在教导「结晶化」的锻练方式；另外一些派别则教导如何「放射光热」，亦即完全去除结晶化作用，使人转化成「太阳」或发射能量的中枢。「那时候，义人在天父的国度里，将好像太阳发射光辉。」（《马太福音》第13章，第43节）
中 512–513 · 英 354
Without doubt you know, dear Unknown Friend, that there are schools—occult or other—which teach and practise crystallisation and that there are other schools which teach and practise radiation, i.e. the complete de-crystallisation of the human being and his transformation into a “sun”, into a centre of radiation. “Then the righteous will shine like the sun in the kingdom of their Father” (Matthew xiii, 43)—this is the practical aim of “schools of radiation”, to which that of Christian Hermeticism belongs.
83—此即「光热派」或是基督隐修之道的灵修目标。「结晶派」的修练方式也有许多类型，而且十分普及。有些是完全秘密的，抱持的是非常严肃的宗旨；有些则广为人知，通常是以「健康、回春、长寿」等一般性的动机做为诉求。我不想谈论那些秘修派的方法，因为那是他们的而非我的秘密。我也不想谈论那些已经变成流行的锻练方式。我即将举例说明的某个玄学派，你一旦了解其宗旨和方法，就会明白我的感受了。我之所以选择下述这个玄学派别做为例子，是因为它介乎秘修派和伪装成流行的养生锻练法之间，更何况它已经选择公开教诲的内容，因此我有权谈论它，引用其资料。
中 513 · 英 354
The “schools of crystallisation” are quite numerous and widespread. There are those which are entirely secret, with very serious intentions; there are also those which are known in the guise of almost popular movements for “health, rejuvenation and longevity”. I shall not speak here of the practices of entirely secret schools, the secret not being mine but that of others. Also I shall not speak about the almost popular movement, because it will certainly be easy to understand their aim and methods after having understood the aim and method of an occult school that I have chosen as an illustrative example. I have chosen this particular occult school as an example, because it is something between the secret schools and the almost popular movements and because it has itself taken the decision to show itself in broad daylight, thus authorising me to speak of it and to cite its documents, accessible to everyone.

中 513 · 英 355
I have in mind the school of G. I. Gurdjieff, and I am going to cite the work In Search of the Miraculous by P. D. Ouspensky. Now, the following is the teaching of Gurdjieff, such as it has been understood and formulated by Ouspensky, concerning the practical task of survival:
85一段内文。以下便是邬斯宾斯基对葛吉夫教诲的理解和诠释，内容是关于星光体如何继续存留的修练方式：在某一次这种类型的聚会上，有人问起轮回的可能性以及和死者沟通的案例是否可信。「许多事都有发生

的可能，」葛吉夫说，「不过我们必须了解，人活著的时候和死后的存在特质，可以说是截然不同的。如果你把人看成是一种机器，一切都受外境影响，那么他此刻是这样，下一刻是那样，再下一刻又变成了第三种模样，可以说毫无未来可言；最终他只会被埋葬。尘归尘，土归土，这句话非常适用于他。因此他为了拥有未来性，必须促成某种结晶化功能，一种内在特质的融合作用，或不受外境影响的自主性。如果人的内在有任何东西可以抵挡外境的影响，那个东西就能抵挡肉体的死亡……不过就算是存活下来，未来还是有许多的可能性。某些结晶化比较彻底的例子显示人死后可以直接转世，其他例子则是所谓的「在另一边继续存活」。这两个例子都意味著生命力会在「星光层」内延续下去，或透过「星光体」的协助延续下去。我想你们都知道这是什么东西，但是你们所熟悉的系统以及这个名称的含义，暗示著所有的人都具备星光体。事实上，这是非常错误的见解。所谓的星光体是透过一种融合作用产生的，过程中必须经历非常艰辛的内在工作和挣扎。人并非生来就具有星光体，而且极少人拥有它。它一旦形成，便可能在肉身死亡后继续存活，也可能在另一个肉体内重生……这种融合作用或内在的合一状态是以「摩擦」的方式达成的，而摩擦又来自于「是」与「不是」之间的挣扎。人的内心如果没有挣扎，如果内在没有任何冲
突而能运作自如的话，他就会安住在自己的真相上面。但如果他的心里出现了挣扎，尤其是在特定的思路上，那么就会形成固著的特质，就会开始「结晶化」…结晶化的作用可能发生在任何基础上，譬如在一名非常厉害、名符其实的土匪身上。我就认识这样的一个高加索土匪，他可以拿著步枪一动也不动地躲在路边的石头后面长达八小时。你办得到吗？可是你要明白，在整个过程中，他的内心是不断在挣扎的。他既热又渴，蚊虫也不停地叮咬，但他却完全不动地站著。另外有一名僧侣，他很恐惧恶魔，整晚不停地磕头祷告，于是结晶化作用就产生了……而这样的人也可能变得「不朽」。(P. D. Ouspensky, In Search of the Miraculous, London, 1969, pp. 32-32)
中 513–515 · 英 355
On one occasion, at one of these meetings, someone asked about the possibility of reincarnation, and whether it was possible to believe in cases of communication with the dead. “Many things are possible,” said G. (Gurdjieff). “But it is necessary to understand that man’s being, both in life and after death, if it does exist after death, may be very different in quality. The ‘man-machine’ with whom everything depends upon external influences, with whom everything happens, who is now one, the next moment another, and the next moment a third, has no future of any kind; he is buried and that is all. Dust returns to dust. This applies to him. In order to be able to speak of any kind of future life there must be a certain crystallisation, a certain fusion of man’s inner qualities, a certain independence of external influences. If there is anything in a man able to resist external influences, then this very thing itself may also be able to resist the death of the physical body…But even if something survives, its future can be very varied. In certain cases of fuller crystallisation, what people call ‘reincarnation’ may be possible after death, and, in other cases, what people call ‘existence on the other side’. In both cases it is the continuation of life in the ‘astral body’, or with the help of the ‘astral body’. You know what the expression ‘astral body’ means. But the systems with which you are acquainted and which use this expression state that all men have an ‘astral body’. This is quite wrong. What may be called the ‘astral body’ is obtained by means of fusion, that, is, by means of terribly hard inner work and struggle. Man is not born with it. And only very few men acquire an ‘astral body’. If it is formed it may continue to live after the death of the physical body, and it may be born again in another physical body…Fusion, inner unity, is obtained by means of ‘friction’, by the struggle between ‘yes’ and ‘no’ in man. If a man lives without inner struggle, if everything happens in him without opposition, if he goes wherever he is drawn or wherever the wind blows, he will remain such as he is. But if a struggle begins in him, and particularly if there is a definite line in this struggle, then, gradually, permanent traits begin to form themselves, he begins to ‘crystallise’…Crystallisation is possible on any foundations. Take for example a brigand, a really good, genuine brigand. I knew such brigands in the Caucasus. He will stand with a rifle behind a stone by the roadside for eight hours without stirring. Could you do this? All the time, mind you, a struggle is going on in him. He is thirsty and hot, and flies are biting him; but he stands still. Another is a monk; he is afraid of the devil; all night long he beats his head on the floor and prays. Thus crystallisation is achieved…Such people can become immortal.” (P. D. Ouspensky, In Search of the Miraculous, London, 1969, pp. 31-32)
86现在让我们思考一下引文中的几个重点。第一，所谓的「星光体」，是从肉体中的精微体产生的活力运输工具。根据这段引文的说法，「不朽」并非人与生俱来的权利，也不是神赐予的礼物，而是透过结晶化制造出来的一种崭新的灵体；它可以抵挡死亡，在肉体毁灭后继续存活。换言之，这崭新的灵体不是神创造的，是人在自己体内制造出来的。它是从「是」与「不是」的摩擦中诞生的结晶体。由此可知，盗贼、僧侣以及练家子型的玄秘家，都是透过挣扎和努力产生电能，进而让自己变得「不朽」。
中 515 · 英 356
Let us now take account of the essential points in the quoted text. Firstly, it is the physical body which gives birth to what is called in the quotation the “astral body” which will be the bearer of survival. Then, according to the text, immortality is neither a birthright of the human soul nor a gift of divine grace—it is made by means of the crystallisation of a new body within the physical body which can resist death and survive the destruction of the physical body. That is to say, the soul created by God does not exist; it must be created by the human being from within the human physical body. Thus it is a quantity of energy crystallised within the human physical body and engendered through this latter, produced by friction or the struggle between “yes” and “no” in man. For both the robber and the monk, and also the occultist, can become immortal through the energy that they produce by their efforts.
87这涉及的是一套人体工程，也就是在人体内建构一座高塔，一栋有「四房」或四层楼的屋子；它从地面高耸至天国，从凡间延伸至永恒世界。《圣经》记载了这种「高耸入云」的建塔方式，以及它如何「显扬我们自己的名，免得我们被分散到世界各地」（《创世纪》第11章，第4节）。此即构建巴别塔的理念和上千年的筑造方式。以下是葛吉夫的说法：

It is a matter of a plan of construction, from within the physical body, of a tower or a “house of four rooms” or levels (Ouspensky, ibid., p. 44), rising from the sphere of mortality to that of immortality, from earth to heaven. 88有一种非常古老的教诲，我们可以在许多旧有的和新的学说中发现线索。它主张一个发展完全的人是由四种精微体构成的。这四种精微程度不同的体相互渗透，形成了四种有机结构，它们之间虽然有明确的关系，却能独立自主地运作。这四种有机结构之所以会出现，是因为人体这个极其复杂的有机体，在某种情况下形成了新的精微体，可以提供意识一个比肉身更方便、更有感应力的使唤工具………在其他情况下，第二个精微体内又会形成第三个精微体，第三个精微体也有自己的特质…这第三个精微体内又可能形成第四个精微体，它们的不同就像第三个不同于第二个、第二个不同于第一个一样。
中 516 · 英 356
According to an ancient teaching, traces of which may be found in many systems, old and new, a man who has attained the full development possible for man, a man in the full sense of the word, consists of four bodies. These four bodies are composed of substances which gradually become finer and finer, mutually interpenetrate one another, and form four independent organisms, standing in a definite relationship to one another but capable of independent action. The reason why it is possible for four bodies to exist is that the human organism, that is, the physical body, has such a complex organisation that, under certain conditions, a new independent organism can grow in it, affording a much more convenient and responsive instrument for the activity of consciousness than the physical body…In this second body, under certain conditions, a third body can grow, again having characteristics of its own…In the third body, under certain conditions, a fourth can grow, which differs as much from the third as the third differs from the second and the second from the first…(p. 40)
89有一种东方教诲用以下的方式来形容这四种精微体的功能、渐进的发展过程以及发展条件：现在让我们想像一下，有一个装满各种金属粉末的容器或曲颈瓶，其中的粉末是分开的，每当瓶子的位置无预警地被移动时，粉末的相对位置就会跟著变动…这些粉末之间的互动不可能以机械化的混合方式固定下来，但粉末是可以融合的，其本质确保了这一点。如果要办到这一点，就必须在容器底端生起一种特殊的火，经由加热让粉末融合结合在一起。以这样的方式融合的粉末会变成一种化合物…容器里的物质因而变成了不可分割的「整体」。这便是第二个精微体形成的过程。
中 516–517 · 英 357
An Eastern teaching describes the functions of the four bodies, their gradual growth, and the conditions of this growth, in the following way: Let us imagine a vessel or a retort filled with various metallic powders. The powders are not in any way connected with each other and every accidental change in the position of the retort changes the relative position of the powders…It is impossible to stabilise the interrelation of powders in a state of mechanical mixture. But the powders may be fused; the nature of the powders makes this possible. To do this a special kind of fire must be lighted under the retort which, by heating and melting the powders, finally fuses them together. Fused in this way the powders will be in the state of a chemical compound…The contents of the retort have become indivisible, “individual”. This is a picture of the formation of the second body. The fire by means of which fusion is attained is produced by “friction”, which in its turn is produced in man by the struggle between “yes” and “no”…
90达成融合作用的火是由「摩擦力」产生的，摩擦力又出自于「是」与「不是」的挣扎……第三种精微体的形成，相当于把新的特性注入原本的化合物中，……第四种精微体的形成则相当于固化那些新加入的特性。只有「四种精微体」都发展齐全的人，才算是真正完整的「人」。这种人拥有许多平常人没有的特质，其中之一就是「不朽」。（同前著作，pp.40,43-44)
中 517 · 英 357
The process of imparting new properties to the alloy corresponds to the process of the formation of the third body…The process of fixing these acquired properties corresponds to the process of the formation of the fourth body. And only the man who possesses four fully developed bodies can be called a “man” in the full sense of the word. This man possesses many properties which ordinary man does not possess. One of these properties is immortality. (P. D. Ouspensky, In Search of the Miraculous, London, 1969, pp. 40, 43-44)
91由此可知，「容器底端生起的火」是由摩擦力造成的，摩擦力又出自于「是」与「不是」的挣扎，因此这种火就是我们所谓的「电能」。有了这种电能或摩擦力，才出现了结晶化的作用过程。
中 517 · 英 357
Now, the “special fire lit under the retort” is due to friction which is, in its turn, the product of the struggle between “yes” and “no”. This fire is therefore what we understand by “electricity”. It is therefore thanks to electricity, or energy produced by friction, that the process of crystallisation works.

中 517 · 英 357
The architects of the tower of Babel also made use of fire for the preparation of its materials of construction. “‘Come, let us make bricks, and burn them thoroughly.’ And they had brick for stone, and bitumen for mortar” (Genesis xi, 3).
93「巴别塔」的建造方式依循的是「反向」的结晶化作用。正常的结晶体「石头」是由气体变成液体、液体变成固体的最后结果。依照这种程序的作用力，蒸汽先是变成水（液体），水又结成冰，所以冰就是结晶化的蒸汽。同样地，一个笼统却热诚的意图会先形成一股逻辑推演的动能，然后才变成定义明确的法则。换一种说法是：灵变成了心，心变成了身。
中 517–518 · 英 357
The essence of the method of “construction of the tower of Babel” is inverse crystallisation. Normal crystallisation—the “stone”—is the final state of the process of transition from the gaseous to the liquid state and from the liquid to the solid state. Thus vapour becomes water (liquid) and water becomes ice. Ice is crystallised vapour. Similarly, a general but warm intention becomes a current of discursive thought which, in its turn, results in a well-defined formula. Or in still other terms: the spiritual becomes psychic and the psychic becomes corporeal.
