Is this scheme realisable or is it simply an illusion, pure and simple? Although this question belongs to the framework of problems of the sixteenth Major Arcanum of the Tarot, and although it will be treated in the sixteenth Letter, let us nonetheless consider some facts with a view to coming to an answer.
98我指的就是那些仍然存在于人间的精微体，所谓的死者或「鬼」的显相。鬼的确是存在的，这不是信或不信的问题，而是真实存在的现象。除了一些个人的亲身经验之外，有太多的资料足以证实鬼的存在。人死亡之后，生前的「某些特质」会伪装成一股活跃的能量，以向外显化的方式（声音、动作等）现形。我们可以说有一种固化的能量会透过死亡释放，它可以凝结住、不至于消散，而且是以实体或「形体」显相的。
中 518–519 · 英 358
The facts that I have in mind are those of corporeal survival, i.e. physical manifestations that one attributes—rightly or wrongly—to dead people or to “ghosts”. Ghosts exist. This is not a question of belief; it is a matter of fact. There is an immense literature, without speaking of facts that one can find in the sphere of personal experience, which bears witness to the existence of ghosts. Now it is no longer a matter of believing or denying; now it is a matter only of understanding and explaining. Ghosts exist therefore. Thus it happens from time to time after someone’s death that this person or “something” of him or similar to him manifests in an outward and physical way (noises, movements, etc.) in the guise of an active energy. It is as if a certain quantity of energy, freed through death, but remaining condensed and not dispersed, manifests as an entity or as an individual “body”.
99我从一则关于鬼这个现象的分析中，摘录了它的几种特征：
中 519 · 英 358
An analysis of the manifestations of ghosts has enabled me to extract from them the following characteristic traits:
1001. 鬼是一种由心理和生理电能构成的实体，相对于活著的正常人，它拥有的意识层次比较低等；
中 519 · 英 359
1. a ghost is an entity made up of psycho-physiological electrical energy, with an inferior consciousness in comparison with that of a normal human person;
1012. 借由鬼的行径和行为方式所观察到的意识状态，是非常狭隘和极端执著的——这使人不禁想用「疯狂」一词来描述，因为它总是以单一的激情、癖好或固著想法的结晶体现身。
中 519 · 英 359
2. the consciousness which is revealed by the actions of a ghost, and by its way of acting in general, is very limited and extremely specialised—one is tempted to characterise it as “maniacal”, since it manifests itself as the crystallisation of a single passion, a single habit, or a single fixed idea;
1023. 鬼的组成能量会随著时间递减——除非身边的人以一种肯定和赞同的态度支持它，否则很快就消散了。你可以透过教会的驱鬼仪式或个人的祈祷使它消失，或者还有一种需要勇气的办法，那就是将它扣住、然后把它的能量吸进自己体内，以驱散它的电能。但是我不推荐后面这种办法，因为当鬼的能量进入你体内时，会带来极强的触电感。在此我必须进一步说明的是，这种触电感也让人确实明了鬼的「灵体」本质上就是电能。同
时这也提供了证据，使人心知肚明鬼并不是死者的灵魂，而是尚未甩掉的沉重包袱。如果有人将鬼的电能吸入体内而将其驱散，不久之后死者必定会以托梦方式表达感激，因为他终于卸下了这个沉重的包袱。
中 519–520 · 英 359
3. the energy of which the ghost is constituted becomes weakened with time—provided that it is not nourished by an affirmative and favourable attitude from its human entourage; it fades away. One can make it disappear through the Church ritual of exorcism, or by individual prayer, or lastly by a special action which demands courage and which consists in clasping and breathing in the ghost, in such a way as to receive it into oneself and of oneself to make the electrical energy of the ghost dissipate. I dare not recommend this latter method because it entails the experience of an electric shock—which can be excessive—at the moment when the energy of the ghost passes into your organism. I may add, however, that it is this experience of an electric shock which gives absolute certainty with respect to the electrical nature of the “body” of the ghost. At the same time it can also supply proof—in the inner forum of consciousness, it goes without saying—that the ghost is not the soul of the departed one, and that it is a burden to him, being bound to the soul of the departed by a heavy link of responsibility. In the case that I have mentioned, soon after the dissipation of the ghost’s electrical energy through its reception into oneself, the departed one hastens to make acknowledgement of his gratitude, by means of a very vivid and clear dream, for his deliverance from this heavy burden.
103所以鬼究竟是什么？套句葛吉夫的说法，鬼就是心理活动的固化作用在肉体内形成的产物；它能够抵挡肉体的死亡。这也是葛吉夫所谓的「星光体」，「它一旦形成，就可以在肉体死亡后继续存活……如果它不重生，那么一阵子之后也会消亡；它并非不朽的，却可以在肉体消失后存留一段时间」。当然，葛吉夫和隐修之道论及的星光体完全无关，因为后者和灵魂的精神记忆有关。
中 520 · 英 359
What, then, is a ghost? It is exactly what Gurdjieff teaches concerning the product of psychic crystallisation effected from within the physical body, and which can resist the death of the latter. This is the “astral body” of which Gurdjieff said that, “if it is formed it may continue to live after the death of the physical body…if it is not re-born, then, in the course of time, it also dies; it is not immortal but it can live long after the death of the physical body” (Ouspensky, op. cit, p. 32). Of course, the “astral body” spoken of by Gurdjieff has nothing to do with the “astral body” of Hermeticism, which latter is, truth to tell, simply the totality of the soul’s psychic memories.
104鬼永远是结晶化作用的产物，亦即欲望、激情或强烈的意图在人体内固化成的一种「心理情结」能量。因此，「厉害的土匪」可以「拿著步枪一动也不动地躲在路边的石头后面长达八小时」，「另外有一名僧侣……他很恐惧恶魔，整晚不停地磕头祷告」，这两者的体内都会形成固化的高密度「情结」能量，这是一种心理活动和电能的「分身」，可以用来阻隔死亡。
中 520 · 英 359
A ghost is always constituted as a consequence of crystallisation, i.e. crystallisation of a desire, a passion, or a purpose of great intensity, which produces a complex of energy in the human being. Thus, a “genuine brigand” who stands “with a rifle behind a stone by the roadside for eight hours without stirring” or “a monk…(who) is afraid of the devil (and) all night long he beats his head on the floor and prays” (Ouspensky, op. cit., p. 32) in fact crystallise within them a complex of energy, a psycho-electrical double, which would be able, as a dense complex, to resist death.
105同样的事情也会发生在具有强烈欲望、激情或意图的人身上；他们是用「建造巴别塔」的科学方式来达成不朽的。人不但能透过结晶化的欲望、激情和意图来驱动精微分身，还能赋予它发达的智识结构和自动式记忆，将物质次元的经验全部贮存起来。这类玄秘家的「自我」会跟他的分身结盟，因为后者拥有他一切的记忆和智识，因此可以重新化成肉身彻底略过炼狱以及净化、启蒙、合一的过程，也就是躲过了灵魂死后
中 520–521 · 英 360
And the same thing that happens with human beings who are possessed by strong desires, passions and intentions can be achieved methodically by making use of the scientific method of the “construction of the tower of Babel”. Then one could not only animate the double crystallised from a desire, a passion or a dominant intention, but also equip it with an intellectual apparatus of very developed functioning and a mechanical memory in which all the facts of experience on the physical plane are accumulated. The “self” of such an occultist would then be allied to this double, who is the bearer of his memory and intellect, and could incarnate himself anew—avoiding purgatory and the whole path of purification, illumination and union which is the lot of the human soul after death.
106必定会面临的挑战³。因此，建构「巴别塔」的理念和方式并不是单纯的幻想，而是另一种成为不朽的方式，也是《创世纪》里的蛇所说的：「你们不会因为吃了善恶树的果子而死。」善恶树的果子令人在「是」与「不是」之间挣扎而产生了内在的摩擦力，这种磨擦力又会形成带电的火，火的结晶化则会形成抵挡死亡的结晶体。这便是蛇的允诺或这套程式的含义。这套程式既是「建造巴别塔」的千年古法基础，也是唯物科学的核心秘密或玄机所
中 521 · 英 360
It is therefore not purely and simply a matter of an illusion in the case of the ideal and method of the “construction of the tower of Babel”. Rather, it is a matter of another kind of immortality, notably that which the serpent of Genesis had in mind when he said, “You will not die if you eat of the fruit of the Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil”. For the fruit of the Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil produces the inner friction in man of the struggle between “yes” and “no”, and this friction in its turn produces the electrical fire which effects the crystallisation whose product will resist death. This is the meaning of the promise—or rather the programme—of the serpent. This programme underlies the millennial-old method of the “construction of the tower of Babel”, and it constitutes the esoteric kernel or the hidden secret of materialistic science in general.
107在。我们虽然用葛吉夫（和邬斯宾斯基）的说法来解释「建造巴别塔」的概念和方式，但葛吉夫曾公开表示自己是纯正的唯物主义者，完全和玄秘事物无关，所以他的观点只能代表一般大众。他只不过清楚地阐述了是什么在激发和促使千千万万的科学家，无觉知或半带觉知地投入长寿的研究，亦即不仰赖上主或密契主义，只透过人为的修练方式来战胜死亡：这才是建造巴别塔真正的理由。
中 521 · 英 360
We have chosen Gurdjieff (and Ouspensky) to exemplify the ideal and the method of the “construction of the tower of Babel”, but Gurdjieff—being openly materialistic in the true sense of this word, and being deprived of all mystical sense—only spoke on behalf of the multitude. All he did was to give forth clearly what animates and impels—in an unconscious or semi-conscious way—millions and millions of scientists devoted to the cause of longevity, i.e. to the victory over death through human science, without God and without mysticism: the universal cause of the construction of the tower of Babel.
108葛吉夫只是一位唯物主义的科学代表；他知道科学想要的是什么，也知道自己想要的是什么。他是一个温厚的人，具有细腻的幽默感，也是一个好儿子、好朋友，而且十分富有常识——我所举出的只是眼前浮现的一些他所具备的特质。因此，我们不该将他视为「阴险的先知」或带有特殊使命的「撒旦使者」。不，他只不过是「世间智慧」的代表，他的体悟是奠基于经验的，因此不带有任何玄秘倾向。比起俄国著名生理学家巴夫洛夫（Pavlov）或任何唯物科学的代表人物，葛吉夫根本算不上是「撒旦拥护者」。
中 521–522 · 英 360
Gurdjieff is simply a representative of the cause of materialistic science; he knew what it wants in reality and he also knew what he wanted himself. He was, moreover, a good-natured man, endowed with an exquisite sense of humour, a good son, a good friend, and very intelligent with respect to common sense—to indicate only the qualities that he possessed which leap out at one. It would therefore be wrong to see in him a “prophet of darkness” or an instrument of a special “Satanic mission”. No, he was simply a good representative of the “wisdom of this world”, i.e. of good sense and empirical experience without any mystical inclination. Gurdjieff was not any more a “Satanist” than the celebrated Russian physiologist Pavlov or any other representative of materialistic science.
109然而无疑地，他所教导的「由下往上的结晶化修练」，无论在实修或理论上都不能和荣格的个体化之道、隐修之道或卡巴拉的教诲相提并论。隐修之道所传授的结晶化修炼是一套由上往下的运作方式；它的灵知是密契体验的结晶，神圣魔法是灵知的结晶，结晶化的神圣魔法则是其隐修哲学。因此，葛吉夫的唯物玄学就是以结晶化的唯物科学取代或废止了密契体验。
中 522 · 英 361
Assuredly, his practical and theoretical teaching of crystallisation from below above is not compatible either with Carl Jung’s process of individuation or with Christian Hermeticism or with the Cabbala. For Hermeticism also teaches a crystallisation but it is a crystallisation from above below, i.e. the crystallisation of which Hermeticism itself—in so far as it is philosophy and knowledge—is the product: crystallised mysticism being gnosis, crystallised gnosis being magic, and crystallised magic being this philosophy and knowledge which passes under the name of “Hermeticism”. Thus if one disregards the intermediary stages, one could say that Hermeticism is crystallised mysticism, whilst Gurdjieff’s materialistic occultism replaces—and abolishes—mysticism by crystallised materialistic science.
110回到我在这封信一开始提出的问题：创世纪的蛇难道是在说谎吗？现在我们可以回答：不，它不是在说谎。它是用另一种不朽，亦即由下往上的「巴别塔」结晶作用，去对抗上主的不朽。它提出了大胆可行的方式，目的是要制造一种由活人和鬼结合成的人类，其灵魂几乎可以不延迟地直接转世，也可以避免经历炼狱到天堂的净化过程。
中 522 · 英 361
In returning to the question posed at the beginning of this Letter: Did the serpent of Genesis simply lie?—we are now able to answer: no, he did not lie. He opposed to divine immortality another immortality: that of crystallisation from below above, or the “tower of Babel”. He advanced the bold programme—but real and realisable—aiming at a mankind which would be composed of the living and of ghosts, with the latter reincarnating almost without delay and avoiding the way which leads through purgatory to heaven.
111你现在应该明白教会为何反对轮回之说了，虽然大部分拥有属灵体验的信徒都知道——不可能不知道——轮回乃真实存在的现象。在这些反对意见的背后有一种深切的理由：以制造鬼身的方式直接轮回或避开净化（炼狱）、启蒙、与神合一的历程，是十分危险的修练方式，因为人活著的时候可能会经不起诱惑而为来世做准备，反倒不为炼狱和天堂做准备了。不去面对永恒的上主、选择死后立即转世，往往涉及结晶化作用和「电能分身」—鬼身的形成，也就是利用鬼身来逃避炼狱和上主的圣光，将其当成立即转世投生的媒介。事实上，人应当在活著的时候发展出彻底觉醒的意识，以便死后面对上主，而彻底觉醒就是一种炼狱经验；同时人也应该在活著的时候体验到当下即永恒的天堂；人不该在活著的时候为来世做准备，因为这么做会导致结晶化作用、产生出鬼身。对轮回一无所知或者完全否定轮回之说，远比将理想和愿景投射到来世，用蛇所建议的方式获得永生要强上一百倍。我再重申一次，基于上述的理由，教会打从一开始就反对轮回之说，甚至竭力不让这个概念在信徒的意识里扎根。
中 522–523 · 英 361
You see now, dear Unknown Friend, why the Church was hostile to the doctrine of reincarnation, although the fact of repeated incarnations was known—and could not remain unknown—to a large number of people faithful to the Church with authentic spiritual experience. The deeper reason is the danger of reincarnation by way of the ghost, where one avoids the path of purification (in purgatory), illumination and celestial union. For humanity could succumb to the temptation of preparing for a future terrestrial life, instead of preparing for purgatory and heaven, during earthly life. To prepare for a future terrestrial life, instead of preparing for the confrontation with Eternity, amounts to crystallisation in the sense of the formation of an electric double—the body of the ghost—which could, in its turn, serve as the bridge from one incarnation to another and be the means of evading purgatory and the confrontation with Eternity. One ought during earthly life to prepare for this meeting with a fully awakened consciousness, which is purgatory, and for the experience of the presence of the Eternal, which is heaven, and not to prepare for a future terrestrial life, which would amount to the crystallisation of the “body” of a ghost. It is worth a hundred times more to know nothing of the fact of reincarnation, and to deny the doctrine of reincarnation, than to turn thoughts and desires towards the future terrestrial life and thus to be tempted to resort to the means offered through the promise of immortality made by the serpent. This is why, I repeat, the Church was, from the beginning, hostile to the idea of reincarnation and did all that it could so that this idea would not take root in consciousness—and above all in the human will.
112我承认，我是因为某个非常重视道德的教派的反对而犹豫了一阵子，最后还是决定将轮回之说的危险性写出来，尤其是滥用它所导致的结果。亲爱的朋友，我相信你能理解，举凡认为轮回非密修者专有而是一种普世现象的人，身上都背负著一份责任；就是这份责任促使我决定将滥用轮回说的现象写出来。因此我恳求你，亲爱的朋友，请以善意和良知去检视十九世纪的法国玄学运动、二十世纪的神智学、人智学和玫瑰十字会，他们把轮回说当成显学来研究且当真地修练，到底是不是一种妥当合宜的做法。
中 523 · 英 361
I confess that it is only after hesitation, due to objections of a very serious moral order, that I have decided to write of the danger that the doctrine of reincarnation entails, and above all of that abuse that can be—and is, in fact—made of it. It is the faith that you, dear Unknown Friend, understand the weight of responsibility that weighs on each person who sees himself treating reincarnation not as belonging to the domain of esoteric (i.e. intimate) experience, but as an exoteric teaching to popularise—called to convince everyone—which has determined me to speak of the practical abuse of the fact of reincarnation. I implore you therefore, dear Unknown Friend, to have the good will to examine, in the light of moral conscience, the question whether the way of treating reincarnation in exoteric teaching that has been adopted and is practised in general both by representatives of the French occult movement of the nineteenth and twentieth centuries and by Theosophists, Anthroposophists, Rosicrucians, etc., is justified and desirable.
113我必须强调的是，上述的分析涉及的不只是逃避炼狱、不肯面对上主所引发的道德危机，同时也涉及到以蛇的不朽取代神的不朽。
中 523–524 · 英 362
I may add that in the last analysis it is a matter not only of the moral danger of evading purgatory and the experience of Eternity, but also of replacing one immortality by another, namely that of God by that of the serpent. For there are two deaths and two immortalities.
114在浪子的寓言故事里，那位父亲所谓的「死」「因为我这个儿子是死而复生、失而复得的」（《路加福音》第15章，第24节），指的就是和天父及天家疏远；肉体的死亡指的则是离开物质界、脱离世间引力的电场（「吊人」卡所涉及的奥义）。拒绝经历炼狱和天堂的人就是拒绝回到天父的家，也就是选择和祂疏远。所以从神的角度来看，全然结晶化即是彻底死亡，全然活著则是「如太阳般的放射」状态，或是结晶体的彻底消解。因此《圣经》所说的：「你们不可以吃善恶树的果子，否则你们一定死亡」，意思就是「一旦吃了善恶树的果子，你们就会离我远去」。蛇的承诺：「你们不会死」，意思则是「你们将脱离上主，独立生活，我有本事让你们在横向世界里继续存活下去，因为我会以智力和身心的电能取代神的智慧与爱」。因此蛇所谓的「生」便是神所谓的「死」，反之亦然。隐修之道基督隐修之道和更古早的隐修传统以及所有真实的密契体验、灵知及神圣魔法的基本理念一向主张：横向的生死是实存的，纵向的生死也是真实不虚的。对隐修之道而言，象征人性的十字架，「各各他的十字架」，代表的就是两种相反的生死方式。复活不但是纵向的生命力战胜了纵向的死亡，更是纵向的生命力战胜了横向的生命力。
图上显示的就是纵向战胜了横向、放射作用克服了固化作用。
中 524–525 · 英 362
The “death” of which the father speaks in the parable of the prodigal son—“my son was dead, and is alive again; he was lost, and is found” (Luke xv, 24)—is remoteness from the Father and his house, whilst the death of the physical body means remoteness from the physical plane and the electrical field of terrestrial gravitation (which was the matter in question in the twelfth Letter, concerning the Arcanum “The Hanged Man”). Now, the refusal to take the way of purgatory and heaven amounts to refusing to return to the house of the Father, i.e. the decision to remain remote from the Father. And it is precisely this which is death in a divine sense. Complete crystallisation is therefore complete death from the divine point of view, whilst complete life is the state of “radiating as the sun”, i.e. that of complete de-crystallisation. Thus the divine words, “You shall not eat from the Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil, for on the day when you eat of it you will die”, state simply that “the day when you eat from the Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil, you will remove yourself from Me”. And the promise of the serpent, “You will not die”, means to say, “You will live remote from God and it will be I who shall attend to the uninterrupted continuation of your life in the horizontal, for I shall make up for the lack of divine wisdom and love by replacing them with the intellect and with psycho-physical electricity, which will be the source of your life”. Therefore the serpent understands by “life” what God understands by “death”—and vice versa. Now, Hermeticism—Christian Hermeticism as well as ancient and pre-Christian Hermeticism—has always advanced the fundamental thesis of all true mysticism, true gnosis, and true sacred magic, that there is vertical Life and Death and that there is horizontal life and death. For Christian Hermeticism the Cross of humanity—the Cross of Calvary—is that of two opposed lives and deaths. Resurrection is not only the triumph of Life over Death, but is moreover the triumph of Life over life.
115因此当妇女们在清晨来到耶稣的墓前却找不到祂的尸体时，那两个人才会对她们说：「你们为什么在死人中找活人呢？」（《路加福音》第24章，第5节）。让我们不再从死人中寻找那位活著的祂，更重要的是不该在死亡的领域由电能构成的智力范畴内，或是在卡巴拉的骑龙恶魔萨麦尔(Samael)的领土里寻求不朽的生命力。
中 525 · 英 363
It is the victory of the vertical over the horizontal, of radiation over crystallisation. This is why when the women who went to the tomb at the break of day did not find the body of Lord Jesus, the two men who appeared to them in resplendent apparel said to them, “Why do you seek the living among the dead?” (Luke xxiv, 5). Therefore, let us no longer seek amongst the dead for he who is living, and above all let us not seek for immortal Life in the domain of death—in the domain of the intellect borne by electricity or, to employ an image borrowed from the Cabbala, in the domain of Samael mounted on the dragon.
116幽灵和鬼显然不是生命存活或不朽的能源。这种能源存在于别的地方。在哪里呢？……在人的核心深处以及和上主的气息、光辉及热力的关系里。人的不朽是源自于对不灭本质的体验。
中 525 · 英 363
It is not phantoms and ghosts which are the source of certainty of survival or immortality. The source of this certainty is found elsewhere. Where, then?…in the experience of the kernel of the human being and his relationship with the breath, light and warmth of God.
117一个人若是体验过自己最深的本质，已经成为真正的自己，浸润在神的气息之中，沐浴在神的光辉里，感受过神的热力，自然会明白且已然成为不朽本身。你或许可以很优雅地向这样的人说明，头脑与神经系统的作用造成了意识的活动，就像彩虹是阳光投射到云层上造成的色彩变化一样，可是他会很清楚你所说的这一切都不对，相反的讲法才是正确的。或许他无法提出有效的论证去反驳你的说法，但仍然会确信自己是对的，因为他有真实的体验做为依据。
中 525–526 · 英 363
Certainty of immortality issues from the participation experienced in that which is intrinsically indestructible and imperishable, and therefore immortal. He who has had experience of the kernel of his being, i.e. he who has once been truly himself, permeated by the divine breath, bathed in divine light and ardent with divine warmth, he knows what immortality is and that he is immortal. You will have beautifully explained to him the “epiphenomenal nature” of consciousness, i.e. that consciousness is only the functioning of the brain and nervous system and that it is like the rainbow—a play of colours resulting from the refraction and reflection of solar rays on the clouds—you will have beautifully said all this, and he will have not even a little grain of doubt that all this is false and the contrary is true. Perhaps he would not be able to find valid arguments for you against epiphenomenalism, but as for him, he has no need of them. For it is not to arguments that he owes his certainty, but rather to experience.
118假如有人向圣十字若望或圣大德兰提出一套现代科学都支持的理论，目的是要向他们证实灵魂只是有机体的化学或电能制造出来的幻象，那么请想像一下他们会有什么反应！他们有过多次的灵魂出体经验，每一次示现的都是肉体完全麻木的状态，但灵魂回来之后又充满了活力和光，其光源不仅超越化学和电能的范围，更超越了感官意像和智力活动的范畴！因此，他们还很可能把提出上述理论的人送去看心理医生或驱魔师！
中 526 · 英 363
Imagine the reaction of a St. John of the Cross or a St. Teresa of Avila to a discourse, armed with all the arguments of modern science, addressed to them so as to prove to them that the soul is only a mirage produced by chemical and electrical reactions of the organism! To prove this to them!—they who many times went out of the body, left in a state of complete insensibility, and returned full of life and light drawn not only from beyond all chemical and electrical reactions but also from beyond all sense imagery and all intellectual activity! I think that they would have sent the author of such remarks either to a psychiatrist or to an exorcist!
119人一旦亲身体验过自己与上主的关系，自然会对不朽产生确信。
中 526 · 英 363
Certainty in immortality can thus be absolute, i.e. in no way dependent on the validity or invalidity of arguments, nor on the good or bad attestation of outer facts. It is when man has had experience of the kernel of his own being and of his essential relationship with God.
120我当然知道人们为什么会从逻辑、哲学和心理学的角度批判勒内·笛卡尔（René Descartes）的「我思故我在」(“I think therefore I am")，我也毫不保留地接受科学审判对它做出的结论。但是让笛卡尔确实认清自己的先验本体或生命本质的，并不是科学审判施展的力道，而是意识的审判使然。在某一天，当笛卡尔以一贯令人钦羡的方式思考时，突然不再受制于逻辑推理，而真正觉知到那个正在思考的思想者！在那个当下，笛卡尔彻底体认到「我思」之中的「我在」并不是某种合乎逻辑的论点，而是「思想中的思想者」。
中 526 · 英 364
I certainly know the logical, philosophical and psychological criticism of the Cartesian argument, cogito ergo sum (“I think therefore I am”), and I accept it without reserve in foro scientiae, but it was not the force of this argument in the tribunal of science which gave René Descartes certainty of his transcendent Self, the kernel of his being, but rather the experience in the tribunal of consciousness (in foro conscientiae) where, whilst thinking in the admirable way which was characteristic of him, he went out of discursive thought and found himself suddenly as the thinker of thoughts! It was therefore not a logical argument but a real and intimate experience of thought—in the process of thinking—which gave Descartes complete certainty of the reality of the “I am” which manifests itself in the “I think”.
121德国哲学家康德（一个如孩子般的纯真灵魂，天生就非常诚实和勤勉）将笛卡尔的自发体验变成了内在求知的新方法，也就是所谓的「先验法」(the transcendental method)。
中 526 · 英 364
The German philosopher Immanuel Kant (a soul of childlike purity, endowed with a remarkable honesty and diligence) made Descartes’ spontaneous experience a new method of inner effort aspiring to knowledge, namely the transcendental method.
122此法乃是要让思想者超越原本浸淫于思维的固著模式，提升至更高的层次去「观察」或「研究」自己的思想。康德的「哥白尼式革命」（Copernican revolution)也是要思想者跳出自己的「天真想法」，在思考过程中跳脱自我迷失，然后专注在其上的一个点，用抽离、无情又清明的真心去检视它这便是康德的「先验批判」精神。《纯粹理性批判》(Critique ofPure Reason)和《对判断的批判》（Critique of Judgement）便是运用这种方式撰写的著作；这个方式修正了我们原先的认知模式，让我们清楚地洞悉自以为能对形上事物—神、灵魂的不朽性及道德自由—进行评断的傲慢态度。透过康德的《实践理性批判》（Critique of Practical Reason），我们进一步地超越了对思想者的检视，开始进入理性逻辑和感官觉知的范畴，于是听见了生命核心想要对自己说的话：
中 526–527 · 英 364
This method amounts to the effort to transcend the thinking in which the thinker is ordinarily immersed, by going out from it and elevating himself above it, in order to observe thought—or “to think about thought”—from a point of observation taken above discursive thought. Kant’s “Copernican discovery” consists above all in detaching the thinker from “naive thought”, i.e. from the state where the thinker is lost in the process of thought, being immersed in it, so as to occupy a point situated above thought, from where the thinker can examine what is thought in an entirely detached way and with implacable and incorruptible truthfulness—this is the “transcendental criticism” of Kant. His works Critique of Pure Reason and Critique of Judgement are the fruits of the application of this method in the sense of the revision of the totality of our knowledge, and in the sense of a clear investigation of the pretensions of the intellect and of the senses to being able to judge things belonging to the metaphysical domain—for example, God, the immortality of the soul, and moral freedom. With respect to his Critique of Practical Reason we find here, beyond the results of the critical look of the thinker turned towards the domain of discursive thought and sense perceptions in which he had been immersed before, in addition what the thinker, this kernel of the human being, has to say himself. This can be summarised as follows:
123假如我说神不存在，我不是自由或不朽的，那么我就必须改变自己的生命基础或消灭自己。我的生命结构足以证实朝著完美神性发展的潜能的确存在，同时也存在著道德的自主性以及灵魂的不朽性。
中 527 · 英 364
I would have to alter the foundation of my very being, or to annihilate myself, if I were to say that God does not exist, that I am not free, and that I am not immortal. The very structure of my being is such that it postulates categorically the existence of God or infinite perfectibility, freedom or morality as such, and the immortality of the soul or the possibility of infinite perfectioning.
124笛卡尔的「我思故我在」后来成了逻辑学家、哲学家及心理学家灭绝式批判的对象，康德在《实践理性批判》中提出的基本理论也遭到这些人的严厉批评。在此我必须指出，令康德确实明白神、自由及不朽的，并不是某种合乎逻辑的结论或合理的论述，而是他在修炼先验法时得到的内在体悟。后者显然被证实为真正的属灵体验，它引领著康德去经验自己的核心本质，从其中发展出三种层次的确信：对神的实相、道德的自主性以及对灵魂不朽本质的确信。
中 527–528 · 英 365
It goes without saying that just as René Descartes’ argument “I think therefore I am” became the object of the annihilating criticism of logicians, philosophers and psychologists, so is the fundamental argument advanced by Immanual Kant in his Critique of Practical Reason the subject of a no less severe criticism—and no less founded in foro scientiae—than in the case of Descartes’ argument. But here also it is necessary to say that it was not a logical conclusion or an argument of discursive thought which gave Kant certainty of God, freedom and immortality, but rather the real and intimate experience that he had when he practised his transcendental method. This latter evidently proved itself to be an authentic spiritual exercise, which led Kant to arrive at experience of the kernel of his being—just as Descartes arrived there—and from which he drew the threefold certainty: the reality of God, the reality of moral freedom and, lastly, the reality of the soul’s immortality.
125同样地，印度智慧瑜伽行者也秉持著批判精神去检视自己的身体，而真实地体悟到「此身非自性」，接著又以相同的态度去检视自己的心理活动欲望、感受、记忆、意像等而进一步体悟到「心理活动亦非自性」，最后他们检视了自己的思想，借由抽离的方式经验到思想者本身，正如同笛卡尔和康德从思想提升至思想者的高度，故而体悟了自性一样。他们借由这种方式，得到了对「我在」（笛卡尔）和「我是自由的、不朽的、神与我同在」（康德）的确信。
中 528 · 英 365
Similarly, in Hindu Jnana-yoga the yogi arrives at the transcendent Self by critically observing firstly his body and arriving at the experience, “This body is not the Self”, then critically examining his psychic life—desires, feelings, memory images, etc.—to arrive at the experience, “This psychic life is not the Self”, and, lastly, critically examining his thinking itself, from which he detaches himself, and experiences the thinker—just as Descartes and Kant arrived at the experience of the transcendent Self by rising from thought to the thinker. From this comes their certainty of “I am” (Descartes) and “I am free, I am immortal (and) I am in the presence of God” (Kant).
126但愿人们能停止针对笛卡尔和康德的批判，以理智克制自己的反应；但愿人们不再干预这两个灵魂对生命本质或超验自性的真实体悟。但愿人们对康德老态龙钟还「陷入年轻时的唯信主义」的批评，能够彻底平息下来。事实上，康德并没有背叛任何原则，也没有陷入任何主义，他的人生和努力都得到了丰硕成果。或许有人希望他一无所获，最终只是一名批判主义和怀疑论的大师？……难道他一生诚实勤勉的努力不足以带来任何体悟，对形上世界产生确信吗？人们不但不为他的确信感到欢喜，反而评断他的缺失，指控他不忠！上帝啊，这样的态度多么刻薄啊！
中 528 · 英 365
May the criticism of Descartes and Kant eventually become silent, restraining itself through understanding; may it no more interfere with that at which these two spirits arrived, namely the intimate experience of the kernel of their being, the transcendent Self. May it one day cease from being repeated to satiety that Kant “proved himself to be unfaithful to his own method and to have betrayed his own principles” and that, in growing old and becoming senile, he “sank into the fideism of his youth”. For the truth is that Kant betrayed nothing and did not sink into anything, but that he arrived at the ripe fruit of his life and work. Or would one wish that he had not arrived anywhere, and that he had finished his life as a master of criticism and doubt only?…that the honest and assiduous effort of his life had not brought any experience, and therefore any certainty, with regard to things belonging to the metaphysical sphere? Instead of rejoicing in and celebrating the fact of his certainty, one speaks of his lapse and one accuses him of unfaithfulness! Good Lord! How petty!
127亲爱的朋友，你现在应该明白了，西方世界的伟大思想家和印度修行者一样，都因为悟到生命的本质或自性，而对不朽产生确信。
中 528 · 英 365
You see therefore, dear Unknown Friend, that the great thinkers of the West—just as the Hindu yogis—have arrived at the experience of the kernel of the human being, the transcendent Self, the experience of which gave them certainty of immortality.
128由于基督隐修之道是密契体验、灵知和神圣魔法的整合，所以会提出三种超越上述「哲学先验法」的体验方式，帮助人们对不朽产生确信。
中 528–529 · 英 365
Christian Hermeticism, being a synthesis of mysticism, gnosis and sacred magic, offers humanity three methods of experience, beyond the “philosophical method” outlined above, for arriving at the certainty of immortality.
129这指的就是净化、启蒙及合一的传统隐修途径，亦即在生前就带著觉知去经验死后的三个阶段—从炼狱到天堂到与神合一。你不但可以从伟大的基督信仰密契者，例如托名亚略巴古的丢尼修、圣文德、圣大德兰与圣十字若望等人的身上认识这个途径；也可以在基督信仰出现之前的隐修教诲，例如赫密士的《神圣的牧羊人》（The Divine Pymander)之中去发现它，同时还能在异教徒与埃及人的神话以及其他的神话作品里见到它。净化、启蒙及合一的三阶段修炼使人认识死后的状态，并对不朽产生确信。就这一点，马克斯·雷维尔4（Jean Marques-Rivière）曾经说过：(以下是）埃及和希腊密教的主要教诲：认识人死后的状态，以便克服对死亡的畏怖，这是人类特有的恐惧。受启蒙者既然已经知道将要面临什么，还会害怕死亡吗？(Jean Marques-Rivière，Histoire des doctrines ésotériques, Paris, 1950, p. 90)
中 529 · 英 366
There is in the first place the traditional mystical way of purification, illumination and union, which is the voluntary and conscious experience of the three stages of the way of the human soul after death—through purgatory to heaven, and from heaven to God. You will find this not only with the great Christian mystics such as Dionysius the Areopagite, Bonaventura, Teresa of Avila, and John of the Cross…not only in the pre-Christian teachings of the Hermetic treatises attributed to Hermes Trismegistus, such as The Divine Pymander, but also in the great mysteries of pagans, Egyptians, and others, where the three stages of catharsis (purification), photismos (illumination) and henosis (union, or identification with the Divine) give consciousness of the post-mortem states and certainty of immortality. Jean Marques-Rivière says concerning this:
(This is) the essential teaching of Egyptian esotericism as well as that of the Greeks: the knowledge of states after death, in order to overcome fear of this death, psychological and human fear. The initiate had knowledge of what awaited him; what could he be afraid of? Jean Marques-Rivière, Histoire des doctrines ésotériques, Paris, 1950, p. 90)
130如果说专注力的修炼是一门「遗忘的艺术」，深层神交或冥想修炼是精通「沉睡的艺术」，那么默观实相的启蒙就是精通「死亡的艺术」。借由遗忘、沉睡和死亡的艺术，过去、现在及未来的人们进入了与神合一的密契体验，因而对不朽产生绝对的确信。无论过去、现在或未来，人们都是借由密契之道的净化、启蒙与合一，而达成了上述的目标。圣十字若望曾经说过，真实的信德会在净化过程中被揭露、实践和逐渐增长；望德既是启蒙的媒介也是结果；灵魂与神合一则是爱德带来的结果。
中 529–530 · 英 366
Just as the practice of concentration is the “art of forgetting” and the practice of profound communion, or meditation, is the mastering of the “art of sleeping”, so does the contemplation of authentic initiation signify the mastering of the “art of dying”. It is through mastering forgetting, sleep and death that one arrived in the past, that one arrives today, and that one will arrive in the future, at the mystical experience of the soul united with God, and therefore at the absolute certainty of immortality. And one arrived there, one arrives there, and one will arrive there through the three stages of the eternal mystical way: those of purification, illumination and union. St. John of the Cross shows that it is authentic faith which is revealed, acts, and increases in purification; that it is hope which is simultaneously both the agent and the fruit of illumination; and that, lastly, it is charity which achieves union of the soul with God (cf. John of the Cross, The Ascent of Mount Carmel II, v).
131这便是进入永恒的途径，任何人都无法发明或发现其他的道路。你当然可以将它分成三十三个甚至九十九个阶段，如果你愿意的话；你也可以用理性的衣裳或简约漂亮的象征符号为它重新著装；你更可以用各种术语，诸如梵文、卡巴拉文、希腊文、拉丁文等语言来呈现它，可是你永远得经历密契体验的这三个阶段：净化、启蒙及合一，因为除此之外没有其他途径了，过去、现在和未来皆然。隐修之道也是一样的，除了永恒的密契之道别无他法。换言之，人如果不净化自己的身心，便无法成为灵知者、神圣魔法师或隐修哲人，也无法从灵知、神圣魔法和隐修哲学中获得启示。
中 530 · 英 366
This is the eternal way, and no one can invent or find another. One can certainly divide it into thirty-three stages—or even into ninety-nine, if one wished; one can re-clothe it in intellectual vestments, or beautiful and simple symbolism; one can present it in diverse terminology—Sanscrit, Cabbalistic, Greek, Latin, etc. But one will always have to do with the sole way—and always the same way—of eternal mysticism: the way of purification, illumination and union. Because there is no other, and there has never been another, and there never will be any other.
132未领受启示的灵知者不可能是真正的灵知者；他只会成为「怪胎」。未领受启蒙的魔法师也只能算是巫师。未领受启蒙的哲人要不是彻底的怀疑论者，就是「智力游戏」的门外汉。因此，灵知者得到上界的启示、神圣魔法师得到法力、隐修哲人得到启发，原因都只有一个：灵魂或多或少直接接触过上主。无论你是以密契体验、灵知、神圣魔法或隐修哲学的任何一种方式修炼，永远是相同的道路。世上有许多小径，但大道只有一条。
中 530–531 · 英 366
Hermeticism, also, has no other way to offer than that of eternal mysticism—the gnostic, magical and philosophical methods being founded on it. In other words, one cannot dispense with purification in order to become a gnostic or a mage or even a philosopher (in the true and original sense of this word). Nor can one any more dispense with illumination in gnosis, in sacred magic and in philosophy (again in the true and original sense of this word). For a non-illumined gnostic would not be a gnostic, but rather an “oddball”; a non-illumined mage would be only a sorcerer; and a non-illumined philosopher would be either a complete sceptic or an amateur at “intellectual play”. And with respect to the primary source from whence the gnostic draws his revelation, the mage his power, and the philosopher his enlightenment—there is only one: the more or less immediate contact of the soul with God. It is therefore always the same way on which one advances, without regard to whether one employs the mystical, gnostic, magical or philosophical method.
133意思是不论你修什么，若想真正地进步和成长，都必须经过净化、启蒙及合一；无论你知道什么，或是有过什么灵性经验，都只能在净化、启蒙及合一中得到成长。我们往往会从果实来审视一棵树，因此我们也会从密契体验者、灵知者、神圣魔法师、隐修哲人的信望爱来审视他们，或是以净化、启蒙及合一过程中的进展来检验他们。
中 531 · 英 367
There are many tracks, but there is only one way. This means to say that whatever one does, one advances and grows only in the sense of purification, illumination and union; and that whatever one knows and whatever experience one has, the criterion of true progress is solely progress in purification, illumination and union. One judges a tree by its fruits; one judges the mystic, gnostic, mage and philosopher by their faith, hope and charity, i.e. by their progress in purification, illumination and union.
134一个人的心灵是否伟大、灵魂进展到什么程度，都只能借由信、望、爱来衡量。佛陀当然明白世界生病了，而且认为是无药可救的，所以才会教导人解脱之道。耶稣基督同样也看见世界病得快死了，却认定它是有救的，因而启动了疗愈的力量，并透过复活示现这种力量。这便是涅磐大师和复活及生命大师的信、望、爱的差异。前者对世人说：「世界已经无药可救；我要教导你们离苦，也就是停止轮回的方法。」后者则对世人说：「世界是有救的；这是我给你们的解药。」同一种病，两位医生的处方竟有天壤之别！
中 531 · 英 367
Spiritual greatness, the calibre of a soul, is measured only through faith, hope and charity (love). Buddha certainly saw that the world is sick—and, considering it incurable, he taught the means to leave it. Christ, also, saw that the world is sick unto death, but he considered it curable and set to work the force for healing the world—that which manifests itself through the Resurrection. Here is the difference between the faith, hope and love of the Master of Nirvana and that of the Master of the Resurrection and the Life. The former said to the world, “You are incurable; here is the means for putting an end to your suffering—to your life.” The latter said to the world, “You are curable; here is the remedy for saving your life.” Two doctors with the same diagnosis—but a world of difference in the treatment!
135认真的密修者和密契家都知道，根据传统的教诲，天使长米迦勒就是掌管天使军团的统领。但为什么是由祂掌管？因为祂的信、望、爱让天使们愿意将管理的职责交托给祂；在属灵世界里，「负责掌管」意味著比其他存有面临较少的质疑、失望和谴责。根据传统的教诲，天使长米迦勒代表的是太阳，天使长加百列（Archangel Galbriel）代表的是月亮，天使长拉斐尔（Archangel Raphael）代表的是水星，天使长亚纳尔（Archangel Anael）代表的是金星，天使长札阿瑞尔（Archangel Zachariel）代表的是木星，天使长欧立菲
中 531–532 · 英 367
Tradition teaches—and every serious esotericist and occultist knows that it is so—that the Archangel Michael is the archistrategist, i.e. that he directs the celestial army. Why is he in charge? Because his faith, hope and love are such that they have put him in charge. For “to be in charge” signifies in the spiritual world to be less subject than others to doubt, despair and condemning judgement.
136尔（Archangel Oriphiel）代表的是土星，天使长萨麦尔(Archangel Samael)代表的是火星。但是米迦勒为什么代表太阳呢？因为太阳象征的就是信、望、爱。它的光同时照耀著好人与坏人，而且从不离开自己的核心岗位。
中 532 · 英 367
Tradition teaches that the Archangel Michael represents the sun, and similarly the Archangel Gabriel—the moon, the Archangel Raphael—Mercury, the Archangel Anael—Venus, Zachariel—Jupiter, Oriphiel—Saturn, and Samael—Mars. Why the sun? Because the sun is the visible symbol, the image itself of faith, hope and love. It sheds light on the good and the wicked, without leaving or quitting its central post.
137是的，对我们而言上主之所以是神圣的，并不是因为祂超越了整个宇宙，或是能够预知、预测和预设「宇宙这台机器」在未来的运作方式，更不是因为祂身为宇宙引力身心灵的中心，而无法被取代。不，上主之所以神圣、使人愿意跪倒在祂面前，就是因为具足了信望爱。如同我们相信祂一样，祂也相信我们——这是以更神圣、伟大及崇高的信德做到的；祂对「宇宙」里的各种生命怀抱著无限希望，如同祂对这些存有抱持无限的爱。
中 532 · 英 367
Yes, the greatness of God himself, i.e. of what is divine for us in him, is not his power in the sense that he is stronger than the totality of forces in the universe, nor his foreknowledge in the sense that he foresees, as a perfect engineer, the future functioning of the forces of the “world machine”, pre-calculated and predetermined, nor even the fact that he is absolutely indispensable as the centre of all gravitation—spiritual, psychic and physical—of the universe. No, what is truly divine in God, i.e. what makes every knee bow before him, is his faith, his hope and his love. For just as we believe in God, so also does God believe in us—but with a divinely greater and more elevated faith; his hope with regard to this immense community of free beings that we call the “world” is infinite, just as his love for these beings is infinite.
138我们信仰上主并不是因为祂比我们有能力或懂得更多，而是因为祂比我们拥有更多的信、望、爱。我们的上主是无限崇高和宽宏的！人们会惧怕祂，多半是违背了祂的崇高和宽宏。
中 532 · 英 368
We do not worship God because he is able to do more than us, or because he knows more than us, but rather because he has more faith, more hope and more love than us. Our God is infinitely noble and generous, and not only all-powerful and all-informed! God is great through his faith, hope and love—and the fear of God is basically that of offending such nobleness and generosity!
139隐修之道是建立于永恒的密契主义；这指的是它的灵修基础和出发点。从哪里出发呢？……从灵知、神圣魔法或隐修哲学的领域出发。
中 532 · 英 368
Now, Christian Hermeticism is based on the way of eternal mysticism; with regard to its practice, this is its basis and its point of departure. Point of departure—where?…in the domain of gnosis and in that of sacred magic, just as in the domain of Hermetic philosophy.
140灵知—不消说，与灵知教派（gnostic sects）的教义或教条都无关本是密契体验对理解力和记忆力的贡献。灵知与纯粹的密契境界有所不同，后者指的是意志被彻底净化而与上主合一了；由于理解力和记忆力无法触及这种境界，因此被排除于外，而且一直留在它的门槛之外。这就是密契体验很难以言语描述的原因。另一方面，灵知的基础也是密契体验，但其中交织著理解力和记忆力；灵知会随著意志一同跨越密契的门槛，而且一路都是清醒的。象征系统的灵炼使它们有效地参与著意志的密契体验。灵知以见证者的身份投入于密契体验之中，一直静默地扮演著镜子的角色。有了它们的见证，密契体验才得以被言传出来。有能力言传密契体验的人，才称得上是灵知家。「上帝是爱；那有了爱在他生命里的人就是有上帝的生命，而上帝也在他的生命里。」（《约翰一书》第4章，第16节）此即对密契体验的描述。「上帝是圣三位一体：圣父、圣子、圣灵」此即对灵知的描述。「父亲和我原为一」（《约翰福音》第10章，第30节）——也是一种对密契体验的描述；「在我父亲家里有许多住的地方」（《约翰福音》第14章，第2节)—则是对灵知的描述。
中 532–533 · 英 368
Gnosis—which, it goes without saying, has nothing to do with any method borrowed from the teachings of gnostic sects or with their articles of faith—is the contribution of mystical experience to understanding and memory. It is distinguished from pure mysticism in that the latter amounts to the experience in which the will—purified and illumined—is in union with the Divine, whilst understanding and memory are excluded from it and remain outside of the threshold of mystical experience. And it is precisely the fact of the non-participation of understanding and memory in mystical experience which renders it inexpressible and incommunicable. Gnosis, on the other hand, is the same mystical experience with the participation of understanding and memory, which pass the threshold together with the will and remain in a state of wakefulness. It is schooling by means of symbolism which renders them capable of participating in the mystical experience of the will without lapsing. They participate only as witnesses, i.e. they maintain complete silence and only play the role of a mirror. But the result of their presence as witnesses to the mystical experience of the will is the ability to express and communicate this experience. This is because understanding and memory have received an impression of it. And this impression is what we understand here by “gnosis”. A mystic is a gnostic in so far as, and as much as, he can express and communicate to others his experiences. “God is love, and he who abides in love abides in God, and God abides in him” (I John iv, 16)—this is a mystical statement. “God is the Trinity: Father, Son and Holy Spirit”—this is a gnostic statement. Or, “I and the Father are one” (John x, 30)—mystical statement; “In my Father’s house are many rooms” (John xiv, 2)—gnostic statement.
141因此，灵知就是理解力和记忆力静默地参与著意志的密契体验。我之所以强调「静默地参与」，是因为若非如此，密契体验就成了理解力和想像力的产物。为了拥有真正的终极体悟，人必须谛听，为了谛听人必须保持静默。理解、记忆和想像的作用静止之后，人才有能力领受上界的启示。
中 533 · 英 368
Gnosis is therefore the fruit of the silent participation of understanding and memory in the mystical experience of the will. I say “silent participation” because otherwise, i.e. in the case of active participation, it would no longer be a matter of revelation, but rather of a statement produced by the understanding and imagination. For in order to apprehend one has to listen, and to listen one has to be silent. The faculty of understanding and that of memory / imagination must be silent if they want to apprehend, i.e. to receive a revelation from above.
142理解力和记忆力静默地投入合一经验就是灵知，神圣魔法则是灵魂的三种能力都与上主融合，因为只有当灵魂与神合一时，才能与邻人及大自然合一。如果这么做的目的是为了产生积极的行动，那么灵魂就会变成神圣魔法师。凡是受密契体验和灵知启发而有积极行动的人，都可以算是神圣魔
中 533 · 英 369
Just as gnosis is the fruit of the participation of understanding and memory in the mystical union of the will with God, so is sacred magic the fruit of the participation of the three forces of the soul in mystical union with God with regard to one’s neighbour and to Nature. When the soul, having had the experience of union with the Divine, turns towards its neighbour and towards Nature, not in order to contemplate but in order to act—then the soul becomes that of a mage. Every mystic is a mage in as much as and in so far as he acts, being inspired by his mystical experience. Sacred magic is the putting into action of what the mystic contemplates and what the gnostic apprehends through revelation.
143法师。隐修哲学则是从密契体验、灵知和神圣魔法整合出的结论，目的是要呼应世间经验以及相关的各种学问。因此，隐修之道可以使人对不朽产生「三重」（密契体验、灵知、神圣魔法的）确信。
中 534 · 英 369
Hermetic philosophy, lastly, draws its conclusions from mystical, gnostic and magical experiences and works with a view to setting these in accord with the experiences of terrestrial life and with the sciences which are occupied therewith. It is thus that Hermeticism can give trismegistic (“thrice greatest”) certainty of immortality, i.e. the threefold certainty of mystical, gnostic and magical experience.
144传统将这种确信称为「新耶路撒冷的降临」，这跟「建造巴别塔」的方式恰好相反。基督降生前后的隐修之道，显然都是以「新耶路撒冷的降临」做为灵修的主旨，目的是要促使「一个新天新地」（《启示录》第21章，第1节）实现在人间，那将会是一个被彻底疗愈的世界。「新耶路撒冷的降临」是灵魂最核心的经验，也是我们星球的历史和演化的结局—「……一切隐藏的事都会被揭发，秘密的事也会被泄漏」（《马太福音》第10章，第26节），所有发生于内在的主观经验终有一天会变成客观现实。历史进程的神奇法则就是，凡主观的终究会变成客观的，今日的渴望、想法和感受终将变成明日的历史事件。「他们播种的是风，收割的是暴风。」(《何西阿书》第8章，第7节)
中 534 · 英 369
As you see, this certainty is produced in three—or four—stages of descending revelatory movement, from above below. It is what tradition calls the “descent of the heavenly Jerusalem”, as opposed to the method outlined above of the “construction of the tower of Babel”. Christian (and pre-Christian) Hermeticism therefore belongs decidedly to the extended tradition which practises the method of the “descent of the heavenly Jerusalem”, which works in the history of mankind with a view to preparing the whole of mankind for the future spiritual event of the “descent of the heavenly Jerusalem” on a universal scale. For the “descent of the heavenly Jerusalem” is at the same time a practical method of spiritual schools. The totality of inner mystical, gnostic, and magical experiences of individuals, the gradual transformation of the whole of human civilisation into a “celestial city”, i.e. where the laws are those of heaven, and, lastly, the Reintegration of the whole of Nature, which is a work of cosmic significance, together comprise the realisation of a “new heaven and a new earth” (Revelation xxi, 1), where the world is healed. The “descent of the heavenly Jerusalem” therefore comprises the most intimate experiences of individual souls as well as the history and evolution of our planet—according to the law: “…nothing is covered that will not be revealed, or hidden that will not be known” (Matthew x, 26), for everything that takes place in subjective intimacy will one day become objective reality. It is the magical law of history that the subjective at some time becomes objective, that the aspirations, thoughts and feelings of today become the events of history tomorrow. “For they sow the wind, and they shall reap the whirlwind” (Hosea ix, 7).
145现在让我们回到第十三张大阿卡纳。我们会发现死神正在收割黑色土壤上的手、脚和头。他收割的并不是长出来的杂草或完整的尸体—画面上没有显现完整的尸体。
中 534 · 英 369
This leads me to return to the Card of the thirteenth Major Arcanum of the Tarot. Here we see that Death reaps hands, feet, and lastly heads, which appear above the level of the black soil. He does not reap growing grass or whole human figures—which, moreover, are not to be found here.
146死神是防守特定「次元」的监护者，他会切断任何侵入这个次元的精微肢体。他执行的是外科手术而不是刽子手的任务……那么他究竟是哪一种外科医生呢？
中 534 · 英 370
Death acts as the guardian of a determined level, and cuts off every member of the human body which appears above this level. It acts as a surgeon rather than as an exterminator…but what kind of surgeon is it?
147我们在前面提到过，「建造巴别塔」的方式是先产生电能并赋予它智识，然后再从物质次元提升至更高的次元；
中 534–535 · 英 370
We have spoken above of the method and ideal of the “construction of the tower of Babel”, i.e. the method and ideal where one makes electrical energy ascend, after having animated and intellectualised it, from the physical organism to higher planes—firstly to the vital or etheric plane, the plane where “grass grows”, according to the Card of the thirteenth Arcanum.
148首先它会升至乙太层，照卡上的画面来看，这是一个「杂草丛生」的世界。如果不按照规矩、不了解后果便擅自提升至乙太次元，亦即在玄学院里进行片面的自我提升修练，那么往往只能在其中形成带电的「手」、「脚」或「头」。这时，死神一定会确保这个次元不受物质界的「密探」侵入。身为名符其实的外科医生，他会切除出现于物质界和乙太层之间的局部精微体成功地切除入侵的部分。「生病」指的就是出现在不该出现的次元里，因此死神防守的是两界的入口处，任务类似于执行某种手术。
中 535 · 英 370
This ascent, provided that it is not effected methodically and in full knowledge of its action, i.e. within an occult school, in practice takes place partially—sometimes it is only the “electric hands” which succeed in ascending to the vital or etheric plane, sometimes it is the “feet”, and sometimes it is only the “head”. Now, Death on our Card sees to it that the vital world is not invaded by “emissaries” from the physical world. It cuts off, as a true surgeon, the electrical “members” of the physical body which appear above the level—which is the threshold of the two worlds—where the realm of the vital world begins. It therefore accomplishes amputations of sick members—“sick” in the sense that they have usurped a domain of existence which does not legitimately belong to them—before the sickness becomes irremediable. What Death does in this Card is therefore to act as a guardian of the threshold between the two worlds and, correspondingly, to apply a kind of surgery.
149那么死神是否代表世间的手术规则？他的任务难道只是杀死和摧毁吗？或者他藉著动手术背负著疗愈任务？
中 535 · 英 370
Now, in general, is not Death the principle of surgery in the world? Is it called to kill, to destroy, or does it not have a mission to heal through surgery?
150亲爱的朋友，我的答案是：死神代表的当然是世间的手术规则。他将无用的精微体部位切除甚至会切除整副精微体目的是要让人得到完整而真实的解脱。
中 535 · 英 370
The answer that I propose, dear Unknown Friend, is that Death is certainly the principle of surgery in the world. It effects the amputation of members that become unusable—even the totality of unusable members, i.e. the whole physical body—so as to free the whole human being.
151在医疗体系中，自然疗法是借由健康的生活规范和习惯，像是饮食、睡眠、呼吸、运动等等来帮助人恢复健康，顺势疗法是用以毒攻毒的方式来协助整个有机体战胜疾病，对抗疗法是采用对抗性的药物来压制疾病的症状，手术则是牺牲有机体的局部以保全性命。因此，整个疗愈「机制」如果以高低顺序来排列，应该是自然疗法、顺势疗法、对抗疗法以及动手术。
中 535 · 英 370
Just as there is natural medicine which re-establishes health through the rules and habits of a sound life—diet, sleep, breathing, exercises, etc.—and just as there is homeopathic medicine which heals by helping the whole organism to overcome the sickness, and just as there is allopathic medicine, which combats sickness through opposites, and just as, lastly, there is surgery which saves the life of the organism by sacrificing part of it, so there is in the world a healing “mechanism” analogously hierarchical to the hierarchical scale of natural, homeopathic, allopathic and surgical medicine.
152在「宇宙医院」里，死神的任务就是动手术；这是救命的最后措施。它上面还有三种维护及重拾健康的方式。它们分别是密契体验、灵知及神圣魔法。因此，我们可以将法国大革命的那句口号⁵改为：
中 535–536 · 英 370
Death corresponds to surgery in the “cosmic hospital”. It is the last expedient to save life. Beyond it there are three further principles for maintaining and re-establishing health in the world and in individual beings. These correspond to mysticism, gnosis and magic. Thus, in paraphrasing a slogan from the French revolution, one could say:
153密契体验、灵知、神圣魔法或者死亡。爱，或者死亡。
中 536 · 英 370
Mysticism, gnosis, magic—or death.

题　辞　一
那女人回答：
「园子里任何树的果子我们都可以吃；
只有园子中间那棵树的果子不可吃。
上帝禁止我们吃那棵树的果子，
甚至禁止我们摸它；
如果不听从，我们一定死亡。」
蛇回答：
「不见得吧！你们不会死。
上帝这样说，是因为他知道你们一吃了那果子，
眼就开了，
你们会像上帝一样能够辨别善恶。」——《〔原译：创世纪 · Genesis〕》第 3 章，第 2-5 节
中 494
340And the woman said to the serpent,
“We may eat of the fruit of the trees of the garden;
but God said, ‘You shall not eat of the fruit of the
tree which is in the midst of the garden,
neither shall you touch it, lest you die.’”
But the serpent said to the woman,
“You will not die.
For God knows that when you eat of it
your eyes will be opened,
and you will be as gods,
knowing good and evil.”
(Genesis iii, 2-5)
出处两句互相矛盾的话
本封信从一处对峙开篇：上帝说「你们吃了当天一定死亡」，蛇说「你们不会死」。汤贝格的问法很特别——他不问谁在撒谎，而问：会不会两句都是真的，只是各自属于不同的世界？

全信的结构就架在这个悬置上：先花二十多页把「遗忘—沉睡—死亡」与「忆起—苏醒—诞生」这两列现象讲透，再回到蛇，最后给出答案——蛇没有撒谎，它只是提供了另一种不朽。

请留意题词的排版：底本把《创世记》这段排成分行的诗体，中译保留了分行，这是对的。
题　辞　二
告诉枯骨要听上主的话。——《以西结书》第 37 章，第 4 节
中 494
O dry bones, hear the word of the LORD.

(Ezekiel xxvii, 4)
校记底本的章数错了，中译改对了
底本作 (Ezekiel xxvii, 4)——《以西结书》第 27 章第 4 节。但「枯骨啊，要听上主的话」出自第 37 章第 4 节（枯骨复生的异象），第 27 章讲的是推罗的哀歌，与枯骨无涉。

已核 Element 版与 Tarcher 版读法相同，均作 xxvii，故非排印偶误，而是随底本一路承袭下来的。

中译径改作「第 37 章」，是对的——但未加说明。本版从中译，并在此出校。

顺带一提：以西结的枯骨异象正是本封信的暗骨架——「这些骸骨能复活吗？」；牌面上那具握镰刀的骸骨，与题词是同一个意象。
1亲爱的不知名朋友：
中 495
Dear Unknown Friend,
2你现在是否因为上帝和蛇对于「堕落造成死亡」这件事的看法相左，感到有些困惑不解？上帝的说法是：「只有那棵能使人辨别善恶的树所结出的果子你们绝对不可吃；你们吃了，当天一定死亡。」但蛇却说：「你们不会死。」在这件事情上面，神和蛇的态度似乎都很绝对。
中 495
Have you ever been struck by the contrary statements concerning death made by God and the serpent in the narrative in Genesis on the Fall? Because God says there, “You shall not eat of the tree of knowledge of good and evil, for on the day when you eat from it you will die”, and the serpent says, “You will not die”. Here God is categorical; the serpent is just as much so.
术语「堕落」与死亡的两种读法
底本 the Fall，指《创世记》第三章的堕落。汤贝格全书把它当作一桩持续发生的事，而不只是一次远古事件。

此处要点在于：上帝与蛇给出的是两个互不相容的死亡定义。往下读会看到，上帝说的「死」是与神疏远（纵向的死），蛇说的「不死」是横向世界里的存续。整封信就是把这两个坐标轴拆开来看。
3难道蛇是在说谎？或者蛇的理念根本是错的？亦或是蛇所认知的真理只适用于它自己的世界，在上帝的国度里就变成是不正确的？换言之，是否存在着两种相左的死亡与不朽——一种是从上帝的视野、一种是从蛇的角度而论？难道蛇所理解的「死」便是神所理解的「生」，蛇所理解的「生」则是神所理解的「死」？
中 496
Did the serpent quite simply lie? Or is it a matter of a fundamental error on the part of the serpent? Or again, did he state a truth from the range of truths proper to the domain of the serpent which are untruths in the domain of truths for God? In other words, are there two immortalities and two different deaths—one from the point of view of God, the other from the point of view of the serpent? 342Thus, is it simply that the serpent understands by “death” what God understands by “life” and that he understands by “life” what God understands by “death”?
4亲爱的朋友，我请你开始着手探寻这个问题的解答，同时留意一下我的思考方向。眼前这张「死神」卡就有上述问题的答案。卡上呈现的是一个骷髅人，正在收割从黑色土壤里冒出来的一些东西——人头和手等等。
中 496
Now, I invite you dear Unknown Friend to set to work with a view to finding an answer to this question, whilst bringing to your attention fruits of the work that I have made towards the same end. For the answer to this question is the Arcanum of the thirteenth Card of the Tarot, “Death”, which represents a skeleton who reaps only what pushes up from the black soil and rises above it—hands, heads, etc.
5经验性的死亡通常指的是存在于物质世界的生命消失不见了。这是我们透过感官所体认到的外在事实。但这种「消失」不仅发生于五官感知到的外境，同时也发生于内在意识里；个中的意像和心象会随着生物的死亡一起消失不见。这便是我们所谓的「遗忘」。这种状况每个夜晚都会出现在我们的记忆、意志和认知领域，直到我们完全忘却自己为止；此即一般所谓的「睡眠」。
中 496
Our empirical experience of death is the disappearance from the physical plane of living beings. Such is the fact of our experience from without, that we have by means of our five senses. But the disappearance as such is not confined to the domain of outward experience of the senses. It is experienced also in the domain of inner experience, in that of consciousness. There the images and representations disappear just as living beings do so for the experience of the senses. This is what we call “forgetting”. And this forgetting extends each night to the totality of our memory, will and understanding—of a kind such that we forget ourselves entirely. This is what we call “sleep”.
6就我们整体的内在与外在经验来说，遗忘、沉睡和死亡其实是同一件事的三种示现方式——是那件「事」导致了一切的「消失」。有人说沉睡是死亡的弟兄。我必须再加上一句：遗忘也是沉睡的弟兄。
中 496
For our whole experience (outer and inner) forgetting, sleep and death are three manifestations of the same thing—namely the “thing” which effects disappearance. It is said that sleep is the younger brother of death. It is necessary to add: forgetting is the brother of sleep.
7因此，遗忘、沉睡和死亡乃同一本质或能量的三种示现方式，只是程度上有所不同，就是它导致了身、心、灵种种现象的消失。事实上，遗忘即是沉睡，沉睡即是死亡。或者可以说：遗忘和记忆的关系等于沉睡和觉知的关系，沉睡和觉知的关系则等于死亡和生命的关系。
中 496
Forgetting, sleep and death are three manifestations—differing in degree—of a sole principle or force which effects the disappearance of intellectual, psychic and physical phenomena. Forgetting is to sleep as sleep is to death. Or again: forgetting is to memory as sleep is to consciousness, and sleep is to consciousness as death is to life.
术语遗忘、沉睡、死亡——同一件事的三个刻度
本封信第一根支柱。汤贝格的说法是：这三者不是三件事，而是同一原理的三种程度——

• 遗忘：自性从星光体、以太体、肉体中撤出（心智层）
• 沉睡：自性与星光体从以太体、肉体中撤出
• 死亡：自性、星光体、以太体一并从肉体中撤出

所以他称之为「减法」（subtraction），与「加法」的化身过程相对。牌面上那把镰刀，割断的正是这三处连结。

请注意这不是比喻。汤贝格接下来会以此推出：凡能解释遗忘的，也就能解释死亡——于是全信的其余部分几乎都在谈记忆。
8人会遗忘、沉睡、死亡，也会忆起、苏醒、诞生。忆起和遗忘对应的是苏醒和沉睡，苏醒和沉睡对应的则是诞生和死亡。人沉睡时会忘记自己，醒来时会记起自己。死亡启动的是遗忘，诞生启动的是记忆。当自然界把我们完全遗忘时，我们便进入了死亡；当我们忘却自己时，则进入了沉睡；当我们对某件事丧失积极的兴趣时，等同于逐渐忘却了它。
中 497
One forgets, one goes to sleep, and one dies. One remembers, one awakes, and one is born. Remembering is to forgetting as awakening is to falling asleep, and awakening is to sleeping as birth is to death. One forgets oneself when one goes to sleep, and one remembers oneself when one awakes. It is also the mechanism of forgetting which is at work when one dies, and it is the mechanism of remembering which works at birth. When Nature forgets us, then we die; when we forget ourselves, then we fall asleep; and when we lose active interest in something, then we forget it.
9但是我们必须记住，遗忘、沉睡和死亡涉及的范畴，比心智的遗忘、生理的睡眠和临床的死亡要深广得多。除了心智的遗忘之外，灵魂和意志也会遗忘，如同它们都有记忆一样，这是一种超越头脑的记忆。譬如，我们可能在脑子里对昔日的朋友还保有清晰的记忆，但心理上却完全忘掉了这个人。虽然我们仍旧记得有这号人物，却不记得过去的情谊了。同样地，人有可能在头脑和心理层面都记得某个人，甚至还保有着鲜活的感受，但意志层面却不记得此人了。或许你对他还存有亲切的回忆，却不想再为他做任何事。
中 497
Nevertheless, it must not be forgotten that the respective domains of forgetting, sleep and death are more vast and more profound than intellectual forgetting, organic sleep, and clinical death. Apart from intellectual forgetting there is also forgetting in the domain of the soul (psychic forgetting) and a forgetting in the domain of the will, just as there is memory in the domain of the soul and memory in the domain of the will—beyond intellectual memory. Thus, for example, one can retain a clear and precise intellectual memory of a friend from the past but at the same time have completely forgotten him psychically. One 343recalls, but without the living friendship of former times. Similarly, one can remember a person intellectually and psychically, i.e. with vivid feeling, but at the same time have forgotten him in the domain of the will. One remembers him with tenderness perhaps, but one does nothing for him.
10除了生理的睡眠（躺在床上忘却自己的一切）之外，还有灵魂和意志的睡眠。我们醒着的十六至十八个小时当中，灵魂的某些层面其实是睡着的。人清醒时的意识，对许多事情是「不知不觉的」……
中 497
Beyond organic sleep, i.e when one is in bed and oblivious of everything including oneself, there is psychic sleep and a sleep of the will. During the sixteen or eighteen hours that we are in the waking state there are layers of our psychic being which are asleep. During the waking state one is “asleep” to many things—facts, people, ideas, God…
11佛陀之所以被视为——被尊崇为——对生、老、病、死彻底觉知的人，是因为未成道者多少都能意识到自己对这些事是缺乏觉知的——不是头脑缺乏认识，而是在心灵和意志上觉知不到自己正经历着生、老、病、死。人的头脑虽然知道有这些事情在发生，但实际上是缺乏体受的。人只有真正明白自己所知道的，而且深切地体受到自己所明白的，同时又能采取正确的行动，才算是真知或确知。
中 497
And if the Buddha is considered—and venerated—as “fully awake” to the facts of human life such as sickness, old age and death, it is because those who are not Buddhas know that they are asleep with regard to these facts—not intellectually, but psychically and in their will. They “know” it and they do not know it at the same time. For one knows truly when one understands what one knows, when one feels what one has understood, and when one put into practice what one has understood and felt.
背景佛陀：「全然觉醒」的那个人
底本 the Buddha is considered—and venerated—as “fully awake”。Buddha 一词的字面义正是「觉者」，汤贝格是照字面用的。

他的论证很细：常人对生老病死头脑知道，心与意志不知道；佛陀被尊为觉者，是因为他在心与意志的层面也醒着。

这条线索会在本封信末尾再出现一次——那时他会把佛陀与基督并置为「两位医生」，说他们对同一种病开出了截然不同的处方（见后文★注）。此处先埋下伏笔。
12除了临床的死亡之外，还有心灵和意志的死亡。我们在七十或八十几年的人生当中，内心有好几层东西已经逐渐死去。我们在灵性和道德上已经丧失了一些东西。失去信、望、爱，是任何辩解、训诫甚至鲜活的典范都无法弥补的。只有神圣魔法——恩宠——能够为死去的东西注入活力。耶稣基督之所以被尊崇为复活之主，是因为受死亡驱迫的人都知道，只有神圣魔法具有起死回生的功能，而复活的耶稣就是它的保证人。
中 498
Similarly, beyond clinical death there is a psychic death and a moral death. During our seventy or eighty years of life we bear within us layers of death in our psychic being. There are things which are missing from our psychic and moral being. The absence of faith, hope and love cannot be remedied either by arguments or by exhortations or even by a living example. An act of divine magic—or grace—is necessary to accomplish the infusion of life into that which is dead. And if Christ is worshipped as the Risen One, it is because those who bear death within them know that it is only divine magic which can raise what is dead within them and that the risen Christ is the guarantor of this.
13遗忘、沉睡和死亡，就如同忆起、苏醒和诞生一样，都有其专属的象征图解。黑色象征着遗忘，草丛象征着沉睡，拿着镰刀的骸骨则是死亡的象征……黑色既代表非自愿和自然的遗忘，也代表圣十字若望所说的自愿和超自然的遗忘——灵魂与上主在感官、认知和意志的三重黑夜里合为一体。草丛之所以象征沉睡，是因为我们熟睡时的状态就像植物一样。熟睡时，人体的有机活动，诸如呼吸、循环、消化及生长，会在「动物性」和「人性」缺席的情况下持续发挥作用。我们熟睡时就像「植物」人一样。而骸骨之所以成为死亡的象征，是因为一个活生生、有意识的人分解成了一堆矿物，也就是骸骨。
中 498
Forgetting, sleep and death—like remembering, waking and birth—have imaginary and symbolic expressions proper to them. Thus black is the image of forgetting, tufts of grass are the image of sleep, and a skeleton with a scythe is the image of death…Black is the symbol both of involuntary and natural forgetting and of that voluntary and supernatural forgetting of which St. John of the Cross speaks—this threefold night of the senses, the understanding and the will, in which the union of the soul with God is accomplished. Tufts of grass or leaves are the symbol of sleep, because deep sleep is the state where we live a vegetative life. Organic life—breathing, circulation, digestion and growth—continue during sleep without “animality” and “humanity” being present. We are “plants” when we are deep in sleep. And the skeleton is the symbol of death because it reduces the phenomenon of the conscious, mobile, living and material man to that which is mineral in him—the skeleton.
出处黑色、草丛、骸骨——三个象征
牌面的三样东西各有所指：黑色＝遗忘，草丛＝沉睡，握镰刀的骸骨＝死亡。

其中黑色一项，汤贝格特意分成两层：非自愿的自然遗忘，与圣十字若望所说的自愿而超自然的遗忘——即「灵魂的黑夜」，感官、认知、意志的三重黑夜。这是本封信第一次引到圣十字若望，后面还会正面引两段（见后）。

草丛象征沉睡的理由也讲得极实在：熟睡时呼吸、循环、消化、生长照常运作，「动物性」与「人性」缺席——人此时确实是植物。
14自然的遗忘使人退化成动物；自然的沉睡使人退化成植物；自然的死亡则使人退化成矿物。就是因为「死」可以分成遗忘、沉睡和死亡，所以画面上才会出现黑色的场域，上面有一撮撮的草丛和一个骷髅人。
中 499
Natural forgetting reduces man to animality; natural sleep reduces him to vegetality; and natural death reduces him to minerality. The whole problem of death, comprising three degrees—forgetting, sleep and death proper, or the Arcanum of death—must therefore be presented to us as the image of a black sphere, beneath which there are tufts of grass and above which there is a skeleton.
15第十三张大阿卡纳所攸关的，其实是「减法」的三种示现方式——遗忘、沉睡和死亡。除了上述的内容之外，画面上还出现了由人头、手、脚代表的第四种元素，稍后我们再深入地加以讨论。
中 499
And it is precisely the thirteenth Card of the Tarot which presents us this image. 344The context of the Card is that of the threefold manifestation of the principle of subtraction by way of forgetting, sleep and death. We have here the black soil, the blue and yellow tufts of grass, and also the skeleton mowing. The Card contains still a fourth element, represented on the Card by the human heads and hands, and one foot, to which we shall return later.
16因此这张卡的主题就是「减法」或死亡，它和「加法」或生命恰好相反。如果要理解遗忘的机制作用，就必须从星光体、〔原译：乙太 · etheric〕体和肉体中「减除」自性的作用；如果要进入沉睡状态，就必须从以太体和肉体中「减除」自性和星光体的作用；如果要变成一具尸体或进入死亡，就必须从肉体中「减除」自性、星光体和以太体的作用。这三重减法形成了「脱离肉身」的过程，对应的三重加法则形成了「化为肉身」的过程，因为化身就是自性先形成星光体，再形成以太体和肉体。
中 499
The thirteenth Arcanum of the Tarot is therefore that of the principle of subtraction or death, and is the opposite of the principle of addition or life. It is necessary to subtract the Self from the astral body, the etheric body and the physical body in order to understand the mechanism of forgetting; it is necessary to subtract the Self and the astral body, from the etheric body and the physical body in order to obtain the state of sleep; and it is necessary to subtract the Self, the astral body and the etheric body from the physical body in order to obtain the corpse, i.e. the fact of death. These three degrees of subtraction in their totality constitute the process of excarnation, just as the corresponding three degrees of addition constitute the totality of the process of incarnation. For incarnation is the addition of an astral body to the Self, the addition of an etheric body to the astral body and the Self, and lastly the addition of a physical body to the etheric body, the astral body and the Self.
17卡上的骸骨握着的镰刀，代表的就是减法或脱离肉身的作用力，亦即割断自性和星光体（遗忘）、星光体和以太体（睡眠）、以太体和肉体（死亡）之间的连结。
中 499
Now, the scythe which is held by the skeleton of the Card represents the work of subtraction. It is this which symbolises the force of excarnation, i.e. that which severs the ties between the Self and the astral body (forgetting), the ties between the astral body and the etheric body (sleep), and the ties between the etheric body and the physical body (death).
术语镰刀＝减法
底本 the scythe…represents the work of subtraction。

镰刀不是刑具而是算符。它割断的三处连结依次是：自性—星光体（遗忘）、星光体—以太体（睡眠）、以太体—肉体（死亡）。

紧接着汤贝格把整封信的主问题提出来：究竟是什么在维系这些连结？——「我们为何以及如何记得往世？为何以及如何在早上醒来？为何以及如何能活几十年？」三个问题其实是同一个问题的三个刻度。
18灵魂和肉体——应该说灵魂和精微体——之间到底有着什么样的连结，却被这把具有三重减法功能的镰刀割断了？究竟是什么连结着自性和星光体、星光体和以太体、以太体和肉体？换言之，我们为何以及如何记得往世的历史？我们为何以及如何到了早上就会醒来？我们为何以及如何能存活数十年？
中 499
What are the ties between the soul and the body—rather, the soul and the bodies—that the scythe of the threefold principle of subtraction severs? What is it that unites the Self to the astral body, the astral body to the vital or etheric body, and the vital body to to the physical body? In other words, how and why do we remember the past, how and why do we wake in the morning, and how and why do we live several decades?
19让我们先撇开与这些问题相关的庞大文献，试着直接去冥想它，亦即不借助外来资讯而是根据亲身体验获得确知。冥想是一种为了内在的确知而进行的深思，因此必须放下假装知道的一般知识，例如科学常识等等。我们在冥想时，必须本着良知问自己：「我确实知道了什么？」而不是：「大家的答案是什么？」
中 500
In the first place, let us disregard the enormous literature where these questions are dealt with and endeavour to undertake a meditative work, i.e. to think directly about the subject which occupies us, without the intermediary of what may be borrowed from sources other than our immediate experience and understanding. To meditate is to think with a view to attaining certainty in the inner forum of consciousness, renouncing all pretension of arriving at things of general validity (i.e. things which may be a contribution to science). In meditation—and these Letters are only meditations—it is a matter above all of the question, posed in all honesty to our own conscience and answered in all honesty by our own conscience: “What do I myself know?”, and not the question: “What is generally known?”
术语「冥想」在此的确切意思
底本这一段是全书方法论的一次自陈：冥想是为了内在的确知而作的深思，因此要先把「假装知道」的一般知识（科学常识等）放下。

要害在两个问句的对比——「我确实知道了什么？」而不是「大家的答案是什么？」。这与第一封信「不用力的专注」是同一路数：先腾出位置，再让东西进来。

下文那一整套关于记忆的分层，正是他不引权威、只靠自省作出来的示范。
20亲爱的朋友，我们在探讨灵魂和身体之间的连结时，应该暂时放下一般人对这个议题的认知和说法，试着去思考自己有能力得到的答案。
中 500
Let us disregard for the moment, dear Unknown Friend, what is generally known and said on the subject of the ties between the soul and the body, and let us 345endeavour to take account—just for ourselves—of what we know and are able to know about it.
21首先让我们思索一下「遗忘和忆起」这件事。从主观的角度来看，忆起算是一种唤回过去事件的魔法。它能够让已经过去的事情出现在当下。如同巫师或招魂预知术士可以唤回死者的灵魂使其显相，记忆同样也可以唤回过去的往事，令其出现在我们脑海里。当下生起的记忆就是主观魔法带来的结果，它帮助人从被遗忘的黑洞里唤出鲜活的过往意像。鲜活的过往意像……是一种灵魂的印记吗？还是象征符号、拷贝或幻影？答案为以上皆是。记忆可以说是一种印章，能够复制来自过去世的印象，同时也是一种象征符号，因为它会利用自我的想像力示现出意像范围之外的真相；它也是一种拷贝作用，目的在于复制过往的原始事件；甚至可以说它是一种幻影，因为是从深渊里冒出来的被遗忘的幽灵。
中 500
Firstly, let us consider the domain of forgetting and remembering—the memory. Memory is the magic, in the subjective domain, which effects the evocation of things from the past. It renders past things present. Just as a sorcerer or necromancer evokes the spirits of the dead by making them appear, so does memory evoke things of the past and make them appear to our inner mental vision. The present remembrance is the result of a magical operation in the subjective domain, where one has succeeded in evoking from the black void of forgetfulness a living image from the past. A living image from the past…imprint? symbol? copy? phantom? It is all of these at once. It is an imprint in so far as it reproduces an impression received in the past; it is a symbol in so far as it makes use of my imagination to represent a reality which goes beyond its imaginary representation; it is a copy in so far as it only aims at reproducing the original from the past; it is a phantom in so far as it is an apparition from the black abyss of forgetfulness and in so far as it recalls to life the past in making it present to my inner vision.
22但究竟是什么力量促成了主观且神奇的唤回作用？
中 500
What is the force at work in the subjective magical operation of remembering?
23人对记忆的体验可以分成四种：机械式或自动式记忆、逻辑式记忆、道德记忆，以及垂直或启示性记忆。
中 500
There are four types of memory that one experiences: mechanical or automatic memory, logical memory, moral memory and vertical or revelatory memory.
术语四种记忆
本封信最有用的一张表，汤贝格自己排的次第（由低到高）：

① 机械式／自动式记忆——按联想律自行浮现，人只需觉察。
② 逻辑式记忆——须主动推想才能补回（他举的例子：忘了三位一体中的毗湿奴，靠「造物—毁灭之间应当有个守护」推出来）。
③ 道德记忆——由爱启动。凡是深深感动过你的，不会忘。
④ 垂直／启示性记忆——不是从过去到现在的横向回忆，而是从高层我到低层我的纵向复制。

他随即给出一条实用的推论：老年失忆的真正原因，是道德记忆没有及时接上——「懂得在事物中看出道德意义的人，不会忘记任何事情」。

请留意这张表的排序原则：越自动，越不可解；越自由，越明白。下文他就是靠这条原则，只从最高一级去解释「唤回」的机制。
24机械式或自动式记忆几乎无须做出任何唤回的举动；它是自然发生的。它会按照联想法则——事物之间的相似性、亲和性及关联性——自动地出现，人除了觉察以外无须做任何事。当我接收到一种想法时，上述类型的记忆就会提供我许多过往的意像，以便从中做出选择。因此当我看见一支烟斗时，立即会生起各种意像，譬如：「我在一九ＸＸ年的某地见到的一位老船长」、「一本和印第安人的仪式有关的烟斗书」、「我的朋友Ｓ先生在上一次大战发生时，拿出自己栽培的烟草并点燃了它，由于当时没有人买得到烟草，因此在场的人都为之兴奋」等等。
中 501
Mechanical or automatic memory hardly makes any demand on the act of recall. The remembrance simply happens. It takes place in an automatic way according to the laws of association—i.e. resemblances, affinities and alliances between things—which effect recall without my taking any other part than that of an observer. This sort of memory supplies me, on the occasion of each impression that I receive, with a host of images of the past from which I can choose. Thus when I see a pipe, I can choose between images of the past which present themselves to my mind, e.g. “an old sea-dog whom I saw at B. in 19…”; “a book on Red Indians where it was a matter of the peace-pipe ritual”, “my friend S. who used to put everyone to flight when he lit his pipe of tobacco cultivated and prepared by himself at the time of the last war when there was no tobacco for sale”, etc.
25与自动式记忆相比，我们必须更主动唤起逻辑式记忆。为了唤回这种记忆，我们必须主动思考。假设我忘了印度教三位一体的其中之一，就必须自问：除了造物主和毁灭主、梵天和湿婆神之外，存在于祂们之间的第三种核心力量「应该」是什么？我必须专注在两者之间的空缺上，试着以推理方式去补足它。「啊，祂应该是守护神——毗湿奴！」我会如此告诉自己。逻辑式记忆的自动化作用，通常小于主动运思的功能。
中 501
In so far as logical memory is concerned, I am more active than in the case of automatic memory. Here I have to think in order to remember things. Thus, for example, if I want to remember the Hindu Trinity, amongst whom I have forgotten one of the three terms, I ask myself: If there is a Creator and a Destroyer, Brahma and Shiva, which third principle ought to be found between the Creator and the Destroyer? I concentrate on the empty place between the two and I make an effort to fill it logically. “Ah, it is the Conserver principle—this is Vishnu—of course!” I say to myself. In logical memory there is less automatism and more conscious effort.
26道德记忆则几乎不包含任何自动化作用，它是一种虽主观却真实存在的魔法。启动道德记忆的就是爱，爱唤回了过往的记忆。凡是能令你深深感动、难以忘怀或立即想起的，往往是跟敬仰、尊重、友谊、感恩、亲情及上千种与正向感受有关的回忆。人在一件事上付出的爱越多，越容易产生道德记忆。
中 501
With respect to moral memory, there is hardly any automatism. Here the remembrance is no longer something which happens but rather it is an authentic magical act, although subjective. It is love which is at work in moral memory when 346it recalls things from the past. Here it is admiration, respect, friendship, gratitude, affection and a thousand other things which have deeply moved you, which render things from the past unforgettable, i.e. evocable at each instant. The more one has loved, the more one remembers through moral memory.
27一般而言，年轻人拥有非常强的自动式记忆，但随着年龄的增长，逐渐需要逻辑式或理性记忆的辅助。届时就必须尽量运用理性思考了。无法发展理性思考能力的人，年老时会有失忆问题：自动式记忆会变得越来越靠不住，原本应该辅助它的逻辑式记忆也减退了。
中 501
As a general rule young people possess a very strong mechanical memory. It becomes feebler with age and it is logical or intellectual memory which comes to its assistance. This demands an effort to think, an intellectual effort. People who have failed to develop a taste for thinking and intellectual effort will have difficulties with their memory in mature age. Mechanical memory will fail them more and more, and logical memory, called to supplement it, will also be lacking.
28在年老时，道德记忆会取代自动式和逻辑式记忆的功能。爱可以滋养和维护道德记忆，以弥补逐渐消逝的上述两种记忆。老化之所以使人渐失记性，是因为道德记忆无法适时地取代逻辑式记忆的功能——更别提自动式记忆的功能了。懂得如何在事物中找到道德价值、看出背后道德意义的人，是不会忘记任何事情的；他们老来仍拥有正常甚至超凡的记性。
中 502
With respect to moral memory, it is above all in old age that it replaces more and more not only mechanical memory but also logical and intellectual memory. It is the heart then which supplies the energy which nourishes and maintains memory and which supplements the growing lapse of mechanical memory and intellectual memory. Senile lapse of memory is due to the fact that the person who suffers from it failed to replace in time the functions of intellectual memory—without mentioning mechanical memory—by those of moral memory. People who know how to and are able to give everything a moral worth and to see a moral sense in everything will not forget anything; they will have a normal, if not excellent, memory to a very advanced age.
29涵盖一切事物的道德记忆，对越不漠视道德的人来说越能生效，这是毫无例外的。由此可知，漠视或者对道德漠不关心，就是记忆衰退的主因。你越不漠视它，就越能忆起过往的经验，也越有能力学习新颖的事物。
中 502
Moral memory—which can comprehend everything without exception—is all the more effective the less one is morally indifferent. Indifference, a lack of moral interest, is the fundamental cause of the lapse of memory which often takes place in old age. The less one is indifferent, the more one remembers of the past and the more one is capable of learning new things.
30除了自动式、逻辑式及道德记忆之外，还有一种「垂直或启示性」记忆。它不是从过去到现在的横向记忆，而是垂直地忆起在现前、高层或更高层发生的事。它不是在身心层次上去连结当下和过去的「印记」，而是一种链接平常意识和高层意识的忆起作用。这也是一种「低层我」复制「高层我」的方式，亦即「高层我」将体悟烙印在「低层我」上面。它是「高层之眼」与「低层之眼」的炼环，能让人拥有真正的信仰和智慧，免于遭受怀疑论、唯物论及命定论的危害。这不但是对上主和一切属灵存有的坚信基础，也是对永恒与确信复活的根基。「黎明是缪斯的挚友」（Dawn is the friend of themuses）这句谚语和其他类似的座右铭，譬如「晨间人的嘴里有黄金」、「人在早晨比在夜晚更有智慧」，都和人在清晨从垂直记忆获益有关；清晨是人从「自然的喜悦」或睡眠层次醒来恢复意识的时段。
中 502
Beyond the three types of memory—mechanical, logical and moral—of which it is a matter here, there is still the kind of memory that we have designated as “vertical or revelatory memory”. It is not a memory of the past in the sense of the horizontal line: today, yesterday, the day before, etc., but rather in the sense of the vertical line: here, higher, still higher, etc. It is a “memory” which does not link the present to the past on the plane of physical, psychic and intellectual life, but which links the plane of ordinary consciousness to planes or states of consciousness higher than that of ordinary consciousness. It is the faculty of the “lower self” to reproduce the experience and knowledge of the “higher Self” or, if you like, the faculty of the “higher Self” to imprint its experience and knowledge upon the consciousness of the “lower self”. It is the link between the “higher eye” and the “lower eye”, which renders us authentically religious and wise, and immune to the assaults of scepticism, materialism and determinism. It is this also which is the source of certainty not only of God and the spiritual world with its hierarchical entities but also of the immortality of our being and reincarnation, wherever it is a matter of reincarnation. “Dawn is the friend of the muses” and similar 347popular proverbs—such as Die Morgenstunde hat Gold im Munde (“the morning hour has gold in its mouth”), or Utro vechera mudreye (“morning is wiser than the evening”), or even De morgenstond heeft goud in den mond (the Dutch version of Die Morgenstunde hat Gold im Munde)—relate to the benefits of vertical memory from which one benefits in the morning, after the return of consciousness from the plane of “natural ecstasy” or sleep.
31垂直记忆比三圣戒——顺服、神贫和守贞——更能直接有效地让下界的人听见、觉知和领收上界的讯息。基本上，垂直记忆就是人发展到更高层次的道德记忆。它和三圣戒的目标一样，都是针对德性的净化而非智力的发展。
中 503
Vertical memory is the more effective to the extent that the three sacred vows—obedience, poverty and chastity—render the lower man capable of listening to, perceiving and receiving things from above without distortion. Vertical memory is fundamentally only moral memory carried in its development to a still higher degree. This is why it is only moral purification, which the practice of the three sacred vows entails, that counts in the case of vertical memory. Intellectual interests, as such, do not count here.
背景三圣戒：顺服、神贫、守贞
底本 the three sacred vows——天主教修会的三愿：servitium（顺服）、paupertas（神贫）、castitas（守贞）。

汤贝格在全书中反复用这三愿作坐标，且从不把它们限在修院之内：他读作意志、占有、欲望三处的自我腾空。

此处的判断很重：垂直记忆比三圣戒更直接有效，因为三愿是净化的手段，垂直记忆是净化到位之后的结果。两者的目标相同——「德性的净化而非智力的发展」。
32以上是关于记忆的概略说明。现在我们要回头来探讨：究竟是什么力量促成了主观神奇的唤回作用？
中 503
This is an outlined inventory of the domain of memory. Let us now return to the question: What is the force at work in the subjective magical operation of recall?
33首先我们必须明白，我们前面所讨论的不同层次的记忆：「自动式记忆」、「逻辑式记忆」、「道德记忆」、「垂直记忆」，会带来不同程度的启悟。事实上，越是自动化的事物越背离直接的悟性。说真的，纯自动化的解释根本算不上是一种解释，因为它将思想和感受转入无意识领域，导致人无法深解事物。譬如，有人将微笑解释为「大脑的中枢神经将电流传至嘴巴及脸颊部位，使得嘴巴及脸颊的肌肉收缩」，但即便这是正确地描述了肌肉和神经的机制作用，仍旧没有解释「微笑」的真正起因；他忽略了「喜悦」才是让肌肉与神经系统产生微笑的主因。◆ 中译在此另起一段，英文原为一整段任何一种自动化的解释都不会是完整的解说，因为问题会从觉知之光转进无意识的暗处。所谓的「自动化」就是缺乏觉知的意思。因此，「自动化」的答案就是一切问题的坟墓。
中 503
It is necessary, firstly, to take account of the fact that in the scale that we have established: “mechanical memory”—“intellectual memory”—“moral memory”—“vertical memory” it is a matter of degrees of remoteness and proximity, concerning an immediate and lucid understanding of the evidence as to “how” and “why” memory functions through consciousness. In fact, the more something is mechanical, the more it is removed from immediate understanding through consciousness, and the more it is removed from the latter, the more it is mysterious and incomprehensible. A purely mechanical explanation is, truth to tell, not at all an explanation, because it removes the object to be explained from the domain where understanding takes place—by shifting it from the domain of comprehensibility, i.e. from “thinkability” and “feelability”—into the domain of the unconscious and therefore of incomprehensibility. He who wants to explain the phenomenon of smiling, for example, by the contraction of muscles in the region of the mouth and cheeks, and this latter through electrical impulses transmitted through the nerves from the centre called the “brain”, will in no way give an explanation of the phenomenon of “smiling”, even if he correctly describes the entire mechanical process in the muscles and nerves, for the simple reason that he disregards the joy of which the smiling is a manifestation and which set in motion both the muscles of the mouth and the electrical impulses of the nerves. For it is not nerves and muscles which are manifested in smiling, but rather joy.

And just as the description of the mechanical process of the muscles and nerves is in no way a reply to the question: What is smiling?, so is every mechanical explanation of whatever it may be in no way an explanation of the whole, but rather it reduces questions to silence by shifting the subject of the questions from the domain of comprehensibility to that of incomprehensibility—from the light of consciousness to the darkness of the unconscious. For what we call “mechanical” 348is in reality only the unconscious (or rather “that which is deprived of consciousness”) and is therefore inaccessible to consciousness, and therefore incomprehensible, unthinkable and unfeelable. “Mechanicalism” is therefore not at all the realm of answers, but rather the graveyard for real questions.
校记中译在此把两段重新切分
底本此处是两段：段一讲四级记忆越自动越难解；段二以「微笑」为例说明机械论解释的性质，结论是「所谓机械，其实只是无意识」。

中译把切点挪到了微笑例子之后：微笑并入上一段，剩下的结论自成一段。文意无损，但两段的重心变了——底本的第二段是以微笑起、以「机械＝无意识」收，一气呵成；中译拆开后，微笑成了上一段的注脚。

本版按中译分段排印，英文栏保留底本原分段，可对照。
34因此，当我们探讨不同层次的记忆时，要避免从「自动式记忆」的角度去理解唤回作用。我们必须从「道德记忆」和「垂直记忆」的角度去探索它。唯有进入整合的层次，才能为过往的探索带来最深的启悟。只有透过最大值，我们才能理解最小值。觉醒使我们认识了自动式或无意识的作用，因为后者就是最小值的意识。
中 503
This is why in the scale of memory under discussion we must not—and in fact cannot—seek to understand the working of recall in the domain where it is unknowable and incomprehensible, i.e. in that of “mechanical memory”. On the contrary, we must seek it at the other end of the scale—where it is least embedded in the darkness of “mechanicalism” and where it most reveals its essence in the light of consciousness, i.e. in the domain of “moral memory” and “vertical memory”. For it is the stage of complete development which illumines and explains the previous stages, and not vice versa. The minimum is only the reduced maximum and it is through the maximum that one understands the minimum, and not vice versa. It is consciousness which renders the mechanical and unconscious comprehensible, the latter being only consciousness reduced to a minimum, and not vice versa. It is man who is the key to the biological evolution of Nature and not the primitive organic cell.
35但究竟是什么力量促成了主观且神奇的唤回作用？
中 504
We must therefore seek for the key to the operation of recall in memory at the highest degree of memory’s development—“moral memory” and “vertical memory”. Therefore, what is the force at work in the subjective magical operation of remembering, such as it is revealed in “vertical memory” and in “moral memory”?
术语同一个问句，问了三遍
「究竟是什么力量促成了主观且神奇的唤回作用？」这句话在本封信里出现三次：提出（段 38）、绕行一圈后重提（段 48 之后）、确定了应从最高一级去找答案之后再提（此处）。

这是汤贝格惯用的推进方式：不急着答，先把提问的位置挪到能答得出的地方。第三次问完，答案立刻就来了——下一段就是拉撒路。
36以下是最高明的答案——其他的都是次级版本：
中 504
The following reveals it to its highest conceivable degree—other degrees being only its analogous, weakened manifestations:
