37耶稣一向爱马大和她的妹妹，也爱拉撒路……耶稣到了伯大尼，知道拉撒路已经在四天前埋葬了……耶稣哭了……心里非常激动。他来到坟墓前；那坟墓是一个洞穴，入口的地方有一块石头堵住。耶稣吩咐：「把石头挪开！」……于是他们把石头挪开……耶稣大声喊：「拉撒路，出来！」那死了的人就出来；他的手脚裹着布条，脸上也包着布。（《约翰福音》第 11 章，第 5-44 节）
中 504
Now Jesus loved Martha and her sister and Lazarus…when Jesus came, he found that Lazarus had already been in the tomb four days…Jesus wept…Then Jesus, deeply moved again, came to the tomb; it was a cave, and a stone lay upon it. Jesus said: Take away the stone… So they took away the stone…Jesus cried with a loud voice: Lazarus, come out. The dead man came out, his hands and feet bound with bandages, and his face wrapped with a cloth. (John xi, 5-44)
出处拉撒路复活（《约翰福音》11）
底本引的是 John xi, 5-44 的节略。汤贝格用它作「唤回」的最高范例，并拆成三步：

① 到来——耶稣从伯大尼走到坟前，即「找到隔开召唤者与被召唤者的那道门」；
② 挪开石头——石头即疑虑、疲惫、绝望，是回忆浮不上来的真正原因；
③ 大声呼喊——爱到痛处才发得出的那一声。

三步之前还有一句被他反复咬住的话：「耶稣一向爱马大和她的妹妹，也爱拉撒路」——唤回的动力是爱，不是技术。这正好接上前文「道德记忆由爱启动」。
38这才是最完整、最强烈以及最高尚的唤回力量。这是一种爱的体现，因为「耶稣一向爱马大和她的妹妹，也爱拉撒路」。
中 504
Here is the force of recall in its most complete, most strong and most elevated manifestation. It is love, for “Jesus loved Martha and her sister and Lazarus”.
39唤回生命有三个步骤：耶稣的到来、挪开石头、耶稣的召唤或大声呼喊。
中 504
The operation of recall to life—or resurrection—comprises three stages: that of coming, that of taking away the stone and that of recall, i.e. “crying with a loud voice”.
40首先是耶稣的到来。「到来」是为了寻找并找到隔绝召唤者与被召唤者的那道门。为了来到拉撒路的坟前，主耶稣从伯大尼走了「约两公里的路」才抵达耶路撒冷，这段路代表的是召唤的第一种作用：目的是为了抵达最接近召唤对象的地点。
中 504
Firstly, coming: “To come and to arrive” is the activity which seeks and finds the last door which separates the recaller from the recalled. The “about two miles” between Bethany and Jerusalem that the Master went in order to arrive at the tomb of Lazarus represent the first effort in the whole operation of recall: that which aims at arriving at the point of maximum approach to the subject of recall.
41接着是挪开石头：这个动作是为了消除所有的忧虑、疲惫和绝望，因为它们就像坟前的石头一样挡住了被召唤者的出口。人如果认为自己永远不可能忆起某件事，或不可能将它唤回到意识之光里，就没有能力运用垂直记忆和道德记忆。疑虑或缺乏信心就像坟前的石头一样，瘫痪了人的忆起能力。石头代表的是缺乏鲜活的感受和信心，更别提对前世或轮回的具体记忆了。你再怎么努力敲门，挡在前面的石头还是会阻碍记忆从深处浮现到觉知之光里。
中 505
Then, the taking away of the stone: this is the effort which vanquishes doubt, 349depression, fatigue and, lastly, despair, which bar the way to the recalled, like the stone placed before the tomb. By analogy, one is powerless to recall in the domain of vertical memory and moral memory things that one believes are lost for ever, or regarding which one believes that it is impossible to call them to the light of consciousness. This doubt and lack of faith paralyses the effort to recall and is like the stone placed before the tomb. This stone is often—if not always—the cause for many people of the absence of all living feeling and conviction, without speaking of precise and concrete remembrances, of former lives, i.e. of reincarnation. The remembrances have knocked at the door in vain, the stone placed before it not allowing them to come out from their depths and enter into the light of consciousness.
42最后要谈的是召唤作用。「大声呼喊」是唤回生命最终极、最崇高的举动；凭着这股爱的力量就能唤回生命和记忆。
中 505
Lastly, recall: “To cry with a loud voice” is the culminating—and the supreme—effort of the operation of recall through the force of love, whether to life, as was the case with Lazarus, or whether to memory, as is the case of recall with vertical and moral memory.
43在物质世界里，声音越是响亮越能被听见，也越能造成空气的振动。在属灵世界里也是一样的，因为只有如此才能表达背后的奋力和痛苦。奋力和痛苦的声音会被属灵存有听见。此即为何我们在念《玫瑰经》时必须复诵《圣母颂》一百五十遍、《主祷文》十五遍。如果念一声「耶稣！」就能被上界听到，那是因为声音中有强烈的痛苦。因此若想让《玫瑰经》的复诵被上界听到，必须使劲地诵念才行。这其实是施展神圣魔法的万能方式。我不把这一点说出来的话，就是对真相不尊重了。
中 505
A voice is louder, i.e. more audible, in the physical world, the more intense the vibrations are that it produces in the air. It is otherwise in the spiritual world. There a voice is more audible, i.e. “louder”, the more it expresses underlying effort and suffering. Work and suffering are the things which render our voices audible to the spiritual world and in the spiritual world. These are the factors which create “vibrations” sufficiently “loud” in the spiritual world in order to render our voices audible. This is why the rosary-prayer repeats the Ave Maria one hundred and fifty times and the Pater Noster fifteen times. For if it is suffering which renders audible the ejaculative prayer of a single word—“Jesus!”, for example—it is effort which renders the rosary-prayer audible. I would lack respect for the truth if I did not say that the effort of the rosary-prayer founded on suffering makes it a powerful means—sometimes almost all-powerful—in sacred magic.
背景《玫瑰经》为什么要念一百五十遍
底本此处讲得很直白：属灵世界里被听见的不是音量，而是声音里的奋力与痛苦。《玫瑰经》复诵一百五十遍《圣母颂》、十五遍《主祷文》，用意即在于此——不是数量的功德，而是把力气耗到位。

他随后加了一句在全书里少见的重话：「我不把这一点说出来的话，就是对真相不尊重了。」

这段与他对「不用力的专注」的推崇并不矛盾：不用力指的是不用头脑硬撑，此处的使劲指的是心的迫切。
44奋力而痛苦的强烈呐喊，是整个召唤过程中的关键之举：「耶稣哭了……耶稣心里又非常激动。他来到坟墓前……耶稣大声喊：『拉撒路，出来！』」爱让耶稣恸哭，也促成了死而复生的奇迹——它同时也是使人恢复记忆的动力。
中 505
Now, the “cry with a loud voice”, which is the decisive act in the whole operation of recall, must be strong both in effort and in suffering: “Jesus wept…Then Jesus, deeply moved again, came to the tomb…Jesus cried with a loud voice: Lazarus, come out.” It is love which wept and which strove to accomplish the miracle of recall from death to life—which is also the case in recall from forgetfulness to memory.
45所以唤回记忆是一种奇迹吗？
中 506
Is recall therefore a miracle?
46……是的，这的确是一种奇迹。但亲爱的朋友，请容许我对我所认为最崇高的奇迹做一点补充，因为这是每一位〔原译：隐修士 · Hermeticist〕和卡巴拉弟子都必须思考的事，那就是：奇迹之外，别无真正的自由。只有活在奇迹中、透过奇迹以及为奇迹而活，才是真正自由的人。
中 506
…yes, a miracle. But allow me, dear Unknown Friend, to say something concerning the miracle that I believe to be of the highest significance, of which every Christian Hermeticist and every Cabbalist should take account: it is that there is no freedom outside of the miraculous and that man is man only in so far as he lives from the miracle, through the miracle and for the miracle.
术语奇迹之外，别无自由
全信思想上的枢纽，也是汤贝格全书最锋利的一段。三句话：

• 奇迹之外没有真正的自由；
• 凡不是机械的（身、心、智三层），就是奇迹的；凡不是奇迹的，就只是机械的；
• 我们只能在「机械与奴役」和「奇迹与自由」之间选择，没有第三项。

底本这里用的一对词是 doing（作为）与 functioning（运作／自动化反应），中译作「行动」与「自动化反应」，译得准。

他随后给出「机械化的人」的程式：「以最少代价换取最多乐趣」——头脑排斥非制式的想法，心排斥不合制式幸福感的东西，身体服从制式本能。要跳出这个程式，只能靠一次不按程式的作为；而不按程式的作为，就是奇迹。

下一步他把这条原则用在山上宝训上：「为诅咒你的人祝福」之所以是奇迹，正因为它不是任何程式的输出。
47在身、心、灵三个层次上，凡是非自动化的事物，都是神奇的，凡是非神奇的事物都是自动化的。我们只能在自动化和被奴役以及奇迹和自由之间做选择。
中 506
All that is not mechanical—physical, psychic and intellectual—is miraculous, and all that is not miraculous is only mechanical—physical, psychic and intellectual. Freedom is a miracle and man is only free in so far as he is not a machine—physical, psychic and intellectual. We have no other choice than between the 350machine and slavery, on the one hand, and the miracle and freedom, on the other hand.
48「机械化的人」依循的法则是「以最少代价换取最多乐趣」，目的只是为了做出精准的反应。他在头脑层面排斥所有非制式的想法及概念，在心灵层面排斥与制式化的「幸福感」不一致的东西，在肉体上则服膺于制式化的「本能反应」所传达的指令。
中 506
The human machine functions according to the determined programme “maximum pleasure at minimum cost” in a way so as to lend itself to precise prediction in its reactions to given circumstances. In the intellectual domain it rejects every notion and every idea which does not harmonise with the intellectual system established in it; in the psychic domain it rejects all that does not harmonise with the complex of “happiness” established in it; and in the physical domain it automatically follows the orders transmitted by the complex “instinct” established in it.
49富人自称为反共者，穷人自称为亲共者，这些都是自动化反应。但富人如果愿意舍弃财产、顺服于神贫精神，如同圣安东尼和其他圣人，以及加尔默罗会、〔原译：方济会 · Franciscan〕和〔原译：道明会 · Dominican〕那些奉行神贫戒律的修士们，那就是一项奇迹了。〔原译：圣方济 · Francis of Assisi〕不但展现了疗愈麻风病患的奇迹，同时也展现了对「神贫圣母」的爱。耶稣基督让拉撒路复活之后，在各各他山被钉上十字架，饱受痛苦折磨，他却说：「父亲哪，赦免他们，因为他们不晓得自己在做什么。」（《路加福音》第 23 章，第 34 节）这难道不是他施展过最崇高的奇迹吗？
中 506
It is only the functioning of the human machine when a rich man declares himself anti-communist and a poor man declares himself pro-communist. But it is a miracle—that is to say an act of freedom—when a rich man abandons his possessions and embraces poverty, as did St. Anthony the Great and also many other saints, and also Carmelites, Franciscans, Dominicans, etc., who took the vow of poverty. The miracle of St. Francis is not only the healing of a leper but also the love of St. Francis for “Lady Poverty”. Did not the miracles of Jesus Christ, after the resurrection of Lazarus, culminate in the cross on Calvary where, in the full agony of torture, he said: “Father, forgive them, for they know not what they do” (Luke xxiii, 34)?
校记富人与穷人的例子
底本：富人自称反共、穷人自称亲共，都只是「人这台机器在运作」。中译译得完整，无删。

值得一提的是汤贝格举的反例：富人若真舍弃财产、顺服神贫——如圣安东尼，如加尔默罗会、方济各会、多明我会那些守贫的修士——那才是奇迹。他随即把方济各治愈麻风病人的奇迹与他对「神贫圣母」的爱并列，暗示两者是同一件事。

最后一记落在各各他：「父啊，赦免他们」——他称之为基督施展过的最崇高的奇迹，比使拉撒路复活更高。
50人所有的自主行动都是神奇的；按「本性」或自动化反应所展现的行为则是机械式的。山上宝训教导的就是「行动胜于自动化反应」。
中 507
All that one does is miraculous; all intellectual, psychic and physical functioning according to “nature”, i.e. according to human automatism, is mechanical. The Sermon on the Mount is the teaching of doing and of the triumph over functioning.
术语山上宝训＝「作为」压过「运作」的教导
底本原句：The Sermon on the Mount is the teaching of doing and of the triumph over functioning.

这一句把整部山上宝训读成了一份反自动化手册：爱仇敌、善待恨你的、为诅咒你的人祝福、为迫害你的人祷告——每一条都是对本能反应的直接违逆。

汤贝格接着说，人只有在这样的作为里才「示现富创造性的『道』」——所以下一段引的是《约翰福音》1:3。
51要爱你们的仇敌，善待恨恶你们的；为诅咒你们的人祝福……并且为迫害你们的人祷告。这样，你们才可以作天父的儿女。（《路加福音》第 6 章，第 27-28 节；《马太福音》第 5 章，第 44-45 节）
中 507
Love your enemies, do good to those who hate you, bless those who curse you…and pray for those who persecute you, so that you may be sons of your Father who is in heaven. (Luke vi, 27-28; Matthew v, 44-45)
52这段教诲不就是为了让人摆脱自动化反应，然后施展奇迹吗？
中 507
Is this not a teaching which aims at the liberation of the machine, i.e. of all functioning, and which is a school for the miraculous?
53为诅咒你的人祝福便是一种奇迹。它不再是自动化反应而是「自主的行动」。人只有透过奇迹，才可能展现真正的本质，也才可能示现富创造性的「道」。
中 507
Because to bless those who curse you is a miracle from the point of view of the “normal and natural” functioning of the reactions of the human machine. This does not just happen, it is done (it is created); and I repeat, one only does miracles, and all that is done is a miracle, and nothing is done without it being a miracle. All that which is not a miracle is not really done—it happens, as part of automatic functioning. It is only through the miracle that true being expresses itself, that the creative Word is revealed.
54因此，《约翰福音》的前几段话并不是在传播普世性的唯理主义，譬如斯多葛学派对「理性」的主张1。不，《约翰福音》宣告的是奇迹的普世性任务。其源头是创造性的道，而不是头脑的自动化反应：
中 507
It is wrong, therefore, to interpret the phrases at the beginning of the Gospel according to John as the teaching of a kind of cosmic rationalism, analogous to the Stoic doctrine of nous (“mind”). No, the phrases at the beginning of the Gospel according to John openly declare the cosmic role of the miracle, and that the world is due to a miracle, i.e. that it was made by the creative Word, and not that it 351was due to any functioning, to any automatic—even highly intellectual—process:
55上帝借着他创造万有；在整个创造中，没有一样不是借着他造的。（《约翰福音》第 1 章，第 3 节）
中 507
All things were made through the Word, and nothing that was made was made without him. (John i, 3)
56「上帝借着他创造万有」，其中包含了垂直记忆和道德记忆的唤回。由于唤回记忆是一种「行动」而非「自动化反应」，所以是一项奇迹。唤回逻辑式记忆，则是「行动」和「自动化反应」的混合体。唤回机械式记忆，则显然是一种「自动化反应」，因为其中的道德成分是最少的。如果唤回之举与拉撒路的复活类似，那么遗忘又是什么呢？
中 508
This is what the Evangelist says; and what we have said above concerning the miracle and the machine—i.e. concerning “doing” and “functioning”—is only the microcosmic analogy to the statement of macrocosmic significance from the Gospel according to John.

Now, “all things made through the Word” includes also recall in vertical and moral memory. The act of recall belongs to the sphere of “doing” and therefore to that of the miraculous, and not to the sphere of “functioning”. Recall in “logical memory” is a mixture of doing and functioning. Lastly, recall in mechanical memory is only functioning, i.e. the act of moral recall reduced to a minimum.

If recall is an act analogous to the resurrection of Lazarus, then what is forgetting?
校记中译在此漏译了一整段
底本此处有三段，中译只有一段。被整段删去的是（英文栏第一段）：

「这是福音书作者所说的；而我们上文关于奇迹与机器——即关于「作为」与「运作」——所说的一切，只不过是这句具有宏观意义的《约翰福音》陈述在微观层面上的类比。」

这一句的分量不在辞藻，而在方法：它是全书「上界如是，下界亦然」那条公式在本封信里的唯一一次明写——把个人心里的「作为／运作」之分，接到《约翰福音》「万有借道而造」的宇宙论上。删掉之后，中译从「万有借他造」直接跳到「其中包含了垂直记忆和道德记忆的唤回」，读者会觉得这个跳转来得突兀——因为那道桥被拆了。

另：底本随后的两段（「万有借道而造」的推论，与「那么遗忘又是什么呢？」）在中译里并成了一段。本版按中译分段排印，英文栏保留底本三段原貌。
57遗忘和唤回一样可以分为不同的层次，它也会自动出现或半自动地出现，或是有意识地自由出现。在机械式记忆里，事情会自动地被遗忘。至于逻辑式记忆，如果不常将它唤回到觉知中，也会逐渐远离和消逝。在垂直和道德记忆里，任何事情都不会被遗忘；如果被遗忘，也是意志基于道德做出的选择。
中 508
Forgetting outlines a scale analogous to that of recall. It can take place automatically, semi-automatically, and in a free and conscious way, according to the category of memory in which it is placed. In mechanical memory one forgets automatically; things are just forgotten. In logical memory things become distant and are effaced little by little if one does not recall them from time to time to the field of conscious attention. In moral memory and in vertical memory nothing is forgotten; forgetting here is a moral act of will.
术语遗忘也是有层次的
对称的一步：既然唤回分四级，遗忘也分四级。

• 机械式记忆里，事情自动被忘；
• 逻辑式记忆里，不常唤回就逐渐消退；
• 垂直与道德记忆里，什么都不会被忘——如果忘了，那是意志出于道德作出的选择。

最后一句是要点：最高层次的遗忘是自由的作为，与最高层次的唤回一样。专注即是有意识地忘掉不相干的事——念《主祷文》时连别的祷文都记不起来。
58现在让我们以相同的方式来探索遗忘，也就是从「遗忘是一种有觉知和可理解的行为」开始谈起。
中 508
Let us follow the same procedure as is the case in recall, i.e. let us begin at the end of the scale where forgetting is an act of consciousness and where it is understandable—where it takes place in the light of consciousness.
59每个人都知道有觉知的行为是需要集中注意力的，集中注意力又意味着带着觉知忘掉不相干的事物。当我们诵念《主祷文》时，不但会暂时忘掉每日的琐事，甚至连其他的祷告文也都记不得了。
中 508
Now, there is no one who does not know through experience that all conscious effort entails concentration or contemplation, and that concentration and contemplation signify conscious and willed forgetting of many things which do not relate to the subject of concentration or contemplation. One knows that when one prays the Pater Noster one forgets not only one’s daily affairs but also all other prayers for the time that one prays the Pater Noster.
60同样地，如果全神贯注于属灵世界和上主的价值，我们也会忘掉现象界的价值。简单地说，灵魂与神结合的三个程序：净化、启蒙、合一，就是灵魂越来越专注于神。有关灵魂与神合一的体验，圣十字若望曾经说过：
中 508
It is the same with spiritual and divine values, and those of the phenomenal world. The three stages on the way towards the soul’s union with God—those of purification, illumination and union—are simply the history of a single growing effort of concentration of the entire soul upon God. St. John of the Cross says of the effect of the experience of actual union of the soul’s powers with God:
61……灵魂在内坛的酒窖里，喝下了具有最高智慧的上主所提供的神秘醇酒后，便忘却了世上的一切。以往的知识、所有的学问和他今日获得的智慧相比，全都显得肤浅又无知。（St. John of the Cross, A Spiritual Canticle xxvi, 7, 10; trsl. D. Lewis, London, 1909, pp. 204-205）
中 508
…all the powers of the soul together, because of the union in the inner cellar, drink of the Beloved…This draught of God’s most deep wisdom makes the soul forget all the things of this world, and consider all its previous knowledge, and the knowledge of the whole world besides, as pure ignorance in comparison with this knowledge. (St. John of the Cross, A Spiritual Canticle xxvi, 7, 10; trsl. D. Lewis, London, 1909, pp. 204-205)
出处圣十字若望：两段引文的出处
底本注明得很细，中译原样保留了西文书名与页码（此处译者做得好）：

• 《灵歌》（A Spiritual Canticle xxvi, 7, 10；D. Lewis 英译，伦敦，1909，页 204–205）——「灵魂在内坛的酒窖里……便忘却了世上的一切」。
• 《攀登加尔默罗山》（The Ascent of Mount Carmel III, i, 5；D. Lewis 英译，伦敦，1906，页 244–245）——「记忆越是与神合一，就越会丧失各式各样的知识……但记忆却达到了完美的程度」。

圣十字若望（San Juan de la Cruz, 1542–1591），西班牙加尔默罗会士、诗人，与阿维拉的德兰共同发起赤足加尔默罗会的改革，1926 年被立为教会圣师。

第二段引文对本封信至关紧要：它说合一之后记忆并未消失，反而更完美——尤其是与道德相关的记忆。汤贝格下文讲圣人与守护天使「习惯于合一之后转身面向地球」，全部依据这一句。
62他还说过：
中 509
352And again:
63……记忆越是与神合一，就越会丧失各式各样的知识，最后这些知识会完全消失，但记忆却达到了完美的程度。当合一境界开始出现时，记忆会突然忘掉各种事情；这些事情的形式和相关的认知会逐渐从记忆中消失……记忆完全被神所吸引。但习惯合一境界的灵魂并不会忘记与道德或伦理相关的事物，反而能更完美地做出恰当和必要的行动，仿佛神用一种特殊方式赐给了灵魂的记忆真正需要的认知和觉知。（St. John of the Cross, The Ascent of Mount Carmel III, i, 5; trsl. D. Lewis, London, 1906, pp. 244-245）
中 509
…the more the memory is united to God the more it loses all distinct knowledge, and at last all such fades utterly away, when the state of perfection is reached. In the beginning, when this is going on, great forgetfulness ensues, for these forms and knowledge fall into oblivion…the memory is lost in God. But he who has attained to the habit of union does not forget, in this way, that which relates to moral and natural reason; he performs in much greater perfection all necessary and befitting actions, though by the ministry of forms and knowledge, in the memory, supplied in a special manner by God. (St. John of the Cross, The Ascent of Mount Carmel III, i, 5; trsl. D. Lewis, London, 1906, pp. 244-245)
64我想再加上一句，胜王瑜伽、奉爱瑜伽及智慧瑜伽的导师，也都是在教人如何忘却现象界的一切，进入彻底的禅定境界。我们也可以从神秘的卡巴拉和伊斯兰的苏菲教派中发现有关遗忘的教诲。
中 509
I may add that the masters of Raja-yoga, Bhakti-yoga and Jnana-yoga teach the practice of complete forgetting of the phenomenal world with a view to attaining perfect contemplation. The teaching of forgetting is found also in the mystical Cabbala and in Mohammedan mysticism, e.g. that of Sufism.
背景三种瑜伽与苏菲
底本 Raja-yoga, Bhakti-yoga and Jnana-yoga——王瑜伽（意志之路）、奉爱瑜伽（情感之路）、智瑜伽（认识之路），是印度传统对三种气质的三条路。中译作「胜王瑜伽、奉爱瑜伽及智慧瑜伽」，可。

汤贝格照例把它们与卡巴拉、苏菲并列，理由不是折衷主义，而是他惯用的那条论证：互不相干的传统给出同一条教诲，这本身就是客观性的证据。

此处三家共同教的是「遗忘的艺术」——进入禅定必先忘却现象界。
65遗忘是从一种意识状态到另一种意识状态的转换方式。人即便是进入睡眠或是「自然的狂喜」状态，也必须忘记白天的世界，才能进入夜晚的世界。我们为了入睡，必须懂得如何忘记。失眠就是无法忘怀所导致的。
中 509
Now, forgetting is the means of transition from one state of consciousness to another. Even in the case of sleep, which can be considered as a “natural ecstasy”, one has to forget the world of the day in order to be able to pass into the world of the night. In order to fall asleep one has to be able to forget. Insomnia is due to the inability to forget.
66那么苏醒呢？苏醒是再度记起白天的世界，忘却夜晚的世界。人如果无法忘记夜晚的世界，就不算是完全醒来——这种情况经常发生。这么一来夜晚和白天势必混淆在一起，日间执行的工作就会出现障碍——因为夜晚的记忆干扰了专注力。
中 510
And awakening? Awakening is simultaneously an act of recall of the world of the day and an act of forgetting the nocturnal world. Awakening would be incomplete—which it, moreover, often is—if one did not forget the experiences of the nocturnal world. The night would then be mingled with the day and human consciousness would be hindered in its capacity with respect to the tasks and duties of the day—its concentration being hampered by the haunting memory of nocturnal remembrances.
67那么出生和死亡呢？
中 510
And birth and death?
68如果说灵魂与神合一是对现象界的遗忘以及对神的忆起，那么死亡就是忆起了上界、忘却了下界。灵魂与神达成合一的三个程序，净化、启蒙、合一，在死亡后会再度出现：首先是进入炼狱接受净化（〔Catharsis〕洗涤心灵），接着是进入天国接受启蒙；天国指的是灵魂与神合一的状态，类似于密修者活着时的密契体验；这种合一状态会变成惯性，也就是所谓的至福感，然后灵魂就会忆起地球和曾经面临的试炼。这种记忆会使人的一切行为变得「更完善」（圣十字若望曾说过，这是因为灵魂习惯与神合一而恢复了记忆的功能），我在此应该解释一下，所谓「人的一切行为」指的是在世间的作为。
中 510
If the soul’s mystical union with God is forgetting of the phenomenal world and recall of God, death is simultaneously the call from above and forgetting below. The three stages of the way leading to the soul’s union with God—purification, illumination and union—are repeated after death: purgatory is purification (catharsis), which precedes illumination or heaven, and heaven is the state of the soul when it arrives at union with God, analogous to that experienced by mystics during their terrestrial life. This union, there as here, becomes habitual—which is a sovereign good for the soul—and then it remembers anew the earth and its trials. Memory then manifests a “greater perfection” (says St. John of the Cross concerning the resuscitated functions of memory in the case of the soul which has the habit of union) in all its action—let us add, actions directed towards the earth.
术语净化、启蒙、合一——生前与死后的同一条路
底本 purification, illumination and union，是基督教密契传统自亚略巴古的狄奥尼修斯以来的三阶次第。

汤贝格在此把它与死后的三段路对齐：
• 炼狱＝净化（他特意补注希腊文 catharsis）
• 天国＝启蒙
• 与神合一＝合一，合一成为「惯性」即至福

然后是关键的一转：合一成为惯性之后，灵魂反而转过身来忆起地球。圣人之所以能为世界做工，守护天使之所以能不被对神的默观夺去注意力，都因为他们「已经习惯」了合一。

这一句解掉了一个常见的误会：与神合一不是终点上的静止，而是可以背对着神、面向世界工作的状态。
69这便是圣人为神做工的动力来源。圣人是「习惯」与神合一的灵魂，因此拥有圣十字若望所说的更高层次的属灵记忆。他们不追求与神合一，因为他们已经与神合为一体了。他们在世间的作为——朝向世界而非神——都是奉主之名在运作的。
中 510
353This is the motivation for the blessed work of saints. Saints are souls who possess the “habit of union” and are therefore in possession of the higher spiritualised memory spoken of by St. John of the Cross. They do not seek union with God; they are united with God. This is why they act—their faces being turned towards the earth and not towards God—in the name of God on earth. They act, being united with God, in the guise of organs of his will.
70天国的属灵存有——天使——也是一样的。守护天使如果一直朝向神，就会着迷于对神的默观而无法保护人类。幸亏祂们已经习惯与神合一，意志已经与神的旨意结合，所以才能履行守护人类的使命。祂们和圣人一样总是面对着地球。这便是祂们的奉献精神背后的动力。
中 510
It is the same with the celestial hierarchies—with Angels, for example. Guardian Angels could never be guardians of human beings if their looks were turned towards God, if they were absorbed in contemplation of God. It is thanks to their habitual union with God, i.e. thanks to the accomplished fact of the union of their will with the divine will, that they are able to fulfill their task as guardians of men. They know the divine will in a “blind” way—through the dim intuition of their own will, i.e. through perfect faith—whilst what they see is the earth and human life on earth. Their faces, as are those of the saints, are turned towards the earth. This is the motivation for devotion to the guardian Angels.
71至于诞生这件事，则可能是源自「神圣」的力量，或「自然」的作用力，亦即顺服于神或「地球的呼唤」所带来的结果。前者意味着臣服于垂直或道德记忆，类似于拉撒路复活的奇迹；后者则是在半自愿、半被迫的情况下，受世间引力吸引而诞生出来，接着便逐渐忘却了上界的经验。
中 511
With respect to birth, it can, also, be either “holy” or “natural”, i.e. it can either be an act of obedience to divine will or rather it can be effected as a consequence of a “call from the earth”. A soul can be sent to the earth or it can be attracted by the earth. In the first case it is an act analogous to the recall of vertical and moral memory, i.e. analogous to the miracle of the resurrection of Lazarus. In the latter case it is an event that is half-voluntary and half-involuntary, where the soul falls—often without realising it—into the sphere of terrestrial attraction, which bears it to birth, and thereby it is made little by little to forget its experiences above. Birth is then a forgetting of heaven and simultaneously a recall to the earth.
术语「天职」与「自定的任务」
底本 a true mission 与 arbitrary missions。汤贝格在此下了本封信最不客气的判断之一：

• 真正的天职——为既有之物的升华与灵性化效劳，使传统更新、更强；
• 自定的任务——以个人创见取代传统，志在改变历史走向。

他说后者「就像彗星横扫过一样」，并在极端处补了一句：自定的任务往往「招惹火星人或金星人前来干涉地球的事务」。这句话在中译里保留了，读来突兀，但底本确有其语——他指的是行星层级的存有介入人类史，与第十五封信论「第三因」的思路一脉相承。

另：底本这一段在中译里被拆成了两段（「人在地球上真正的任务……」与「极端一点的说法是……」）。本版按中译分段排印。
72「圣诞」显然不是这样的，因为促使灵魂降生的是对上主的记忆。灵魂并没有因化身成人而忘却神，它对神仍保有清晰的记忆。灵魂是在与神合一的「惯性状态」下投生的。这个烙印会一直在灵魂意志的记号中发挥作用，从「圣诞」一直到它在地球上的生命结束为止。这样的灵魂才有资格谈「天职」或是「被拣选」。真正的天职并不是按自己的品味、兴趣甚至理想去达成的。自行决定的「任务」，即使出发点是为了造福世界，也可能为人类史带来一场大混乱。这些不合时宜的「任务」导致了许多灾难的发生——就像彗星横扫过一样，干扰了真正的祥和以及富建设性的能量流动。
中 511
This is not so with “holy birth”. Here it is the remembrance of the divine which is the force which accomplishes incarnation. It is not thanks to forgetting of the divine that the soul is then incarnated, but rather thanks to its remembering. It is in the state of “habitual union” with God that the soul is incarnated. Then its will does not lose memory of the divine. This memory acts in it, imprinted as it is in the soul’s will, during the whole terrestrial life which follows a “holy birth”. One could then speak of a “mission”, or an “election”…and rightly, because such a mission is the only one which really exists. For the true mission is not what the human being proposes to do on the earth according to his tastes, his interests and even his ideals, but rather what God wants him to do. Arbitrary “missions”, although due to the best intentions in the world, have only contributed confusion to human history. It is to these inopportune “missions” that we owe many crises upsetting the life of mankind’s living traditions—interrupting, in the guise of passing comets, the peaceful and constructive flow of true progress.
73人在地球上真正的任务，就是为灵性的升华及净化效劳。这股驱力能活化和强化灵修传统。自行决定的任务则是以个人的创见取代传统，目标是要改变历史的走向。◆ 中译在此另起一段，英文原为一整段极端一点的说法是：只有天职才能让地球上的人类——家庭、文明、文化、宗教等等变得更完美；自行决定的任务则往往会招惹火星人或金星人前来干涉地球的事务。
中 511
A true mission on the earth serves the cause of the ennoblement and spiritualisation of that which is, i.e. of what lives as tradition. It brings an impulse effecting the rejuvenation and intensification of tradition. Arbitrary missions, on the contrary, aim at revolutionising the course of mankind’s history and substituting specific innovations for what lives as tradition. In pushing this to the extreme one 354could say: a true mission brings to greater perfection everything human on the earth—the family, civilisation, culture, religion, etc., whilst arbitrary missions can result in summoning the intervention of Martians or Venusians so that they rule affairs on the earth!
74诞生、苏醒、忆起，以及死亡、沉睡、遗忘，构成了现实的两股「支柱般的力量」。它们会显现在忆起和遗忘里，也会显现在苏醒和沉睡、诞生和死亡的韵律中，在呼吸、血液循环和营养的吸收之中也有它们的作用力。它们是所有领域——身、心、灵三个层次的「是」与「不是」。
中 512
Now, birth, awakening and recall, on the one hand, and death, falling asleep and forgetting, on the other hand, constitute, so to say, the two “pillar-forces” of reality. They manifest in remembering and forgetting, in the rhythm of sleeping and waking and in that of birth and death—as well as in the respiration of organisms, in the circulation of blood, and in alimentation. They are the “yes” and “no” in every domain—mental, psychic and physical.
术语两根柱子：「是」与「不是」
底本 the two “pillar-forces” of reality——诞生／苏醒／忆起为一柱，死亡／沉睡／遗忘为另一柱。两柱贯穿身、心、灵三层，也贯穿呼吸、血液循环与营养的节律。

「柱」这个词是有出处的：所罗门圣殿门前的雅斤与波阿斯，也是卡巴拉生命树的左右两柱。汤贝格没有点名，但接下来引《马太福音》5:37「你们说话，是，就说是，不是，就说不是；再多说便是出于那恶者」，正是把这一对柱子读成实在本身的结构——「多余的话」就是蛇。
75〔原译：福音《箴言》中所提到的 · The Gospel maxim〕：「你们说话，是，就说是，不是，就说不是；再多说便是出于那邪恶者。」（《马太福音》第 5 章，第 37 节）彰显出了上面那段话的含义。「是」与「不是」乃现实的本质或真相，任何「多余的话」都是出自魔或蛇的作用力。创世记里蛇所说的话都是「多余的话」——「是」与「不是」之外的第三种话。
中 512
The Gospel maxim, “Let what you say be simply ‘Yes’ or ‘No’; anything more than this comes from evil” (Matthew v, 37) reveals its significance in this context. The “yes” and the “no”—this is the essential of reality, i.e. the truth, pure and simple, whilst the “surplus” comes from evil, i.e. it belongs to the sphere of the serpent. For the serpent of Genesis has his own word—the word which is the “surplus” over “yes” and “no”. He is in possession of a third term.
校记「福音《箴言》」——这里不是《箴言》书
底本作 The Gospel maxim：maxim 是「箴言、格言」的通名，整个词组的意思是「福音书里的那句格言」，所引正是紧接着的《马太福音》5:37。

中译作「福音《箴言》中所提到的」，把 maxim 当成了书名号里的《箴言》——旧约智慧书之一。于是读者会以为汤贝格引的是《箴言》书，而《箴言》里并没有这句话。

本版改作「福音书里的那句箴言」，点橙色处可回看原译。

这一处值得留意的地方在于：紧接着的整段推理全靠这句「是与不是」立住——它是「两根柱子」的经文依据，也是下文判定蛇「多说了话」的尺子。出处一错，尺子就悬空了。
76现在让我们回到这封信一开始所提到的：蛇说「你们不会死」，难道它是在说谎吗？或者它说的是真相，只不过这种真相只专属于蛇的世界？换言之：除了「是」与「不是」所代表的「生」与「死」之外，蛇究竟多说了什么？
中 512
And here we return to the question that we posed at the beginning of this Letter, namely; Has the serpent, having said, “You will not die”, simply told a lie, or has he stated a truth from the range of truths proper to the domain of the serpent? In other words: What is the “surplus” that the serpent adds to the “yes” and “no” understood as life and death?
77亲爱的朋友，假如你认识了前几封信里提到的有关「生命」与「电能」、「圣洁本质」与「蛇的本质」在根本上的差异，就能洞悉在「是」与「不是」所代表的「生」与「死」之外的那些「多余的话」背后的秘密。
中 512
If you accept, dear Unknown Friend, what we have said in the preceding Letters on the difference in principle that there is between life and electricity, between the principle of the Virgin and that of the serpent, you will certainly be able to penetrate more deeply the secret of the “surplus” offered and promised by the serpent to humanity concerning the “yes” and “no” understood as life and death.
术语「结晶化」与「放射」——本信后半的主轴
底本 crystallisation 与 radiation。这是汤贝格从炼金术借来的一对词，用来分判两类灵性道路：

• 结晶派：靠「是」与「不是」之间的挣扎产生摩擦，摩擦生电，电使质料固化——把自己炼成一块能抵挡死亡的结晶。
• 放射派（他自认所属）：反其道，彻底消解结晶，使人成为发光发热的中枢。经文根据是《马太福音》13:43「义人在天父的国里，要发出光来，像太阳一样」。

这一对词会一直用到本封信结尾：牌面上的骸骨（结晶的极致）与复活（放射的极致），十字的横轴与纵轴。
78这个秘密是：蛇允诺了一种「结晶化功能」，若是按照它的「卷曲」法则来修练，人便有可能抵挡死亡或是对死亡「免疫」。这种结晶化功能是透过摩擦力产生的；它是一种在「是」与「不是」之间挣扎所产生的电能。
中 512
Here is this secret: the serpent offers and promises such a crystallisation, according to the principle of enfoldment, that the human being will resist death and become, so to say, “death-proof”, immune to death. This crystallisation is effected through friction, i.e. by the electrical energy which is produced by the struggle between “yes” and “no” in man.
79亲爱的朋友，你或许已经知道某些门派，像是密修派或其他派别，一直在教导「结晶化」的锻练方式；另外一些派别则教导如何「放射光热」，亦即完全去除结晶化作用，使人转化成「太阳」或发射能量的中枢。「那时候，义人在天父的国度里，将好像太阳发射光辉。」（《马太福音》第 13 章，第 43 节）——此即「光热派」或是〔原译：基督隐修之道 · Christian Hermeticism〕的灵修目标。
中 512
Without doubt you know, dear Unknown Friend, that there are schools—occult or other—which teach and practise crystallisation and that there are other schools which teach and practise radiation, i.e. the complete de-crystallisation of the human being and his transformation into a “sun”, into a centre of radiation. “Then the righteous will shine like the sun in the kingdom of their Father” (Matthew xiii, 43)—this is the practical aim of “schools of radiation”, to which that of Christian Hermeticism belongs.
80「结晶派」的修练方式也有许多类型，而且十分普及。有些是完全秘密的，抱持的是非常严肃的宗旨；有些则广为人知，通常是以「健康、回春、长寿」等一般性的动机做为诉求。我不想谈论那些秘修派的方法，因为那是他们的而非我的秘密。我也不想谈论那些已经变成流行的锻练方式。我即将举例说明的某个〔原译：玄学派 · occult school〕，你一旦了解其宗旨和方法，就会明白我的感受了。我之所以选择下述这个玄秘团体做为例子，是因为它介乎秘修派和伪装成流行的养生锻练法之间，更何况它已经选择公开教诲的内容，因此我有权谈论它，引用其资料。
中 513
The “schools of crystallisation” are quite numerous and widespread. There are those which are entirely secret, with very serious intentions; there are also those which are known in the guise of almost popular movements for “health, rejuvenation and longevity”. I shall not speak here of the practices of entirely secret schools, 355the secret not being mine but that of others. Also I shall not speak about the almost popular movement, because it will certainly be easy to understand their aim and methods after having understood the aim and method of an occult school that I have chosen as an illustrative example. I have chosen this particular occult school as an example, because it is something between the secret schools and the almost popular movements and because it has itself taken the decision to show itself in broad daylight, thus authorising me to speak of it and to cite its documents, accessible to everyone.
81我指的就是葛吉夫学派（G. I. Gurdjieff）。我将引用邬斯宾斯基2的《寻找奇迹》（In Search of the Miraculous）中的一段内文。以下便是邬斯宾斯基对葛吉夫教诲的理解和诠释，内容是关于星光体如何继续存留的修练方式：
中 513
I have in mind the school of G. I. Gurdjieff, and I am going to cite the work In Search of the Miraculous by P. D. Ouspensky. Now, the following is the teaching of Gurdjieff, such as it has been understood and formulated by Ouspensky, concerning the practical task of survival:
背景葛吉夫与邬斯宾斯基
G. I. 葛吉夫（George Ivanovich Gurdjieff, 约 1866–1949），亚美尼亚–希腊裔灵性教师，「第四道」的创立者，1922 年起在枫丹白露办「人类和谐发展学院」。其核心论断是：人是机器，且没有一个统一的「我」；不朽不是天赋，须靠内在挣扎「结晶」出来。

P. D. 邬斯宾斯基（Pyotr Demianovich Ouspensky, 1878–1947），俄国数学家、哲学作家，1915 年在莫斯科遇见葛吉夫，后与之决裂。《寻找奇迹》(In Search of the Miraculous, London, 1949) 是他记录葛吉夫口授教诲的书，也是这套体系最系统的文本——汤贝格所引即此书 1969 年版。

汤贝格为什么偏偏选中葛吉夫？他自己交代得很清楚：这一派「介乎秘修派与流行养生法之间」，而且已经选择公开，所以他有权引用而不算泄密。这是他一贯的分寸——不谈别人不公开的东西。

更要紧的是下一层：他并不把葛吉夫当作敌人。后文他会说葛吉夫「是个温厚的人，有细腻的幽默感，是好儿子、好朋友，富有常识」，只是「世间智慧的代表」——比起巴甫洛夫，他「根本算不上是撒旦拥护者」。汤贝格要驳的是路线，不是人。
82在某一次这种类型的聚会上，有人问起轮回的可能性以及和死者沟通的案例是否可信。「许多事都有发生的可能，」葛吉夫说，「不过我们必须了解，人活着的时候和死后的存在特质，可以说是截然不同的。如果你把人看成是一种机器，一切都受外境影响，那么他此刻是这样，下一刻是那样，再下一刻又变成了第三种模样，可以说毫无未来可言；最终他只会被埋葬。尘归尘，土归土，这句话非常适用于他。因此他为了拥有未来性，必须促成某种结晶化功能，一种内在特质的融合作用，或不受外境影响的自主性。如果人的内在有任何东西可以抵挡外境的影响，那个东西就能抵挡肉体的死亡……不过就算是存活下来，未来还是有许多的可能性。某些结晶化比较彻底的例子显示人死后可以直接转世，其他例子则是所谓的「在另一边继续存活」。这两个例子都意味着生命力会在「星光层」内延续下去，或透过「星光体」的协助延续下去。我想你们都知道这是什么东西，但是你们所熟悉的系统以及这个名称的含义，暗示着所有的人都具备星光体。事实上，这是非常错误的见解。所谓的星光体是透过一种融合作用产生的，过程中必须经历非常艰辛的内在工作和挣扎。人并非生来就具有星光体，而且极少人拥有它。它一旦形成，便可能在肉身死亡后继续存活，也可能在另一个肉体内重生……这种融合作用或内在的合一状态是以「摩擦」的方式达成的，而摩擦又来自于「是」与「不是」之间的挣扎。人的内心如果没有挣扎，如果内在没有任何冲突而能运作自如的话，他就会安住在自己的真相上面。但如果他的心里出现了挣扎，尤其是在特定的思路上，那么就会形成固着的特质，就会开始「结晶化」……结晶化的作用可能发生在任何基础上，譬如在一名非常厉害、名符其实的土匪身上。我就认识这样的一个高加索土匪，他可以拿着步枪一动也不动地躲在路边的石头后面长达八小时。你办得到吗？可是你要明白，在整个过程中，他的内心是不断在挣扎的。他既热又渴，蚊虫也不停地叮咬，但他却完全不动地站着。另外有一名僧侣，他很恐惧恶魔，整晚不停地磕头祷告，于是结晶化作用就产生了……而这样的人也可能变得「不朽」。（P. D. Ouspensky, In Search of the Miraculous, London, 1969, pp. 32-32）
中 513
On one occasion, at one of these meetings, someone asked about the possibility of reincarnation, and whether it was possible to believe in cases of communication with the dead. “Many things are possible,” said G. (Gurdjieff). “But it is necessary to understand that man’s being, both in life and after death, if it does exist after death, may be very different in quality. The ‘man-machine’ with whom everything depends upon external influences, with whom everything happens, who is now one, the next moment another, and the next moment a third, has no future of any kind; he is buried and that is all. Dust returns to dust. This applies to him. In order to be able to speak of any kind of future life there must be a certain crystallisation, a certain fusion of man’s inner qualities, a certain independence of external influences. If there is anything in a man able to resist external influences, then this very thing itself may also be able to resist the death of the physical body…But even if something survives, its future can be very varied. In certain cases of fuller crystallisation, what people call ‘reincarnation’ may be possible after death, and, in other cases, what people call ‘existence on the other side’. In both cases it is the continuation of life in the ‘astral body’, or with the help of the ‘astral body’. You know what the expression ‘astral body’ means. But the systems with which you are acquainted and which use this expression state that all men have an ‘astral body’. This is quite wrong. What may be called the ‘astral body’ is obtained by means of fusion, that, is, by means of terribly hard inner work and struggle. Man is not born with it. And only very few men acquire an ‘astral body’. If it is formed it may continue to live after the death of the physical body, and it may be born again in another physical body…Fusion, inner unity, is obtained by means of ‘friction’, by the struggle between ‘yes’ and ‘no’ in man. If a man lives without inner struggle, if everything happens in him without 356opposition, if he goes wherever he is drawn or wherever the wind blows, he will remain such as he is. But if a struggle begins in him, and particularly if there is a definite line in this struggle, then, gradually, permanent traits begin to form themselves, he begins to ‘crystallise’…Crystallisation is possible on any foundations. Take for example a brigand, a really good, genuine brigand. I knew such brigands in the Caucasus. He will stand with a rifle behind a stone by the roadside for eight hours without stirring. Could you do this? All the time, mind you, a struggle is going on in him. He is thirsty and hot, and flies are biting him; but he stands still. Another is a monk; he is afraid of the devil; all night long he beats his head on the floor and prays. Thus crystallisation is achieved…Such people can become immortal.” (P. D. Ouspensky, In Search of the Miraculous, London, 1969, pp. 31-32)
83现在让我们思考一下引文中的几个重点。第一，所谓的「星光体」，是从肉体中的精微体产生的活力运输工具。根据这段引文的说法，「不朽」并非人与生俱来的权利，也不是神赐予的礼物，而是透过结晶化制造出来的一种崭新的灵体；它可以抵挡死亡，在肉体毁灭后继续存活。换言之，这崭新的灵体不是神创造的，是人在自己体内制造出来的。它是从「是」与「不是」的摩擦中诞生的结晶体。由此可知，盗贼、僧侣以及练家子型的玄秘家，都是透过挣扎和努力产生电能，进而让自己变得「不朽」。
中 515
Let us now take account of the essential points in the quoted text. Firstly, it is the physical body which gives birth to what is called in the quotation the “astral body” which will be the bearer of survival. Then, according to the text, immortality is neither a birthright of the human soul nor a gift of divine grace—it is made by means of the crystallisation of a new body within the physical body which can resist death and survive the destruction of the physical body. That is to say, the soul created by God does not exist; it must be created by the human being from within the human physical body. Thus it is a quantity of energy crystallised within the human physical body and engendered through this latter, produced by friction or the struggle between “yes” and “no” in man. For both the robber and the monk, and also the occultist, can become immortal through the energy that they produce by their efforts.
84这涉及的是一套人体工程，也就是在人体内建构一座高塔，一栋有「四房」或四层楼的屋子；它从地面高耸至天国，从凡间延伸至永恒世界。《圣经》记载了这种「高耸入云」的建塔方式，以及它如何「显扬我们自己的名，免得我们被分散到世界各地」（《创世记》第 11 章，第 4 节）。此即构建巴别塔的理念和上千年的筑造方式。以下是葛吉夫的说法：
中 515
It is a matter of a plan of construction, from within the physical body, of a tower or a “house of four rooms” or levels (Ouspensky, ibid., p. 44), rising from the sphere of mortality to that of immortality, from earth to heaven. Now, the Bible knows of the method of building “a tower with its top in the heavens” and of making “a name for ourselves lest we be scattered abroad upon the face of the whole earth” (Genesis xi, 4). It is the ideal and millennial-old method of construction of the tower of Babel. The “tower of Babel” is a very ancient method. The following is what Gurdjieff says about it:
85有一种非常古老的教诲，我们可以在许多旧有的和新的学说中发现线索。它主张一个发展完全的人是由四种精微体构成的。这四种精微程度不同的体相互渗透，形成了四种有机结构，它们之间虽然有明确的关系，却能独立自主地运作。这四种有机结构之所以会出现，是因为人体这个极其复杂的有机体，在某种情况下形成了新的精微体，可以提供意识一个比肉身更方便、更有感应力的使唤工具……在其他情况下，第二个精微体内又会形成第三个精微体，第三个精微体也有自己的特质……这第三个精微体内又可能形成第四个精微体，它们的不同就像第三个不同于第二个、第二个不同于第一个一样。
中 516
According to an ancient teaching, traces of which may be found in many systems, old and new, a man who has attained the full development possible for man, a man in the full sense of the word, consists of four bodies. These four bodies are composed of substances which gradually become finer and finer, mutually interpenetrate one another, and form four independent organisms, standing in a definite relationship to one another but capable of independent action. The reason why it is possible 357for four bodies to exist is that the human organism, that is, the physical body, has such a complex organisation that, under certain conditions, a new independent organism can grow in it, affording a much more convenient and responsive instrument for the activity of consciousness than the physical body…In this second body, under certain conditions, a third body can grow, again having characteristics of its own…In the third body, under certain conditions, a fourth can grow, which differs as much from the third as the third differs from the second and the second from the first…(p. 40)
术语四体说与「曲颈瓶里的金属粉末」
葛吉夫这段比喻是全信最具体的一处技术描述：曲颈瓶里装着各色金属粉末，瓶子一动，粉末的相对位置就跟着变——这就是普通人。要让它们固定，只能在瓶底生火，靠加热熔合成一块不可分割的合金。

那团火即摩擦，摩擦来自「是」与「不是」之间的挣扎。熔合出来的即第二体（星光体）；再注入新性质即第三体；再固化即第四体。四体齐备的人才是「完整的人」，其属性之一就是不朽。

汤贝格接受这个描述的准确性，只是要问它的方向——这一问就引出了下面的巴别塔。
86有一种东方教诲用以下的方式来形容这四种精微体的功能、渐进的发展过程以及发展条件：现在让我们想像一下，有一个装满各种金属粉末的容器或曲颈瓶，其中的粉末是分开的，每当瓶子的位置无预警地被移动时，粉末的相对位置就会跟着变动……这些粉末之间的互动不可能以机械化的混合方式固定下来，但粉末是可以融合的，其本质确保了这一点。如果要办到这一点，就必须在容器底端生起一种特殊的火，经由加热让粉末融合结合在一起。以这样的方式融合的粉末会变成一种化合物……容器里的物质因而变成了不可分割的「整体」。这便是第二个精微体形成的过程。达成融合作用的火是由「摩擦力」产生的，摩擦力又出自于「是」与「不是」的挣扎……
中 516
An Eastern teaching describes the functions of the four bodies, their gradual growth, and the conditions of this growth, in the following way: Let us imagine a vessel or a retort filled with various metallic powders. The powders are not in any way connected with each other and every accidental change in the position of the retort changes the relative position of the powders…It is impossible to stabilise the interrelation of powders in a state of mechanical mixture. But the powders may be fused; the nature of the powders makes this possible. To do this a special kind of fire must be lighted under the retort which, by heating and melting the powders, finally fuses them together. Fused in this way the powders will be in the state of a chemical compound…The contents of the retort have become indivisible, “individual”. This is a picture of the formation of the second body. The fire by means of which fusion is attained is produced by “friction”, which in its turn is produced in man by the struggle between “yes” and “no”…
87第三种精微体的形成，相当于把新的特性注入原本的化合物中，……第四种精微体的形成则相当于固化那些新加入的特性。只有「四种精微体」都发展齐全的人，才算是真正完整的「人」。这种人拥有许多平常人没有的特质，其中之一就是「不朽」。（同前著作，pp. 40, 43-44）
中 517
The process of imparting new properties to the alloy corresponds to the process of the formation of the third body…The process of fixing these acquired properties corresponds to the process of the formation of the fourth body. And only the man who possesses four fully developed bodies can be called a “man” in the full sense of the word. This man possesses many properties which ordinary man does not possess. One of these properties is immortality. (P. D. Ouspensky, In Search of the Miraculous, London, 1969, pp. 40, 43-44)
88由此可知，「容器底端生起的火」是由摩擦力造成的，摩擦力又出自于「是」与「不是」的挣扎，因此这种火就是我们所谓的「电能」。有了这种电能或摩擦力，才出现了结晶化的作用过程。
中 517
Now, the “special fire lit under the retort” is due to friction which is, in its turn, the product of the struggle between “yes” and “no”. This fire is therefore what we understand by “electricity”. It is therefore thanks to electricity, or energy produced by friction, that the process of crystallisation works.
89巴别塔的建筑师就是用这种火来调配建材。「『来吧，我们要做砖，把砖烧透了。』他们就拿砖当石头，又拿石漆当灰泥」（《创世记》第 11 章，3 节）。
中 517
The architects of the tower of Babel also made use of fire for the preparation of its materials of construction. “‘Come, let us make bricks, and burn them thoroughly.’ And they had brick for stone, and bitumen for mortar” (Genesis xi, 3).
90「巴别塔」的建造方式依循的是「反向」的结晶化作用。正常的结晶体——「石头」——是由气体变成液体、液体变成固体的最后结果。依照这种程序的作用力，蒸汽先是变成水（液体），水又结成冰，所以冰就是结晶化的蒸汽。同样地，一个笼统却热诚的意图会先形成一股逻辑推演的动能，然后才变成定义明确的法则。换一种说法是：灵变成了心，心变成了身。
中 517
The essence of the method of “construction of the tower of Babel” is inverse crystallisation. Normal crystallisation—the “stone”—is the final state of the process 358of transition from the gaseous to the liquid state and from the liquid to the solid state. Thus vapour becomes water (liquid) and water becomes ice. Ice is crystallised vapour. Similarly, a general but warm intention becomes a current of discursive thought which, in its turn, results in a well-defined formula. Or in still other terms: the spiritual becomes psychic and the psychic becomes corporeal.
术语正向结晶与反向结晶
本封信全部论证的落点，值得慢读。

正常的结晶化是由上往下：气→液→固；蒸汽→水→冰。对应到人身上：一个笼统而热诚的意图先成为一股推理的动能，再落成一条定义明确的法则——灵变成心，心变成身。

「建造巴别塔」的方式是由下往上：由身转化成心和灵。既然灵化为身的过程使人变得必朽，那么身升为灵的过程，会不会反过来使人不朽？

——这就是蛇的整套方案。汤贝格不否认它行得通；他要指出的是它通向哪里。英文栏内的两幅图（底本原图）就是这两个方向。

《创世记》11:3-4 那句「来吧，我们要做砖，把砖烧透了」被他读作技术说明：烧砖用的火，就是摩擦生出的电。
91因此，正常的结晶化过程是「由上往下」的体现：
中 518
The process of normal crystallisation is therefore one of concretisation from above below:
92反之，「巴别塔的建造方式」则是「由下往上」的结晶化过程：
中 518
The process of crystallisation designated as the “construction of the tower of Babel”, takes place, in contrast, from below above:
93后者是由身转化成心和灵的过程。因此，人的确有可能战胜死亡而变得不朽……形体上的不朽。人如果在灵、心、身的转化过程中会变得必朽，那么由身转化成心和灵的过程中，不也可以变得不朽吗？
中 518
With regard to this latter process, it is a matter of transformation into “body” of the psychic and spiritual. And it is thus that one can conquer death and realise immortality…corporeal immortality. For if the spiritual and the psychic, in becoming corporeal, become mortal, would it not be possible that the corporeal, in rising to the psychic and spiritual, becomes immortal?
94这究竟是一种可行的假设，还是一种幻想？虽然这个问题属于第十六张大阿卡纳的探讨范围，而第十六封信也会就这个问题做出解答，但是为了得到完全正确的答案，我们必须先思考一些待解的现象。
中 518
Is this scheme realisable or is it simply an illusion, pure and simple? Although this question belongs to the framework of problems of the sixteenth Major Arcanum of the Tarot, and although it will be treated in the sixteenth Letter, let us nonetheless consider some facts with a view to coming to an answer.
95我指的就是那些仍然存在于人间的精微体，所谓的死者或「鬼」的显相。鬼的确是存在的，这不是信或不信的问题，而是真实存在的现象。除了一些个人的亲身经验之外，有太多的资料足以证实鬼的存在。人死亡之后，生前的「某些特质」会伪装成一股活跃的能量，以向外显化的方式（声音、动作等）现形。我们可以说有一种固化的能量会透过死亡释放，它可以凝结住、不至于消散，而且是以实体或「形体」显相的。
中 518
The facts that I have in mind are those of corporeal survival, i.e. physical manifestations that one attributes—rightly or wrongly—to dead people or to “ghosts”. Ghosts exist. This is not a question of belief; it is a matter of fact. There is an immense literature, without speaking of facts that one can find in the sphere of personal experience, which bears witness to the existence of ghosts. Now it is no longer a matter of believing or denying; now it is a matter only of understanding and explaining. Ghosts exist therefore. Thus it happens from time to time after someone’s death that this person or “something” of him or similar to him manifests in an outward and physical way (noises, movements, etc.) in the guise of an active energy. It is as if a certain quantity of energy, freed through death, but remaining condensed and not dispersed, manifests as an entity or as an individual “body”.
96我从一则关于鬼这个现象的分析中，摘录了它的几种特征：
中 519
An analysis of the manifestations of ghosts has enabled me to extract from them the following characteristic traits:
背景鬼的三条特征
这三条中译原样保留，且底本本身就是汤贝格自述的观察摘要，未注出处（他只说「从一则关于鬼这个现象的分析中摘录」）。三条依次是：由心理–生理电能构成的实体、意识极狭窄且执著到近乎疯狂、能量随时间递减，除非旁人以肯定态度供养它。

第三条末尾那个「把它的能量吸进自己体内」的办法，以及随之而来的强烈触电感，是全书少数几处近乎实操记述的段落之一。汤贝格随即把这种触电感当作证据：鬼的「灵体」本质上就是电。

请留意他对这三条的用法：不是猎奇，而是拿来证明结晶化确实能造出抵抗死亡的东西——蛇的方案不是空话。
971. 鬼是一种由心理和生理电能构成的实体，相对于活着的正常人，它拥有的意识层次比较低等；
中 519
3591. a ghost is an entity made up of psycho-physiological electrical energy, with an inferior consciousness in comparison with that of a normal human person;
982. 借由鬼的行径和行为方式所观察到的意识状态，是非常狭隘和极端执着的——这使人不禁想用「疯狂」一词来描述，因为它总是以单一的激情、癖好或固着想法的结晶体现身。
中 519
2. the consciousness which is revealed by the actions of a ghost, and by its way of acting in general, is very limited and extremely specialised—one is tempted to characterise it as “maniacal”, since it manifests itself as the crystallisation of a single passion, a single habit, or a single fixed idea;
993. 鬼的组成能量会随着时间递减——除非身边的人以一种肯定和赞同的态度支持它，否则很快就消散了。你可以透过教会的驱鬼仪式或个人的祈祷使它消失，或者还有一种需要勇气的办法，那就是将它扣住、然后把它的能量吸进自己体内，以驱散它的电能。但是我不推荐后面这种办法，因为当鬼的能量进入你体内时，会带来极强的触电感。在此我必须进一步说明的是，这种触电感也让人确实明了鬼的「灵体」本质上就是电能。同时这也提供了证据，使人心知肚明鬼并不是死者的灵魂，而是尚未甩掉的沉重包袱。如果有人将鬼的电能吸入体内而将其驱散，不久之后死者必定会以托梦方式表达感激，因为他终于卸下了这个沉重的包袱。
中 519
3. the energy of which the ghost is constituted becomes weakened with time—provided that it is not nourished by an affirmative and favourable attitude from its human entourage; it fades away. One can make it disappear through the Church ritual of exorcism, or by individual prayer, or lastly by a special action which demands courage and which consists in clasping and breathing in the ghost, in such a way as to receive it into oneself and of oneself to make the electrical energy of the ghost dissipate. I dare not recommend this latter method because it entails the experience of an electric shock—which can be excessive—at the moment when the energy of the ghost passes into your organism. I may add, however, that it is this experience of an electric shock which gives absolute certainty with respect to the electrical nature of the “body” of the ghost. At the same time it can also supply proof—in the inner forum of consciousness, it goes without saying—that the ghost is not the soul of the departed one, and that it is a burden to him, being bound to the soul of the departed by a heavy link of responsibility. In the case that I have mentioned, soon after the dissipation of the ghost’s electrical energy through its reception into oneself, the departed one hastens to make acknowledgement of his gratitude, by means of a very vivid and clear dream, for his deliverance from this heavy burden.
100所以鬼究竟是什么？套句葛吉夫的说法，鬼就是心理活动的固化作用在肉体内形成的产物；它能够抵挡肉体的死亡。这也是葛吉夫所谓的「星光体」，「它一旦形成，就可以在肉体死亡后继续存活……如果它不重生，那么一阵子之后也会消亡；它并非不朽的，却可以在肉体消失后存留一段时间」。当然，葛吉夫和〔原译：隐修之道 · Hermeticism〕论及的星光体完全无关，因为后者和灵魂的精神记忆有关。
中 520
What, then, is a ghost? It is exactly what Gurdjieff teaches concerning the product of psychic crystallisation effected from within the physical body, and which can resist the death of the latter. This is the “astral body” of which Gurdjieff said that, “if it is formed it may continue to live after the death of the physical body…if it is not re-born, then, in the course of time, it also dies; it is not immortal but it can live long after the death of the physical body” (Ouspensky, op. cit, p. 32). Of course, the “astral body” spoken of by Gurdjieff has nothing to do with the “astral body” of Hermeticism, which latter is, truth to tell, simply the totality of the soul’s psychic memories.
术语鬼＝结晶化的产物
此处把两条线接了起来：葛吉夫说的「星光体」（靠挣扎熔合出来、能在肉身死后存活一阵的东西），与民间所说的「鬼」，是同一样东西。

汤贝格特意补了一句划界的话：这与赫尔墨斯主义所说的星光体完全无关——后者关乎灵魂的精神记忆，不是电能的固化物。同名而异实，容易混，他先挑明。

那位「拿着步枪一动不动躲八小时的高加索土匪」与「整晚磕头祷告的僧侣」，在这个框架里是同一类人：两人体内都固化出了高密度的「情结」能量。结晶化不问善恶，只问强度。
101鬼永远是结晶化作用的产物，亦即欲望、激情或强烈的意图在人体内固化成的一种「心理情结」能量。因此，「厉害的土匪」可以「拿着步枪一动也不动地躲在路边的石头后面长达八小时」，「另外有一名僧侣……他很恐惧恶魔，整晚不停地磕头祷告」，这两者的体内都会形成固化的高密度「情结」能量，这是一种心理活动和电能的「分身」，可以用来阻隔死亡。
中 520
A ghost is always constituted as a consequence of crystallisation, i.e. crystallisation 360of a desire, a passion, or a purpose of great intensity, which produces a complex of energy in the human being. Thus, a “genuine brigand” who stands “with a rifle behind a stone by the roadside for eight hours without stirring” or “a monk…(who) is afraid of the devil (and) all night long he beats his head on the floor and prays” (Ouspensky, op. cit., p. 32) in fact crystallise within them a complex of energy, a psycho-electrical double, which would be able, as a dense complex, to resist death.
102同样的事情也会发生在具有强烈欲望、激情或意图的人身上；他们是用「建造巴别塔」的科学方式来达成不朽的。人不但能透过结晶化的欲望、激情和意图来驱动精微分身，还能赋予它发达的智识结构和自动式记忆，将物质次元的经验全部贮存起来。这类玄秘家的「自我」会跟他的分身结盟，因为后者拥有他一切的记忆和智识，因此可以重新化成肉身——彻底略过炼狱以及净化、启蒙、合一的过程，也就是躲过了灵魂死后必定会面临的挑战3。
中 520
And the same thing that happens with human beings who are possessed by strong desires, passions and intentions can be achieved methodically by making use of the scientific method of the “construction of the tower of Babel”. Then one could not only animate the double crystallised from a desire, a passion or a dominant intention, but also equip it with an intellectual apparatus of very developed functioning and a mechanical memory in which all the facts of experience on the physical plane are accumulated. The “self” of such an occultist would then be allied to this double, who is the bearer of his memory and intellect, and could incarnate himself anew—avoiding purgatory and the whole path of purification, illumination and union which is the lot of the human soul after death.
103因此，建构「巴别塔」的理念和方式并不是单纯的幻想，而是另一种成为不朽的方式，也是《创世记》里的蛇所说的：「你们不会因为吃了善恶树的果子而死。」善恶树的果子令人在「是」与「不是」之间挣扎而产生了内在的摩擦力，这种磨擦力又会形成带电的火，火的结晶化则会形成抵挡死亡的结晶体。这便是蛇的允诺或这套程式的含义。这套程式既是「建造巴别塔」的千年古法基础，也是唯物科学的核心秘密或玄机所在。
中 521
It is therefore not purely and simply a matter of an illusion in the case of the ideal and method of the “construction of the tower of Babel”. Rather, it is a matter of another kind of immortality, notably that which the serpent of Genesis had in mind when he said, “You will not die if you eat of the fruit of the Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil”. For the fruit of the Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil produces the inner friction in man of the struggle between “yes” and “no”, and this friction in its turn produces the electrical fire which effects the crystallisation whose product will resist death. This is the meaning of the promise—or rather the programme—of the serpent. This programme underlies the millennial-old method of the “construction of the tower of Babel”, and it constitutes the esoteric kernel or the hidden secret of materialistic science in general.
104我们虽然用葛吉夫（和邬斯宾斯基）的说法来解释「建造巴别塔」的概念和方式，但葛吉夫曾公开表示自己是纯正的唯物主义者，完全和玄秘事物无关，所以他的观点只能代表一般大众。他只不过清楚地阐述了是什么在激发和促使千千万万的科学家，无觉知或半带觉知地投入长寿的研究，亦即不仰赖上主或密契主义，只透过人为的修练方式来战胜死亡：这才是建造巴别塔真正的理由。
中 521
We have chosen Gurdjieff (and Ouspensky) to exemplify the ideal and the method of the “construction of the tower of Babel”, but Gurdjieff—being openly materialistic in the true sense of this word, and being deprived of all mystical sense—only spoke on behalf of the multitude. All he did was to give forth clearly what animates and impels—in an unconscious or semi-conscious way—millions and millions of scientists devoted to the cause of longevity, i.e. to the victory over death through human science, without God and without mysticism: the universal cause of the construction of the tower of Babel.
105葛吉夫只是一位唯物主义的科学代表；他知道科学想要的是什么，也知道自己想要的是什么。他是一个温厚的人，具有细腻的幽默感，也是一个好儿子、好朋友，而且十分富有常识——我所举出的只是眼前浮现的一些他所具备的特质。因此，我们不该将他视为「阴险的先知」或带有特殊使命的「撒旦使者」。不，他只不过是「世间智慧」的代表，他的体悟是奠基于经验的，因此不带有任何玄秘倾向。比起俄国著名生理学家〔原译：巴夫洛夫 · Pavlov〕（Pavlov）或任何唯物科学的代表人物，葛吉夫根本算不上是「撒旦拥护者」。
中 521
Gurdjieff is simply a representative of the cause of materialistic science; he knew what it wants in reality and he also knew what he wanted himself. He was, moreover, a good-natured man, endowed with an exquisite sense of humour, a good son, a good friend, and very intelligent with respect to common sense—to indicate only the qualities that he possessed which leap out at one. It would therefore be wrong to see in him a “prophet of darkness” or an instrument of a special “Satanic mission”. No, he was simply a good representative of the “wisdom of this 361world”, i.e. of good sense and empirical experience without any mystical inclination. Gurdjieff was not any more a “Satanist” than the celebrated Russian physiologist Pavlov or any other representative of materialistic science.
背景巴甫洛夫与葛吉夫，谁更「撒旦」
伊万·巴甫洛夫（Иван Павлов, 1849–1936），俄国生理学家，条件反射学说的创立者，1904 年诺贝尔生理学或医学奖得主。他的工作把行为解释成刺激—反应的机械链条，正是本封信所说「自动化」的科学化身。

汤贝格在此作了一个刻意的比较：比起巴甫洛夫或任何唯物科学的代表人物，葛吉夫根本算不上撒旦拥护者。——葛吉夫至少承认有东西可以结晶、可以存续；彻底的反射论则连这一层都不留。

这个比较也解释了他为何对葛吉夫用词宽和：他真正针对的是把人整个化约为机械的那种科学，葛吉夫只是这条路上走得比较远、也比较诚实的一个人。
106然而无疑地，他所教导的「由下往上的结晶化修练」，无论在实修或理论上都不能和荣格的个体化之道、赫尔墨斯主义或卡巴拉的教诲相提并论。赫尔墨斯主义所传授的结晶化修炼是一套由上往下的运作方式；它的灵知是密契体验的结晶，神圣魔法是灵知的结晶，结晶化的神圣魔法则是其〔原译：隐修哲学 · Hermetic philosophy〕。因此，葛吉夫的〔原译：唯物玄学 · materialistic occultism〕就是以结晶化的唯物科学取代或废止了密契体验。
中 522
Assuredly, his practical and theoretical teaching of crystallisation from below above is not compatible either with Carl Jung’s process of individuation or with Christian Hermeticism or with the Cabbala. For Hermeticism also teaches a crystallisation but it is a crystallisation from above below, i.e. the crystallisation of which Hermeticism itself—in so far as it is philosophy and knowledge—is the product: crystallised mysticism being gnosis, crystallised gnosis being magic, and crystallised magic being this philosophy and knowledge which passes under the name of “Hermeticism”. Thus if one disregards the intermediary stages, one could say that Hermeticism is crystallised mysticism, whilst Gurdjieff’s materialistic occultism replaces—and abolishes—mysticism by crystallised materialistic science.
背景为什么要提荣格
C. G. 荣格（Carl Gustav Jung, 1875–1961），瑞士精神病学家、分析心理学创立者。汤贝格此处提的是他的个体化之道（Individuation）——人格各部分向着「自性」整合的历程。

并列的三项是：荣格的个体化、赫尔墨斯主义、卡巴拉。三者共同的特征在下一句：由上往下的结晶化——密契体验结晶为灵知，灵知结晶为神圣魔法，神圣魔法结晶为赫尔墨斯哲学。

汤贝格在第一封信里已把荣格列为「把古代魔法／炼金术转化为深层心理学」的当代范例（见后记一段）。这里再提，是为了给「由上往下」找一个现代的、非宗教的见证。
107回到我在这封信一开始提出的问题：创世记的蛇难道是在说谎吗？现在我们可以回答：不，它不是在说谎。它是用另一种不朽，亦即由下往上的「巴别塔」结晶作用，去对抗上主的不朽。它提出了大胆可行的方式，目的是要制造一种由活人和鬼结合成的人类，其灵魂几乎可以不延迟地直接转世，也可以避免经历炼狱到天堂的净化过程。
中 522
In returning to the question posed at the beginning of this Letter: Did the serpent of Genesis simply lie?—we are now able to answer: no, he did not lie. He opposed to divine immortality another immortality: that of crystallisation from below above, or the “tower of Babel”. He advanced the bold programme—but real and realisable—aiming at a mankind which would be composed of the living and of ghosts, with the latter reincarnating almost without delay and avoiding the way which leads through purgatory to heaven.
108你现在应该明白教会为何反对轮回之说了，虽然大部分拥有属灵体验的信徒都知道——不可能不知道——轮回乃真实存在的现象。在这些反对意见的背后有一种深切的理由：以制造鬼身的方式直接轮回或避开净化（炼狱）、启蒙、与神合一的历程，是十分危险的修练方式，因为人活着的时候可能会经不起诱惑而为来世做准备，反倒不为炼狱和天堂做准备了。不去面对永恒的上主、选择死后立即转世，往往涉及结晶化作用和「电能分身」——鬼身——的形成，也就是利用鬼身来逃避炼狱和上主的圣光，将其当成立即转世投生的媒介。事实上，人应当在活着的时候发展出彻底觉醒的意识，以便死后面对上主，而彻底觉醒就是一种炼狱经验；同时人也应该在活着的时候体验到当下即永恒的天堂；人不该在活着的时候为来世做准备，因为这么做会导致结晶化作用、产生出鬼身。对轮回一无所知或者完全否定轮回之说，远比将理想和愿景投射到来世，用蛇所建议的方式获得永生要强上一百倍。我再重申一次，基于上述的理由，教会打从一开始就反对轮回之说，甚至竭力不让这个概念在信徒的意识里扎根。
中 522
You see now, dear Unknown Friend, why the Church was hostile to the doctrine of reincarnation, although the fact of repeated incarnations was known—and could not remain unknown—to a large number of people faithful to the Church with authentic spiritual experience. The deeper reason is the danger of reincarnation by way of the ghost, where one avoids the path of purification (in purgatory), illumination and celestial union. For humanity could succumb to the temptation of preparing for a future terrestrial life, instead of preparing for purgatory and heaven, during earthly life. To prepare for a future terrestrial life, instead of preparing for the confrontation with Eternity, amounts to crystallisation in the sense of the formation of an electric double—the body of the ghost—which could, in its turn, serve as the bridge from one incarnation to another and be the means of evading purgatory and the confrontation with Eternity. One ought during earthly life to prepare for this meeting with a fully awakened consciousness, which is purgatory, and for the experience of the presence of the Eternal, which is heaven, and not to prepare for a future terrestrial life, which would amount to the crystallisation of the “body” of a ghost. It is worth a hundred times more to know nothing of the fact of reincarnation, and to deny the doctrine of reincarnation, than to turn thoughts and desires towards the future terrestrial life and thus to be tempted to resort to the means offered through the promise of immortality made by the serpent. This is why, I repeat, the Church was, from the beginning, hostile to the idea of reincarnation and did all that it could so that this idea would not take root in consciousness—and above all in the human will.
背景教会为什么反对轮回说
全信最需要读慢的一段，也是汤贝格立场最见分寸的一处。

他不否认轮回——「大部分拥有属灵体验的信徒都知道，不可能不知道，轮回乃真实存在的现象」。他要说的是另一件事：把轮回当作显学去修，会把人的注意力从「死后面对上主」挪到「为来世做准备」；而为来世做准备的技术，恰恰就是结晶化——造一个能带着记忆与智识直接投胎的电能分身，绕过炼狱、绕过上主的光。

所以他给出的结论极重：对轮回一无所知，甚至完全否定轮回，也远比用蛇的办法获得永生要好一百倍。教会自始反对轮回说，理由不在教义的洁癖，而在这层实际的危险。

接着他做了一件很少有人做的事：点名请读者去检视自己所属的传统——十九世纪的法国玄秘运动、二十世纪的神智学、人智学、玫瑰十字会，把轮回当显学来研究和修练，是否妥当。这几乎是在批评他自己出身的那个圈子。

他也交代了犹豫的理由：「因为某个非常重视道德的教派的反对而犹豫了一阵子」。最终决定写出来，是因为凡认为轮回是普世现象的人，都背着一份责任。
109我承认，我是因为某个非常重视道德的教派的反对而犹豫了一阵子，最后还是决定将轮回之说的危险性写出来，尤其是滥用它所导致的结果。亲爱的朋友，我相信你能理解，举凡认为轮回非密修者专有而是一种普世现象的人，身上都背负着一份责任；就是这份责任促使我决定将滥用轮回说的现象写出来。因此我恳求你，亲爱的朋友，请以善意和良知去检视十九世纪的法国〔原译：玄学运动 · the occult movement〕、二十世纪的神智学、人智学和玫瑰十字会，他们把轮回说当成显学来研究且当真地修练，到底是不是一种妥当合宜的做法。
中 523
I confess that it is only after hesitation, due to objections of a very serious moral 362order, that I have decided to write of the danger that the doctrine of reincarnation entails, and above all of that abuse that can be—and is, in fact—made of it. It is the faith that you, dear Unknown Friend, understand the weight of responsibility that weighs on each person who sees himself treating reincarnation not as belonging to the domain of esoteric (i.e. intimate) experience, but as an exoteric teaching to popularise—called to convince everyone—which has determined me to speak of the practical abuse of the fact of reincarnation. I implore you therefore, dear Unknown Friend, to have the good will to examine, in the light of moral conscience, the question whether the way of treating reincarnation in exoteric teaching that has been adopted and is practised in general both by representatives of the French occult movement of the nineteenth and twentieth centuries and by Theosophists, Anthroposophists, Rosicrucians, etc., is justified and desirable.
110我必须强调的是，上述的分析涉及的不只是逃避炼狱、不肯面对上主所引发的道德危机，同时也涉及到以蛇的不朽取代神的不朽。
中 523
I may add that in the last analysis it is a matter not only of the moral danger of evading purgatory and the experience of Eternity, but also of replacing one immortality by another, namely that of God by that of the serpent. For there are two deaths and two immortalities.
111在浪子的寓言故事里，那位父亲所谓的「死」——「因为我这个儿子是死而复生、失而复得的」（《路加福音》第 15 章，第 24 节），指的就是和天父及天家疏远；肉体的死亡指的则是离开物质界、脱离世间引力的电场（「吊人」卡所涉及的奥义）。拒绝经历炼狱和天堂的人就是拒绝回到天父的家，也就是选择和祂疏远。所以从神的角度来看，全然结晶化即是彻底死亡，全然活着则是「如太阳般的放射」状态，或是结晶体的彻底消解。因此《圣经》所说的：「你们不可以吃善恶树的果子，否则你们一定死亡」，意思就是「一旦吃了善恶树的果子，你们就会离我远去」。蛇的承诺：「你们不会死」，意思则是「你们将脱离上主，独立生活，我有本事让你们在横向世界里继续存活下去，因为我会以智力和身心的电能取代神的智慧与爱」。因此蛇所谓的「生」便是神所谓的「死」，反之亦然。赫尔墨斯主义——基督教赫尔墨斯主义和更古早的〔原译：隐修传统 · the Hermetic tradition〕——以及所有真实的密契体验、灵知及神圣魔法的基本理念一向主张：横向的生死是实存的，纵向的生死也是真实不虚的。对赫尔墨斯主义而言，象征人性的十字架，「各各他的十字架」，代表的就是两种相反的生死方式。复活不但是纵向的生命力战胜了纵向的死亡，更是纵向的生命力战胜了横向的生命力。
中 524
The “death” of which the father speaks in the parable of the prodigal son—“my son was dead, and is alive again; he was lost, and is found” (Luke xv, 24)—is remoteness from the Father and his house, whilst the death of the physical body means remoteness from the physical plane and the electrical field of terrestrial gravitation (which was the matter in question in the twelfth Letter, concerning the Arcanum “The Hanged Man”). Now, the refusal to take the way of purgatory and heaven amounts to refusing to return to the house of the Father, i.e. the decision to remain remote from the Father. And it is precisely this which is death in a divine sense. Complete crystallisation is therefore complete death from the divine point of view, whilst complete life is the state of “radiating as the sun”, i.e. that of complete de-crystallisation. Thus the divine words, “You shall not eat from the Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil, for on the day when you eat of it you will die”, state simply that “the day when you eat from the Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil, you will remove yourself from Me”. And the promise of the serpent, “You will not die”, means to say, “You will live remote from God and it will be I who shall attend to the uninterrupted continuation of your life in the horizontal, for I shall make up for the lack of divine wisdom and love by replacing them with the intellect and with psycho-physical electricity, which will be the source of your life”. Therefore the serpent understands by “life” what God understands by “death”—and vice versa. Now, Hermeticism—Christian Hermeticism as well as ancient and pre-Christian Hermeticism—has always advanced the fundamental thesis of all true mysticism, true gnosis, and true sacred magic, that there is vertical Life and Death and that there is horizontal life and death. For Christian Hermeticism the Cross of humanity—the Cross of Calvary—is that of two opposed lives and deaths. Resurrection is not only the triumph of Life over Death, but is moreover the triumph of Life over life.
出处浪子的比喻里，「死」是什么意思
底本引《路加福音》15:24：「我这个儿子是死而复生、失而复得的」。父亲说的「死」，指的不是肉体的死，而是与父家的疏远。

汤贝格用这一节完成全信的定义置换：
• 上帝说的死＝离我远去（纵向）
• 蛇说的不死＝脱离上主而在横向世界里独立存续

于是那两句互相矛盾的话不再矛盾——蛇所谓的「生」正是神所谓的「死」，反之亦然。开篇悬置的问题，到此才算落地。

顺带一提：他把肉体的死亡归给「吊人」（第十二张大阿卡纳）的奥义——脱离世间引力的电场。全书的牌与牌之间就是这样互相引证的。
112图上显示的就是纵向战胜了横向、放射作用克服了固化作用。因此当妇女们在清晨来到耶稣的墓前却找不到祂的尸体时，那两个人才会对她们说：「你们为什么在死人中找活人呢？」（《路加福音》第 24 章，第 5 节）。让我们不再从死人中寻找那位活着的祂，更重要的是不该在死亡的领域——由电能构成的智力范畴内，或是在卡巴拉的骑龙恶魔萨麦尔（Samael）的领土里——寻求不朽的生命力。
中 525
363It is the victory of the vertical over the horizontal, of radiation over crystallisation. This is why when the women who went to the tomb at the break of day did not find the body of Lord Jesus, the two men who appeared to them in resplendent apparel said to them, “Why do you seek the living among the dead?” (Luke xxiv, 5). Therefore, let us no longer seek amongst the dead for he who is living, and above all let us not seek for immortal Life in the domain of death—in the domain of the intellect borne by electricity or, to employ an image borrowed from the Cabbala, in the domain of Samael mounted on the dragon.
术语十字：纵横两轴的生与死
英文栏内的图是底本原图：纵轴上端为「生」（Life）、下端为「死」（Death）；横轴两端则是另一套「死」（death）与「生」（life）。同一个词，大小写不同，意思相反。

汤贝格称之为「各各他的十字架」——象征人性的十字，代表的正是两种相反的生死方式。

因此复活的定义要读两遍：不只是纵向的生命战胜纵向的死亡，更是纵向的生命战胜横向的生命。后半句才是重点——被战胜的不是死，是另一种生。

接着那句「你们为什么在死人中找活人呢？」（路 24:5）在这个坐标里就有了新的读法：不要在由电能构成的智力范畴里、在骑龙的萨麦尔的领地里，去找不朽。
113幽灵和鬼显然不是生命存活或不朽的能源。这种能源存在于别的地方。在哪里呢？……在人的核心深处以及和上主的气息、光辉及热力的关系里。
中 525
It is not phantoms and ghosts which are the source of certainty of survival or immortality. The source of this certainty is found elsewhere. Where, then?…in the experience of the kernel of the human being and his relationship with the breath, light and warmth of God.
114人的不朽是源自于对不灭本质的体验。一个人若是体验过自己最深的本质，已经成为真正的自己，浸润在神的气息之中，沐浴在神的光辉里，感受过神的热力，自然会明白且已然成为不朽本身。你或许可以很优雅地向这样的人说明，头脑与神经系统的作用造成了意识的活动，就像彩虹是阳光投射到云层上造成的色彩变化一样，可是他会很清楚你所说的这一切都不对，相反的讲法才是正确的。或许他无法提出有效的论证去反驳你的说法，但仍然会确信自己是对的，因为他有真实的体验做为依据。
中 525
Certainty of immortality issues from the participation experienced in that which is intrinsically indestructible and imperishable, and therefore immortal. He who has had experience of the kernel of his being, i.e. he who has once been truly himself, permeated by the divine breath, bathed in divine light and ardent with divine warmth, he knows what immortality is and that he is immortal. You will have beautifully explained to him the “epiphenomenal nature” of consciousness, i.e. that consciousness is only the functioning of the brain and nervous system and that it is like the rainbow—a play of colours resulting from the refraction and reflection of solar rays on the clouds—you will have beautifully said all this, and he will have not even a little grain of doubt that all this is false and the contrary is true. Perhaps he would not be able to find valid arguments for you against epiphenomenalism, but as for him, he has no need of them. For it is not to arguments that he owes his certainty, but rather to experience.
115假如有人向圣十字若望或〔原译：圣大德兰 · St. Teresa of Avila〕提出一套现代科学都支持的理论，目的是要向他们证实灵魂只是有机体的化学或电能制造出来的幻象，那么请想像一下他们会有什么反应！他们有过多次的灵魂出体经验，每一次示现的都是肉体完全麻木的状态，但灵魂回来之后又充满了活力和光，其光源不仅超越化学和电能的范围，更超越了感官意像和智力活动的范畴！因此，他们还很可能把提出上述理论的人送去看心理医生或驱魔师！
中 526
Imagine the reaction of a St. John of the Cross or a St. Teresa of Avila to a discourse, armed with all the arguments of modern science, addressed to them so as to prove to them that the soul is only a mirage produced by chemical and electrical reactions of the organism! To prove this to them!—they who many times went out of the body, left in a state of complete insensibility, and returned full of life and light drawn not only from beyond all chemical and electrical reactions but also from beyond all sense imagery and all intellectual activity! I think that they would have sent the author of such remarks either to a psychiatrist or to an exorcist!
背景两位圣人会怎么反应
汤贝格设想的场面很生动：若有人拿一套现代科学都支持的理论去向圣十字若望或阿维拉的德兰证明灵魂只是有机体的化学幻象，他们的反应会是——把这人送去看心理医生或驱魔师。

理由不是权威，而是经验：两人都有过多次灵魂出体的经历，每次肉体完全麻木，而灵魂回来时充满活力与光。对他们来说，这不是需要论证的命题，而是发生过的事。

阿维拉的德兰（Teresa de Ávila, 1515–1582），西班牙加尔默罗会改革者，《七宝楼台》(Las Moradas) 的作者，1970 年被立为首位女性教会圣师。她与圣十字若望是本封信引得最多的一对。
116人一旦亲身体验过自己与上主的关系，自然会对不朽产生确信。
中 526
Certainty in immortality can thus be absolute, i.e. in no way dependent on 364the validity or invalidity of arguments, nor on the good or bad attestation of outer facts. It is when man has had experience of the kernel of his own being and of his essential relationship with God.
校记「绝对的确信」被压掉了
底本这一段说的是确信的性质：

「因此，对不朽的确信可以是绝对的——即丝毫不依赖论证成立与否，也不依赖外部事实的佐证是好是坏。这发生在人经验到自己存在的内核、以及自己与神的本质关系之时。」

中译作：「人一旦亲身体验过自己与上主的关系，自然会对不朽产生确信。」

后半句保住了，前半句整个不见了——「绝对的」没了，「不依赖论证、不依赖外部事实」这个界定也没了。而这正是本段唯一的信息量所在：汤贝格要划的界线是体验性的确知与论证性的知识不属于同一范畴，前者不会因后者被推翻而动摇。

这一句一失，紧接着关于笛卡尔与康德的四页就悬空了——那四页讲的正是：让他们确知的不是论证，而是体验。本版保留中译原文，在此出校。
117我当然知道人们为什么会从逻辑、哲学和心理学的角度批判勒内·笛卡尔（René Descartes）的「我思故我在」（"I think therefore I am"），我也毫不保留地接受科学审判对它做出的结论。但是让笛卡尔确实认清自己的先验本体或生命本质的，并不是科学审判施展的力道，而是意识的审判使然。在某一天，当笛卡尔以一贯令人钦羡的方式思考时，突然不再受制于逻辑推理，而真正觉知到那个正在思考的思想者！在那个当下，笛卡尔彻底体认到「我思」之中的「我在」并不是某种合乎逻辑的论点，而是「思想中的思想者」。
中 526
I certainly know the logical, philosophical and psychological criticism of the Cartesian argument, cogito ergo sum (“I think therefore I am”), and I accept it without reserve in foro scientiae, but it was not the force of this argument in the tribunal of science which gave René Descartes certainty of his transcendent Self, the kernel of his being, but rather the experience in the tribunal of consciousness (in foro conscientiae) where, whilst thinking in the admirable way which was characteristic of him, he went out of discursive thought and found himself suddenly as the thinker of thoughts! It was therefore not a logical argument but a real and intimate experience of thought—in the process of thinking—which gave Descartes complete certainty of the reality of the “I am” which manifests itself in the “I think”.
背景笛卡尔：真正让他确知的不是那个推理
勒内·笛卡尔（René Descartes, 1596–1650），法国哲学家、数学家，近代哲学的开端。Cogito, ergo sum（我思故我在）出自《方法谈》(1637)。

汤贝格完全接受逻辑学、哲学、心理学对这条推理的批判——他说自己「毫不保留地接受科学审判对它做出的结论」。他要救的不是论证，而是论证背后的那一刻：某天笛卡尔在思考中忽然不再受制于推理，而觉知到了那个正在思考的思想者。

换句话说：「我思故我在」作为三段论是可以被拆穿的，作为一次体验的记录则拆不动。这正是上一条校记里被删掉的那句界定的应用。
118德国哲学家康德（一个如孩子般的纯真灵魂，天生就非常诚实和勤勉）将笛卡尔的自发体验变成了内在求知的新方法，也就是所谓的「先验法」（the transcendental method）。
中 526
The German philosopher Immanuel Kant (a soul of childlike purity, endowed with a remarkable honesty and diligence) made Descartes’ spontaneous experience a new method of inner effort aspiring to knowledge, namely the transcendental method.
背景康德：一个被人当作怀疑论大师的信者
伊曼努尔·康德（Immanuel Kant, 1724–1804）。汤贝格给他的形容是全书里少见的温柔：「一个如孩子般的纯真灵魂，天生就非常诚实和勤勉」。

他把康德的先验法（the transcendental method）读成一种灵性操作：让思想者从思想中抽身，站到更高一层去观察自己的思想——这与印度智瑜伽行者依次否掉身体、心理活动、思想而抵达思想者本身，是同一套动作。所谓「哥白尼式革命」，就是把观察的中心从对象挪到观察者。

三部批判在他笔下各有位置：《纯粹理性批判》与《判断力批判》修正认知的傲慢，划出形上学不能妄断的边界；到《实践理性批判》，则听见了生命核心对自己说的那句话——「假如我说神不存在、我不自由不不朽，我就必须改变自己生命的根基，或者消灭自己。」

末尾那段辩护值得注意：世人讥康德晚年「陷入年轻时的唯信主义」，汤贝格反问——难道一生诚实勤勉的努力，就不许结出确信的果子？人们不为他的确信欢喜，反倒指控他不忠。「上帝啊，这样的态度多么刻薄啊！」

这几句话其实也是他为自己这本书辩护。

又：中译把《判断力批判》作《对判断的批判》，可通而不合大陆通行译名，此处不改，仅出注。
119此法乃是要让思想者超越原本浸淫于思维的固着模式，提升至更高的层次去「观察」或「研究」自己的思想。康德的「哥白尼式革命」（Copernican revolution）也是要思想者跳出自己的「天真想法」，在思考过程中跳脱自我迷失，然后专注在其上的一个点，用抽离、无情又清明的真心去检视它——这便是康德的「先验批判」精神。《纯粹理性批判》（Critique of Pure Reason）和《对判断的批判》（Critique of Judgement）便是运用这种方式撰写的著作；这个方式修正了我们原先的认知模式，让我们清楚地洞悉自以为能对形上事物——神、灵魂的不朽性及道德自由——进行评断的傲慢态度。透过康德的《实践理性批判》（Critique of Practical Reason），我们进一步地超越了对思想者的检视，开始进入理性逻辑和感官觉知的范畴，于是听见了生命核心想要对自己说的话：
中 526
This method amounts to the effort to transcend the thinking in which the thinker is ordinarily immersed, by going out from it and elevating himself above it, in order to observe thought—or “to think about thought”—from a point of observation taken above discursive thought. Kant’s “Copernican discovery” consists above all in detaching the thinker from “naive thought”, i.e. from the state where the thinker is lost in the process of thought, being immersed in it, so as to occupy a point situated above thought, from where the thinker can examine what is thought in an entirely detached way and with implacable and incorruptible truthfulness—this is the “transcendental criticism” of Kant. His works Critique of Pure Reason and Critique of Judgement are the fruits of the application of this method in the sense of the revision of the totality of our knowledge, and in the sense of a clear investigation of the pretensions of the intellect and of the senses to being able to judge things belonging to the metaphysical domain—for example, God, the immortality of the soul, and moral freedom. With respect to his Critique of Practical Reason we find here, beyond the results of the critical look of the thinker turned towards the domain of discursive thought and sense perceptions in which he had been immersed before, in addition what the thinker, this kernel of the human being, has to say himself. This can be summarised as follows:
120假如我说神不存在，我不是自由或不朽的，那么我就必须改变自己的生命基础或消灭自己。我的生命结构足以证实朝着完美神性发展的潜能的确存在，同时也存在着道德的自主性以及灵魂的不朽性。
中 527
I would have to alter the foundation of my very being, or to annihilate myself, if I were to say that God does not exist, that I am not free, and that I am not immortal. The very structure of my being is such that it postulates categorically the existence of God or infinite perfectibility, freedom or morality as such, and the immortality of the soul or the possibility of infinite perfectioning.
121笛卡尔的「我思故我在」后来成了逻辑学家、哲学家及心理学家灭绝式批判的对象，康德在《实践理性批判》中提出的基本理论也遭到这些人的严厉批评。在此我必须指出，令康德确实明白神、自由及不朽的，并不是某种合乎逻辑的结论或合理的论述，而是他在修炼先验法时得到的内在体悟。后者显然被证实为真正的属灵体验，它引领着康德去经验自己的核心本质，从其中发展出三种层次的确信：对神的实相、道德的自主性以及对灵魂不朽本质的确信。
中 527
365It goes without saying that just as René Descartes’ argument “I think therefore I am” became the object of the annihilating criticism of logicians, philosophers and psychologists, so is the fundamental argument advanced by Immanual Kant in his Critique of Practical Reason the subject of a no less severe criticism—and no less founded in foro scientiae—than in the case of Descartes’ argument. But here also it is necessary to say that it was not a logical conclusion or an argument of discursive thought which gave Kant certainty of God, freedom and immortality, but rather the real and intimate experience that he had when he practised his transcendental method. This latter evidently proved itself to be an authentic spiritual exercise, which led Kant to arrive at experience of the kernel of his being—just as Descartes arrived there—and from which he drew the threefold certainty: the reality of God, the reality of moral freedom and, lastly, the reality of the soul’s immortality.
122同样地，印度智慧瑜伽行者也秉持着批判精神去检视自己的身体，而真实地体悟到「此身非自性」，接着又以相同的态度去检视自己的心理活动——欲望、感受、记忆、意像等——而进一步体悟到「心理活动亦非自性」，最后他们检视了自己的思想，借由抽离的方式经验到思想者本身，正如同笛卡尔和康德从思想提升至思想者的高度，故而体悟了自性一样。他们借由这种方式，得到了对「我在」（笛卡尔）和「我是自由的、不朽的、神与我同在」（康德）的确信。
中 528
Similarly, in Hindu Jnana-yoga the yogi arrives at the transcendent Self by critically observing firstly his body and arriving at the experience, “This body is not the Self”, then critically examining his psychic life—desires, feelings, memory images, etc.—to arrive at the experience, “This psychic life is not the Self”, and, lastly, critically examining his thinking itself, from which he detaches himself, and experiences the thinker—just as Descartes and Kant arrived at the experience of the transcendent Self by rising from thought to the thinker. From this comes their certainty of “I am” (Descartes) and “I am free, I am immortal (and) I am in the presence of God” (Kant).
123但愿人们能停止针对笛卡尔和康德的批判，以理智克制自己的反应；但愿人们不再干预这两个灵魂对生命本质或超验自性的真实体悟。但愿人们对康德老态龙钟还「陷入年轻时的唯信主义」的批评，能够彻底平息下来。事实上，康德并没有背叛任何原则，也没有陷入任何主义，他的人生和努力都得到了丰硕成果。或许有人希望他一无所获，最终只是一名批判主义和怀疑论的大师？……难道他一生诚实勤勉的努力不足以带来任何体悟，对形上世界产生确信吗？人们不但不为他的确信感到欢喜，反而评断他的缺失，指控他不忠！上帝啊，这样的态度多么刻薄啊！
中 528
May the criticism of Descartes and Kant eventually become silent, restraining itself through understanding; may it no more interfere with that at which these two spirits arrived, namely the intimate experience of the kernel of their being, the transcendent Self. May it one day cease from being repeated to satiety that Kant “proved himself to be unfaithful to his own method and to have betrayed his own principles” and that, in growing old and becoming senile, he “sank into the fideism of his youth”. For the truth is that Kant betrayed nothing and did not sink into anything, but that he arrived at the ripe fruit of his life and work. Or would one wish that he had not arrived anywhere, and that he had finished his life as a master of criticism and doubt only?…that the honest and assiduous effort of his life had not brought any experience, and therefore any certainty, with regard to things belonging to the metaphysical sphere? Instead of rejoicing in and celebrating the fact of his certainty, one speaks of his lapse and one accuses him of unfaithfulness! Good Lord! How petty!
124亲爱的朋友，你现在应该明白了，西方世界的伟大思想家和印度修行者一样，都因为悟到生命的本质或自性，而对不朽产生确信。
中 528
You see therefore, dear Unknown Friend, that the great thinkers of the West—just as the Hindu yogis—have arrived at the experience of the kernel of the human being, the transcendent Self, the experience of which gave them certainty of immortality.
125由于基督教赫尔墨斯主义是密契体验、灵知和神圣魔法的整合，所以会提出三种超越上述「哲学先验法」的体验方式，帮助人们对不朽产生确信。
中 528
Christian Hermeticism, being a synthesis of mysticism, gnosis and sacred magic, offers humanity three methods of experience, beyond the “philosophical method” outlined above, for arriving at the certainty of immortality.
126这指的就是〔原译：净化、启蒙及合一的传统隐修途径 · the traditional mystical way of purification, illumination and union〕，亦即在生前就带着觉知去经验死后的三个阶段——从炼狱到天堂到与神合一。你不但可以从伟大的基督信仰密契者，例如托名〔原译：亚略巴古的丢尼修 · Dionysius the Areopagite〕、〔原译：圣文德 · Bonaventura〕、阿维拉的德兰与圣十字若望等人的身上认识这个途径；也可以在基督信仰出现之前的〔原译：隐修教诲 · the Hermetic treatises〕，例如〔原译：赫密士 · Hermes Trismegistus〕的《神圣的牧羊人》（The Divine Pymander）之中去发现它，同时还能在异教徒与埃及人的神话以及其他的神话作品里见到它。净化、启蒙及合一的三阶段修炼使人认识死后的状态，并对不朽产生确信。就这一点，〔原译：马克斯·雷维尔 · Jean Marques-Rivière〕4（Jean Marques-Rivière）曾经说过：

366There is in the first place the traditional mystical way of purification, illumination and union, which is the voluntary and conscious experience of the three stages of the way of the human soul after death—through purgatory to heaven, and from heaven to God. You will find this not only with the great Christian mystics such as Dionysius the Areopagite, Bonaventura, Teresa of Avila, and John of the Cross…not only in the pre-Christian teachings of the Hermetic treatises attributed to Hermes Trismegistus, such as The Divine Pymander, but also in the great mysteries of pagans, Egyptians, and others, where the three stages of catharsis (purification), photismos (illumination) and henosis (union, or identification with the Divine) give consciousness of the post-mortem states and certainty of immortality. 底本此处开列的是走过「净化—启蒙—合一」的见证人：亚略巴古的狄奥尼修斯、波纳文图拉、阿维拉的德兰、十字若望；再往前，还有基督之前托名赫尔墨斯的文献，以及异教、埃及的密仪。

《神圣的牧羊人》（The Divine Pymander）是 17 世纪英译本给《赫尔墨斯文集》所起的旧题，得名于文集开篇的《波伊曼德列斯》(Poimandres)——那篇正是讲灵魂上升穿过诸天、层层脱去习气的历程，与三阶次第严丝合缝。
